| **Cross-Plan Deps** | Cross-Plan Dependency Graph |

## Goal

Ship the Spec-021 Electron desktop app and React + Vite renderer as a three-process system (main / renderer / the background service main starts and watches) that lands the renderer-untrusted trust stance, the narrow typed preload bridge, the full Electron Fuses + ASAR-integrity + CSP hardening baseline, main's registry of windows in which every window is alike and none is the main one, a service started detached so that quitting the app leaves the service and every run and shell running, `electron-updater` 6.8.10 auto-update with its stock GitHub provider, control-plane calls forwarded to the background service, which holds every credential, code signing on macOS / Windows / Linux, and crash reports that Crashpad writes to this machine only and the service turns into stripped reports. The desktop app carries no WebAuthn: passkeys are used only outside it. The renderer ships the layout chrome, router, and composer, and **mounts** the V1 Signature Feature views that their owning plans author in those plans' own features — this plan creates a mount point and a route, never a view body. It is the desktop surface, not the authority. **The console** — the Meridian surface set [Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian)](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#console-design-meridian) fixes (the icon rail, the sessions list, the session views a window holds side by side, the pane-kind set with any side pane movable into a window of its own, and the fixture scenarios the app starts on with `--fixture <scenario>`) — is registered by this plan at §Target Areas Renderer and built as the supplement Phase 1C inside that spec envelope; Phase 5 **adopts** it by mounting and moving, never by re-authoring (T-020r-5-1 EXTEND). Preview and the detached panes are built in Phase 8.

## Scope

- `apps/desktop/` — **new app package.** The Electron main process (`apps/desktop/src/main/`), the preload bridge (`apps/desktop/src/preload/`), and the React + Vite renderer (`apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/`).
- Electron runtime target: **44.x** (`44.5.1`, pinned exact) per [ADR-015 §Decision](../decisions/015-electron-desktop-app.md#decision). Electron 41 reached end-of-support on 2026-08-25 with the 44.0.0 release, and the detached-`WebContentsView` bounds fix the console's `browser` pane kind depends on (electron/electron PR #53031) reached 42 / 43 / 44 only, so V1 admits 44 alone. Every release on a supported branch carries the fixes for the Q1 2026 CVE batch (CVE-2026-34769, -34770, -34771, -34772, -34774, -34764, -34776, fixed 2026-04-02 in 39.8.5 / 40.8.5 / 41.1.0 per the [Electron GHSA feed](https://github.com/electron/electron/security/advisories)). The desktop app's `package.json` pin moves at T-020p-1B-4, together with the `better-sqlite3` 13.0.3 move [ADR-021 §Decision](../decisions/021-v1-toolchain-selection.md#decision) binds to it.
- Build toolchain: **`electron-vite`** (alex8088) `6.0.0-beta.5`, pinned exactly, because the build runs on Vite 8 and only v6 declares it — Vite-powered main / preload / renderer build with HMR, source-code protection, and a single `electron.vite.config.ts`. Alternative considered + rejected in §Risks And Blockers.
- Packaging + auto-update: **`electron-builder`** 26.17.x + **`electron-updater`** 6.8.10 per [Desktop App Implementation Notes](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md) §Build And Packaging and §Auto-Updater. The packages are the macOS `.dmg` to install and `.zip` for updates (arm64 and x64), NSIS per user on Windows x64 and arm64, AppImage on Linux x64 and arm64, and `.deb` and `.rpm`, which add the project's signed apt or dnf repository, hosted on Cloudsmith's open-source plan, when installed. There is no Snap, no Flatpak, no MSI and no staged rollout. A `.deb` or `.rpm` install is the package manager's to update: it never runs the updater's own Debian or RPM installer, which would bypass the repository's signature.
- Renderer stack: React 19 + Vite 8 + TypeScript strict (per [ADR-015 §Success Criteria](../decisions/015-electron-desktop-app.md#success-criteria)).
- Preload bridge: single `window.desktopBridge` object via `contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld`; narrow typed capability surface (no `any`) per [Preload Bridge Contract](../architecture/contracts/preload-bridge-contract.md).
- Security hardening: every window a `BaseWindow` and every document it shows a `WebContentsView` created with the exact `webPreferences` set in [Spec-021 §Security Hardening Baseline](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#security-hardening-baseline); every release build packaged with the Electron Fuses Spec-021 declares + the ASAR Integrity Digest, both set through `electron-builder` (its `electronFuses` setting and its integrity embed); strict CSP per Spec-021.
- Service start and watch: main never parents the background service, because a quit must leave the service, every run and every shell running. Starting the service detached, watching it and bringing it back are [Plan-025](./025-remote-control.md) Phase 1's; this plan composes that start and watch into main's startup (T-020r-3-3) and registers its `daemon.status` handler (CP-020-1).
- Auto-update: `electron-updater` as shipped, with its stock GitHub provider and nothing of the project's on top. It checks each download's SHA-512 against the update files `electron-builder` publishes, and the operating system's code signature decides the rest on macOS (Squirrel.Mac's designated requirement) and Windows (the installer's publisher, `verifyUpdateCodeSignature`). Main refuses a release whose version is not above the last one seen, which `updater-state.json` keeps, and `allowDowngrade` stays off. `autoDownload` and `autoInstallOnAppQuit` are off: a download starts on the person's press, is a blockmap delta where the platform has one (NSIS, the macOS zip once a previous update is cached, and AppImage), and Squirrel.Mac is handed an update only at `Restart to finish`. The app does not roll itself back; a bad release is fixed by the next one.
- Code signing: until the Apple Developer ID certificate exists ([BL-108](../backlog.md#bl-108-windows--macos-signing-procurement-evidence)), every macOS build is signed with the project's own self-signed code-signing identity, held as a release secret, with one bundle identifier on every build, so every update meets the installed app's designated requirement and one-press update works; only notarization and the first-launch Gatekeeper prompt wait on the certificate, after which it is Developer ID Application + `xcrun notarytool`. The Windows installers are signed through SignPath Foundation's free open-source program, so Windows names `SignPath Foundation` as the publisher; if the project is not eligible, that goes back to the person and the distribution plan does not change silently. The project signs no Linux package itself: Cloudsmith's per-repository key signs each RPM on upload and the apt and dnf repository metadata, and a `.deb` is checked through apt's signed Release file. A release publishes its files and a checksum list.
- Crash reporting: while `Keep crash reports` is on, which is its default, Crashpad writes each crash to this machine only (`crashReporter.start({ uploadToServer: false })`); nothing is uploaded anywhere. The service turns each dump into a report on the machine — the process type, the app and service versions, the platform and the crashing thread's stack, file paths cut to their names and session ids replaced by stable hashes — symbolicates it there, deletes it with the diagnostic logs, and serves the kept reports through `daemon.crashList` to Remote Control and `sidekicks crash list`. The raw dump never leaves the machine.
- Deep-link: register the `sidekicks://` protocol handler on macOS, Windows and Linux; URL parsing happens in the main process, never in the renderer.
- E2E test harness: **Playwright `_electron`** namespace (marked experimental in the [Playwright class-electron docs](https://playwright.dev/docs/api/class-electron), but the only production-viable option; Spectron is archived).
- Monorepo placement: `apps/desktop/` as a workspace package, sibling to `packages/runtime-daemon/`, `packages/contracts/`, `packages/control-plane/` per the monorepo topology in [container-architecture.md](../architecture/container-architecture.md) §Client Delivery Sequence.

## Non-Goals

- **Pixel-level mockups and per-screen visual specifications.** [Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian)](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#console-design-meridian) fixes the design language, the surface set, the pane and window model, the budgets, and — through [Desktop App Implementation Notes §Console Libraries](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#console-libraries) — the component-library and theme decisions; this plan registers the console's renderer folders and their adoption path (§Target Areas Renderer, T-020r-5-1 EXTEND) and carries its invariants (I-020-6, I-020-7), while the per-surface composition is the console's own code and fixture scenarios.
- **Daemon internals.** Owned by `daemon.md` and Spec-006 / Plan-005. This plan consumes Plan-005's daemon contract; it does not re-specify it.
- **PTY supervision from the main process.** Per [Plan-021 §Target Areas](./021-rust-pty-sidecar.md#target-areas), `PtyHostSelector` + `RustSidecarPtyHost` + `NodePtyHost` are owned by the **daemon** (`packages/runtime-daemon/src/pty/`). The main process never consumes the PtyHost contract directly. A Plan-020 edit wiring PTY supervision into the main process is a review rejection.
- **The CLI client.** The CLI's own surfaces are owned by a separate plan. Plan-020 is desktop-only.
- **The web client's and the phone apps' hosts.** They run this front end through their own implementations of the one bridge interface and are built in [Plan-025](./025-remote-control.md)'s frontend phase ([Spec-021 §Scope](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#scope)); this plan builds the desktop host.
- **Provider-driver protocols.** Owned by Spec-004.
- **Workflow authoring semantics.** Owned by the workflow-engine track — V1 feature 14 per ADR-014 (Spec-015 / Plan-014; the node-graph builder under ADR-024). The console mounts Plan-014's workflow screens under the Workflows destination — the runs list, a run's page and the builder — and authors none of their bodies; no workflow screen is a pane.
- **MSIX and MSI distribution.** V1 ships NSIS per user on Windows x64 and arm64. The MSI installer exists for an organization's deployment tools, which one user does not have, and `electron-updater` does not update it; MSIX, the Store's format, is not shipped either.
- **Reproducible builds.** Not claimed by V1 and no reproducible-build job, per [Desktop App Implementation Notes §Build And Packaging](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#build-and-packaging); neither `electron-builder` nor `electron-vite` guarantees bit-reproducibility.
- **Snap and Flatpak packaging.** Not shipped. Linux gets AppImage, and `.deb` and `.rpm` through the project's signed apt and dnf repository.
- **Reconciling `security-architecture.md` §Local Daemon Authentication.** Plan-020 consumes the renderer-untrusted stance as declared in [Spec-021 §Trust Stance](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#trust-stance), which `security-architecture.md` §Local Daemon Authentication states the same way.

## Preconditions

Spec-021, which this plan is paired with, and the ADRs below underlie **both** the partial and the remainder. The four `**(Remainder only.)**` items below — Plan-005's daemon-control surface, Plan-025's tRPC + WebSocket clients in the background service, the Plan-005-owned contracts type exports, and the Plan-005 / Plan-025 subscription request channel — gate **only the remainder** (full main-process behavior consumes those plans' surfaces), and each is named in the precondition of every phase in [§Remainder PR Sequence](#remainder-pr-sequence) it gates. The partial has no upstream-plan preconditions per [§Partial PR Sequence](#partial-pr-sequence) — it is a standalone substrate slice authored against ADR-015 + Spec-021 alone.

- ADR-015 (Electron desktop app) — the supported-branch subset 42 / 43 / 44, with V1 on 44.x alone.
- ADR-010 (Tokens, Passkeys And The Remote Channel) — the desktop app presents no token of its own, its control-plane calls riding the background service, which holds this machine's tokens, and it carries no passkey ceremony, because passkeys are used only outside it.
- ADR-009 (JSON-RPC IPC wire format) — the wire format the preload bridge forwards.
- ADR-013 (tRPC control-plane API) — the transport the background service carries main's control-plane calls over.
- ADR-019 (V1 deployment model) — declares the hosted-SaaS and self-host options.
- ADR-023 (Electron main-process window retention) — the GC/lifecycle reachability mechanism the [Spec-021 §Acceptance Criteria](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#acceptance-criteria) window-retention bullet verifies.
- **(Remainder only.)** Plan-005 exposes `daemon.hello`, `daemon.flush`, `daemon.stop`, `daemon.restart` and `daemon.status.read`, which Plan-025 Phase 1's start and watch of the service and main's bridge call; that start and watch, `daemon.ping` and the `daemon.status` topic are Plan-025 Phase 1's. Plan-005 ownership of the daemon-start contract is load-bearing; if Plan-005 ships without an explicit `DaemonHello` readiness signal, Phase 3 below blocks until it lands.
- **(Remainder only.)** Plan-025 exposes a tRPC v11 client + WebSocket (JSON-RPC 2.0) client surface the background service instantiates; main forwards each renderer control-plane call to the service, which sends it under this machine's credentials. The main process is a forwarder, not a protocol implementer.
- **(Remainder only.)** `packages/contracts/src/` exports `DaemonMethod` / `DaemonParams` / `DaemonResult` / `DaemonEvent` / `DaemonEventPayload` typed union per Spec-006 (owned by Plan-005). The `PlatformBridge` interface and the desktop's preload API type import them from there and re-export none of them.
- **(Remainder only.)** Plan-005 / Plan-025 resolve the per-subscription request channel [Preload Bridge Contract](../architecture/contracts/preload-bridge-contract.md) names — `daemon.subscribe(event, params, handler, onEnded?)` — so `sessionId` and `afterCursor` travel, and `session.subscribe`, the session's one stream, which a window that fell behind re-opens after the last position it kept, has a shape to call. Type-name availability does not satisfy this; the parameterized signature must exist.

## Target Areas

### New package

- `apps/desktop/` — **created by this plan.** Workspace package, sibling to `packages/*`.
- `apps/desktop/package.json` — declares the Electron 44.x runtime, `electron-vite` `6.0.0-beta.5` (exact) as the build tool, `electron-builder` 26.17.x + `electron-updater` 6.8.10 as packaging + update toolchain (the Electron pin itself moves at T-020p-1B-4; `electron-builder`'s `electronFuses` setting flips the fuses and its integrity embed writes the ASAR integrity digest), `@electron/fuses` for the packaged-binary fuse test's read of the fuse wire. Renderer deps: `react` v19, `react-dom` v19. Playwright `_electron` for E2E testing. **Console dependencies (exact-pinned, each admitted by a [Desktop App Implementation Notes §Console Libraries](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#console-libraries) verdict row and landing with the console PR that first imports it):** `@tanstack/react-virtual`, `micromark` + `mdast-util-from-markdown` + the GFM extensions, `remend`, `katex`, `mermaid` (lazy), `@xterm/xterm` + the WebGL / fit / search / unicode11 / serialize addons, `anser`, `diff`, `@xyflow/react` (with `@xyflow/system` in lockstep), `@dagrejs/dagre` (the graph's layout), `react-resizable-panels`, `overlayscrollbars` 2.16.0 with `overlayscrollbars-react` 0.5.6 (every scroller but the transcript), `perfect-freehand`, `@base-ui/react`, `tinykeys`, `@floating-ui/react-dom`, `zustand`, `idb`, `unplugin-icons` + `@iconify-json/tabler`, and the IBM Plex Sans / Mono foundry packages; test-only `@vitest/browser-playwright`, `@playwright/test`, `axe-core` (MPL-2.0, the [ADR-019 §License](../decisions/019-v1-deployment-model-and-oss-license.md#license) waiver — bare rather than the `@axe-core/playwright` wrapper, which binds to a Playwright `Page` that the browser-mode tier does not expose), `memlab`, `size-limit`, `knip`. Nothing on the AVOID column of that table may appear here.

**No crash SDK.** `@sentry/electron` is not a dependency. Crash reports stay on this machine, so nothing needs an upload client, and its `beforeSend` scrubber never reached the raw minidump bytes, which hold heap memory; the service builds the stripped report on the machine instead, with `minidump-stackwalk` 0.27.0 in the daemon, measured before it is adopted. Self-hosted Sentry was considered and not taken: it needs 16 GB of memory and is FSL-licensed.

**No WebAuthn dependency.** `koffi`, `oauth4webapi` and an own-built macOS WebAuthn addon are not dependencies: the desktop app carries no WebAuthn, because passkeys are used only outside it.

**No keystore library.** `@napi-rs/keyring` is not a dependency of the desktop app: main keeps no keystore entry and holds no credential, and the background service holds every secret.

**No drag library for reordering.** The terminal's and Preview's tabs, the waiting messages, the panes and the session views move on the console's own pointer drag, inside a window and between windows (T-020r-5-11, [ADR-040](../decisions/040-drag-is-our-own-on-pointer-events.md)). The hidden console page runs all the renderer's code and opens every window a person sees with `window.open`, so a library must listen on the document of the element being dragged, and none fits. `@atlaskit/pragmatic-drag-and-drop` binds `dragstart` on the global `document` of the page that loaded it and `dragover` and `drop` on its global `window`, so a drag in a window the console opened reaches no listener, and its maintainer expects each window to manage its own drag ([issue #24](https://github.com/atlassian/pragmatic-drag-and-drop/issues/24), [issue #123](https://github.com/atlassian/pragmatic-drag-and-drop/issues/123)); it is removed with its code in the change that lands the own drag. `@dnd-kit/core` works in such a window but trails the pointer by a frame, has had no release since December 2024, and its maintainer's work has moved to a 0.x line that listens on the global document ([discussion #1803](https://github.com/clauderic/dnd-kit/discussions/1803)). `motion`'s Reorder reads the hidden page's `requestAnimationFrame`, which never fires. No library surveyed docks a view into another window or tears one off live.

**No colorer in the renderer.** Code is colored once in the daemon and every surface paints the spans it is handed ([ADR-033](../decisions/033-one-syntax-colorer-in-the-daemon.md)), so `shiki` is a dependency of `packages/runtime-daemon` at the same pin and not of this package, and no Worker here colors a block or a diff. **Vitest-family lockstep.** `@vitest/browser-playwright` peers on `vitest` at the exact same version, so it is admitted through the `testing` catalog in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` beside `vitest` and `@vitest/coverage-v8`, never as a desktop-local exact pin — the catalog entry is the single pin, the family moves together, and the registry-latest rule yields to catalog lockstep for any package whose peer is exact-versioned.

### Main process

- **Main's entry point**, at the root of `main/` — **created.** Calls `app.requestSingleInstanceLock()`; reads startup config; starts the crash reporter **first** (before any other module loads); registers the `sidekicks://` protocol handler; reads `--fixture` / `--session` through main's fixture launch; builds the hidden console window, whose document opens the window used last first and the rest from the kept layout; then runs [Plan-025](./025-remote-control.md) Phase 1's start and watch of the service.
- **The window factory**, in `main/windows/` — **created.** Every window is a `BaseWindow` hosting the renderer as a `WebContentsView` with the exact `webPreferences` set in [Spec-021 §Security Hardening Baseline](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#security-hardening-baseline). The two ESLint selectors refuse a window or web view built outside the window factory and a hardened `webPreferences` key written as anything but its hardened literal, `factory.test.ts` checks the factory builds through the locked block, and the launch smoke test asserts the hardening at run time. One private `constructLockedWindow` owns the locked literal and nothing outside the module calls it. **Every window is alike and none is the main one.** Main keeps a registry of windows — their focus order (the window used last), each one's kept place in main's window-place file, and the hidden console window built at start, never shown, its background throttling off and kept until quit — and builds every window a person sees when the hidden console document calls `window.open`: `setWindowOpenHandler` answers `{ action: 'allow', createWindow }`, builds main's own `BaseWindow` from main's own options and the place the frame name's window id keys, and adopts the handed `webContents` in a `WebContentsView`, which keeps the renderer's sandbox, context isolation and CSP. The child carries no preload and no bridge of its own: one renderer drives every window, so a session's store and daemon subscriptions serve its view and its pane windows alike, and no window re-subscribes or holds a store of its own. Main pairs the two closes both ways (the window's `closed` closes the child `webContents`, the child's `destroyed` closes the window) and mirrors each window document's title onto its `BaseWindow`. A side pane moved into its own window is built the same way, and no such window is ever in Preview's relay target list. The navigation policy denies every popup except these `window.open` calls from the renderer itself. **Phase 1B (T-020p-1B-2):** the hidden console window's `loadURL` over the custom scheme and the private `constructLockedWindow`. **Phase 1B (T-020p-1B-5), before Phase 2's bridge:** the registry, the window-open handler and the window-place file.
- **The application menu**, at the root of `main/` — **created at Phase 1B (T-020p-1B-2).** One static template with the platform's own roles on every platform — on macOS the app menu first, with the `about`, `hide`, `hideOthers`, `unhide` and `quit` roles (About, Hide, Hide Others, Show All, Quit), and on Windows and Linux a Help menu whose one row is the `about` role, `About AI Sidekicks`; an Edit menu whose Undo, Redo, Cut, Copy, Paste and Select All reach the focused field through the system's own chain, the File menu's `New window`, whose accelerator is the live `New window` binding (`⌘⇧N` until the person rebinds it) and moves with it, the View menu's `System · Light · Dark` color-scheme choice with the one in force ticked, and Electron's own `{ role: "windowMenu" }`, whose platform verbs list every open window by its title — plus, on macOS, the menu-bar icon (`Tray`) that stays when the last window closes, its face one of three template images, idle, working or needs you, kept live by T-020r-5-17. Main fills the About panel once at start with `app.setAboutPanelOptions` (T-020p-1B-5 EXTEND). No other module registers a section into it; a rebind of `New window` rebuilds the template from the same source.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/services/renderer/protocol.ts` — **created.** Custom protocol (`sidekicks-renderer://`) the renderer bundle is served from — not `file://`, per Spec-021's `GrantFileProtocolExtraPrivileges` fuse-disabled posture. **Registered privileged before `app.ready` (I-020-6):** `protocol.registerSchemesAsPrivileged([{ scheme: 'sidekicks-renderer', privileges: { standard: true, secure: true, supportFetchAPI: true } }])` runs at module top level in `main/index.ts`'s import order, ahead of the ready handler, because a scheme not registered `standard` has no origin and therefore no IndexedDB and no `localStorage` — the console's persisted layouts and pins ([Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian)](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#console-design-meridian) §Persistence on the renderer scheme) would have no home. A test asserts the registration precedes `whenReady()`. **Created at Phase 1B (T-020p-1B-1)** beside the bundle handler: `installRendererProtocol(rendererRoot, rootStamp)` serves the built tree through the pure `resolveRendererAsset` (root-containment guard, closed content-type map, the locked CSP header on every response, streaming bodies via `net.fetch`, empty-bodied 403 / 404).
- `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/install-handlers.ts` — **created.** IPC handler registration. One `ipcMain.handle` per preload-bridge method from [Preload Bridge Contract](../architecture/contracts/preload-bridge-contract.md).
- `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/daemon.ts` — **created.** `daemon.call` / `daemon.subscribe` handlers. Forwards to the Plan-005 daemon client over the main process's own connection, which presented the session token in its `daemon.hello`. **Waits on the request channel in §Preconditions:** every parameterized subscription the remainder needs (`session.subscribe` with `{ sessionId, afterCursor }` is the concrete case) travels on [Preload Bridge Contract](../architecture/contracts/preload-bridge-contract.md)'s `daemon.subscribe(event, params, handler, onEnded?)`, whose end arm tells the page a subscription stopped.
- **Main's control-plane handler behind the preload bridge** — **created.** The `controlPlane.call` (request-response) handler. Main attaches no control-plane credential and holds none: the handler forwards the renderer's request, unchanged, to the background service's own `controlPlane.call` over main's own connection to the daemon, and the service sends it to the control plane under this machine's access token and a fresh DPoP proof. The handler returns only the sanitized response to the renderer, so no control-plane token crosses the bridge. A procedure the control plane defines as anonymous stays reachable while the service holds no credential: anonymous means authentication is not _required_, not that a held credential must be stripped, and the relay counts its sign-in routes per source address ([Spec-019 §Canonical Endpoint Group Registry](../specs/019-rate-limiting-policy.md#canonical-endpoint-group-registry)), so a present credential changes neither admission nor throttling.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/native/` — **created.** One module per native handler: `open-dialog.ts` (`showOpenDialog`), `clipboard.ts` (`copyToClipboard`, plain text and a formatted flavor in one write), `file-intake.ts` (a dropped file and a pasted picture, each handed back as an opaque `FilePathRef` token), `notification-permission.ts` (whether the operating system lets the app post a notification) and `editors/` (opening a file or folder in the chosen editor); `openExternal`'s allowlist is `apps/desktop/src/main/windows/navigation.ts`'s. The save dialog, notifications and reveal-in-file-explorer are T-020r-2-4's.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/update.ts` — **created.** `update.getState` / `subscribe` / `requestCheck` / `requestDownload` / `requestRestart`. Forwards to the `updater.ts` state machine.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/services/updater.ts` — **created.** `electron-updater` with its stock GitHub provider + the refusal of a release not above the last one seen, which main's updater record, `<app-user-data>/updater-state.json`, keeps + the install-kind detection behind the cannot-update lines. Scheduled update checks on 4-hour cadence + startup, both only while `Check for updates automatically` is on, + explicit user request.
- **The crash reporter**, in the main process — **created.** Starts Crashpad with `crashReporter.start({ uploadToServer: false })` while the machine settings file's `Keep crash reports` is on, read straight from the file because it starts before the service first answers. Each crash is written to this machine only. The module uploads nothing and scrubs nothing itself: the service builds the stripped report from the dump (the process type, the versions, the platform and the crashing thread's stack, file paths cut to their names, session ids replaced by stable hashes), symbolicates it on the machine, deletes it with the diagnostic logs, and serves `daemon.crashList`. The raw dump never leaves the machine. No renderer-side crash module exists.
- **The `sidekicks://` link handler**, in the main process — **created.** `app.setAsDefaultProtocolClient('sidekicks')`. On macOS: `open-url` event handler. On Windows / Linux: `second-instance` event handler (the URL arrives via `argv` on secondary-instance launch). The URL is parsed in the main process by the one `sidekicks://` parser `packages/contracts` exports — the same form `Copy link` copies and `sidekicks open` sends — and the parsed request, `session/<id>` or `workflow-run/<runId>`, is handed to the renderer through `window.subscribeToNavigationRequest`. The renderer, which sees every window, places it: the view already showing that session is brought forward, otherwise the focused view of the window used last switches to it, and with no window open a window opens on it. A link to a session this install's list does not hold waits until the session list has loaded and then says so in one line, `That session is not here.`; a link held through a cold launch takes the same path. A notification click and a line in the bell's list arrive the same way. The renderer receives only the parsed request and never the raw URL.
- **Main's window-place file** — **created at T-020p-1B-5,** in `main/windows/` beside the window factory. Under `<app-user-data>`, beside the appearance record whose kept background paints its first frame: one entry per window, keyed by the window id the renderer's kept layout shares, holding its place, size, display and maximized or fullscreen state, put back before the window is built, a rectangle off every attached display pulled back on screen. A pane's own window keeps its place and size per pane kind. Read and written defensively: an empty or unreadable file brings the app up at the display's default size.
- **Main's fixture launch**, at the root of `main/` — **created.** Parses `--fixture <scenario>` and `--session <session-id>`, checks the name against the scenario catalog in the desktop app's `fixtures/` folder, and starts the window with fixture composition on and that name. An unknown scenario, a missing value, or a session the scenario does not hold stops startup with an error that names it; nothing falls back to another scenario. It is the one main-process module that imports `fixtures/`. A build without fixture capability contains no loader, so `--fixture` there is a startup error too, never a silent normal launch.
- **The Preview page host** — **created at Phase 8,** in the main process. One `WebContentsView` per open Preview page in the `persist:preview` partition, the in-process debugger link that carries the relay's traffic to the service, the device-metrics fit, popups as pages, downloads through the system save dialog, per-site clearing, the page budget and the page's right-click menu; §Phase 8 names each piece.

### Preload

- `apps/desktop/src/preload/index.ts` — **created.** Single `contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld('desktopBridge', bridge)` call per Spec-021. The bridge object's type is the desktop's preload API type, `PreloadApi`, in `apps/desktop/src/shared/preload-api.ts`, shared by main, the preload and the desktop's `PlatformBridge` implementation, which alone reads `window.desktopBridge`; a plan wiring a bridge namespace adds its members to that type and to the object the preload builds, rather than re-declaring the bridge shape.

### Contracts

- `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/services/platform/` — the `PlatformBridge` interface from [Preload Bridge Contract](../architecture/contracts/preload-bridge-contract.md), the front end's host-agnostic capability boundary that the desktop, the web client and the phone apps each implement, with `PlatformBridgeProvider`, through which the renderer receives it, and the desktop's implementation over the preload API. It imports the Plan-005 daemon types and the Plan-025 control-plane types from `packages/contracts` and declares `OpenDialogOptions` / `SaveDialogOptions` / `NotificationOptions` / `FilePathRef` / `UpdateState`. Exported as **type-only**, except the provider and the implementations. `PlatformBridgeProvider` resolves, beside the bridge, the clock the window runs on — one `RealClock` for a window that reads the preload, and a composition's own clock handed over with the bridge it builds — so neither a clock nor a fixture's scenario engine is a member of any bridge. No bridge carries an attention member: the attention projection is `attention.projectionRead` through `daemon`.
- `apps/desktop/src/shared/preload-api.ts` — `PreloadApi`, the desktop's preload API type, the type of `window.desktopBridge`, shared by main, the preload and the desktop's `PlatformBridge` implementation, and `createStubBridge()`, a bridge in which every member throws `NotImplementedError`, which the bridge-surface test compares the live bridge against and which frame tests mount as a bridge whose calls reach nothing. The preload lists every member itself (`createPreloadApi`), each carried over IPC; a member whose main handler is not built yet rejects with Electron's own no-handler error, which its caller surfaces in its own words. The negative test ([Spec-021 §Acceptance Criteria](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#acceptance-criteria): "No auth material appears on the bridge") is a TypeScript conditional-type test beside it.

**Bridge namespaces beyond the Spec-021 block (authoring rule).** [Preload Bridge Contract](../architecture/contracts/preload-bridge-contract.md)'s interface block binds the **partial** bridge shape verbatim; a remainder namespace joins it only where a spec section names this plan as the owner of that surface. A namespace with no such named delegation is a review rejection, and each one added here extends the same `PlatformBridge` interface and the desktop's preload API type so T-020r-2-1's exhaustive registration keeps failing the build on an unhandled method.

### Renderer

- `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/main.tsx` — **created.** React entrypoint. Renders `<App />` into `#root`.
- `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/app/App.tsx` — **created.** Top-level router + layout. Mounts each Signature Feature view as a route; the views themselves are created by their owning plans in their own `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/` subtrees ([Spec-021 §Signature Feature Composition Sketches](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#signature-feature-composition-sketches) names the owner of each). This plan owns the router, the layout chrome, and the composition points — never a feature view's body.
- `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/layout/`, `app/AppRouter.tsx` and `features/composer/` — **created by this plan in the remainder.** The Plan-020-owned layout chrome, route switch and session composer. `features/composer/` is the composer's pinned home — deliberately in this plan's own folder and never inside a feature-view tree owned by Plan-010 or Plan-013.
- The console's renderer folders under `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/` (`app/`, `layout/`, `features/`, `registries/`, `services/`, `store/`, `routing/`, `components/`, `hooks/`, `lib/`, `styles/` and `assets/`) — **built as the supplement Phase 1C** (T-020p-1C-1..8) inside the [Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian)](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#console-design-meridian) envelope. They hold the Meridian tokens and shared components, the icon rail, the all-sessions list, the session views a window holds side by side (each view's header, transcript, composer and pane layout), the pane registry and its single mount point, the command palette and keybinding service, the settings pages' frame, and the console's test tiers. **Fixtures sit outside the tree.** The desktop app's `fixtures/` folder holds scenario definitions and scripted data only — `scenarios/<scenario>.ts` per scenario, shared `data/`, and `index.ts`, the catalog main's fixture launch checks — and each fixture implementation sits beside the real boundary it substitutes (a service module's `<module>.fixture.ts` beside it, a feature's `fixtures/`), answering only the operations that boundary's real client exposes. The renderer's `app/` picks the fixture implementations once, at startup, and nothing else checks for fixtures. Fixture-only types stay in the desktop app and never reach `packages/contracts`, and no fixture code or catalog is compiled into a release build. **Phase 1C rule.** The tree is built ahead of Phase 5 because every surface it holds is a projection over bridge namespaces the corpus already registers or that a task in this plan builds, and because [Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian)](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#console-design-meridian) is the envelope it stays within; a console task mints no wire method, event type, error code, table, or column, and one it needs is stated in its owning spec and plan first. It **absorbs by import** the shipped partial renderer components and any later plan-owned feature, and never re-authors or moves a body another plan owns: it authors none of Plan-010's transcript rows (`features/transcript/rows/`) or its context ring (in `features/composer/`), and nothing in Plan-002's run controls (`features/composer/run/controls/`), which their owners build in those folders and the console mounts on the CP-020-2 host-mounts shape, while the composer's folder, `features/composer/`, is this plan's own and holds the composer's pinned home (authored by T-020p-1C-3, wired live by T-020r-5-2). **Adoption.** Phase 5 adopts the console by making `AppShell` (`layout/AppShell/`) the thin host and by moving the app's chrome T-020r-5-1 names into the console's app frame (T-020r-5-1 EXTEND). When the renderer scheme is not privileged, the console's persistence layer runs on an in-memory adapter and reports that state in its own settings page.
- `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/desktop-bridge.d.ts` — **already created; the remainder EXTENDS, does not CREATE.** Types the `window.desktopBridge` global the preload installs with `contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld`, which carries no static type of its own: a `declare global` block giving `Window` a `desktopBridge` member of the preload API type, `PreloadApi` from `src/shared/preload-api.ts`. Both renderer type graphs include it, the production one and the test one. Remainder work extends this surface as the bridge grows; it does not re-create the file or re-declare the global.

**The frame, as build targets.** The console's app frame is the one surface no feature owns, so this plan builds it and every pane kind lands inside it. Each target below names what must be built and how the build knows it is done.

- **The three-column grid.** The icon rail at its own derived width · the sessions track · the row of session views taking the rest. The sessions track is nothing wide while it is closed and, while it holds the sessions list or the notifications list, takes the width the person last dragged it to, and until they have dragged it the width a session row's own furniture needs at the current text size. The grid's own track widths animate, so the row of views slides across rather than being covered by a layer. **Done when** opening and closing the track moves the row of views, the dragged width survives a close and reopen, and nothing is drawn over the views.
- **The row of session views.** A window holds one or more session views side by side, each one session's header, transcript, composer and pane row, sharing the window's width at no less than the conversation's floor plus one open pane's floor each, the rest of its panes scrolling inside its own pane row; when the window cannot hold every view at its floor, the row scrolls sideways and keeps the focused view in sight. A new window opens with one view. **Done when** a window of three views narrower than their floors scrolls sideways with the focused view in sight, and no view is drawn under its floor.
- **The floors.** A window-height floor derived as the shortest window in which the header, the flow and the composer still stack. The width floor is summed at run time from the parts that must fit at once — the rail, the sessions track and one session view's floor (the conversation's own floor and one pane's floor) — rather than written down as a figure, so it moves with the text size, and a window holding several views scrolls them sideways rather than growing its floor. A pane's own window refuses to shrink below that pane's floor. **Done when** neither floor is a constant in the source and no window can be sized under its floor.
- **The fold, which a desktop window never reaches.** A desktop window never shrinks below its floor, so it never folds. The one grid shape change, the fold to a single column, belongs to Remote Control's phone and browser surfaces, which run this same front end through their own implementation of the one bridge interface — a member a host cannot serve is absent — and have no window floor. There the content decides the fold from what fits (the flow's floor against what the sessions track and an open pane need at the current text size), never a device breakpoint and never a media query on a device width, and a folded screen shows one level at a time with one back control. **Done when** the desktop window refuses a size under its floor at every text size, and the browser tier drives the screen folded at the width the content needs, at no fixed figure, as well as at 1440 × 900.
- **The pane row.** A view's panes form one block beside the conversation: a row of main panes — the inspector, Review, Sidekicks, Preview and the file view, each at most once, the file view being where a chat's written file opens — side by side, as many as are open, with no cap. Opening a pane adds it to the row beside the focused pane and replaces none. The block sits to the right of the conversation by default and moves to the left as a whole, and the side is kept per session. Each pane keeps its own floor and the conversation keeps its own; when the next pane does not fit, the pane row scrolls sideways, refusing nothing and folding nothing, and a newly opened pane is scrolled into view through the scroll chokepoint. Review, Terminal, Preview and the file view can each be taken full, which collapses every other pane. **Done when** opening a second and a third pane keeps the first open, a row wider than the view scrolls sideways with the newly opened pane in view and no pane and no conversation drawn under its floor, full width leaves one pane drawn, and leaving full width restores the row as it was.
- **The terminal row.** The terminal sits above or below the main panes, at the terminal height and as wide as the pane row; with no main pane open it fills the column beside the conversation. The session column spans the height whichever side the terminal is on. **Done when** a terminal above and below the row measures the row's width, and the session column is untouched by either.
- **The drag.** While a divider is under the pointer the frame knows which pane that divider belongs to, or that it is the terminal's height being dragged; a pane's header drags the pane to a new place in the row, to the other side of the conversation, or the terminal above or below the row, and Alt+Shift with an arrow key moves the focused pane the same way. **Done when** a divider drag changes only the width or the height it belongs to, and a header drag and the arrow chord each land the pane where they aimed (T-020r-5-12).
- **The main column.** Inside the session track: header · surface · the docked composer, in that order. **Done when** the composer is docked to the column and never positioned against the window.

### Build / Config

- `apps/desktop/electron.vite.config.ts` — **created.** `electron-vite` config: main / preload / renderer targets; source-code protection (opt-in; obfuscates main + preload output); HMR on dev; strict TypeScript. The renderer's `build.cssTarget` names the Chromium the pinned Electron carries and the Safari and iOS versions the web client and the phone app run on, so the build writes the `-webkit-` forms the one front end needs in WebKit, and `index.html` declares its doctype and `utf-8`. The fixture gate is on in the `development` and `fixtures` modes and in no release build.
- `apps/desktop/electron-builder.yml` — **created.** Multi-platform config: macOS (`.dmg` to install and `.zip` for updates, arm64 + x64, hardened runtime, the self-signed identity until the Developer ID certificate exists), Windows (NSIS per user, x64 + arm64, signed through SignPath Foundation), Linux (AppImage, `.deb` and `.rpm`, x64 + arm64, the packages published to the signed apt and dnf repository). No MSI, Snap, Flatpak or staged rollout. ASAR packing; the `electronFuses` setting carrying the fuse posture from [Spec-021 §Security Hardening Baseline](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#security-hardening-baseline) and the ASAR integrity embed, both applied by `electron-builder` before it signs, so the digest and the fuse wire are part of what gets signed; the `publish` entry for the stock GitHub provider; the install kind recorded in `resources/package-type`; `mac.icon` pointing at `build/icon.icon`, with an `.icns` made from it for macOS before 26 and the Windows and Linux icon files made from the same source.
- `apps/desktop/build/code-sign.ts` — **created.** Orchestrates signing: macOS (detached `xcrun notarytool submit` + resumable poll + `xcrun stapler staple` — see §Notarization Submission And Poll below); Windows (SignPath Foundation's signing of the NSIS installers and of `sidekicks-windows-half.exe` for x64 and arm64); Linux (no project key: Cloudsmith's per-repository key signs each RPM on upload and the apt and dnf repository metadata, and a `.deb` is checked through apt's signed Release file).
- `apps/desktop/eslint.config.mjs` — **created; a structure-gate carrier.** Flat-config ESLint; `no-restricted-imports` bans `require` / `process` / `global` / `electron` modules from the renderer (`apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/**`), and the same config carries the structural rules `apps/desktop/AGENTS.md` enumerates — the bridge-global, refresh and spawn chokepoints, the `export default` and module-level-`let` bans, the stylesheet-import and directory-`import.meta.glob` rules, the text-snapshot-matcher ban, because a text snapshot records whatever the code produced on its first run and passes, and the two `eslint-plugin-perfectionist` member-order rules (`sort-modules` and `sort-classes`, both `type: 'unsorted'` and both scoped to `src/renderer/src/**`, carrying the renderer file-section order and the class order that `apps/desktop/AGENTS.md` lists among its mechanical gates, and nothing alphabetical) — each stating its own scope and its lifted file classes. This is a **CI gate** — any violation fails the build (I-020-9).
- `knip.json` (repository root, the `apps/desktop` workspace) — **created.** The `structure:dead-code` gate's configuration: entry roots, project globs, the Vitest plugin, and `ignoreExportsUsedInFile`. Run from the workspace root, so the pnpm catalog and the root tooling manifest resolve; `--treat-config-hints-as-errors` makes a stale pattern fail the gate. It carries no `ignore` entry for an export: an export a document still claims and nothing imports yet carries a `@consumedBy <plain words>` tag naming no document or task, deleted in the change that imports it (I-020-9).
- The desktop app's `.dependency-cruiser.mjs` — **created.** The `structure:layering` gate's rule set: `no-circular`, `no-orphans`, the process-boundary rules, `shared-imports-nothing`, `feature-isolation`, and one `layering-<layer>` rule per renderer layer encoding the renderer import direction [Desktop App Implementation Notes §Console Test Tiers](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#console-test-tiers) names as forbidden upward edges (I-020-9).
- `apps/desktop/AGENTS.md` + `apps/desktop/CLAUDE.md` — **created.** The package's structure standard, tool-neutral so every agent in the repository reads it; `CLAUDE.md` is the one-line `@AGENTS.md` import. It states the layout, the import boundaries, the chokepoints, the module shape, and the mechanical gates as binding developer rules, and it is the home of every structural rule the gate configs cannot express. It mints nothing and states no rule this plan or `Spec-021` does not already carry.

#### Notarization Submission And Poll

[Desktop App Implementation Notes §Code Signing And Notarization](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#code-signing-and-notarization) supplies the **requirement** — the release pipeline "must include timeout + retry logic rather than synchronous blocking on notarization" — and records the observed Apple-queue delay range of **24–120+ hours**. It states no timeout value; this plan owns the concrete mechanism and the ceiling, set above that observed range so a normal queue never trips it. The design is **detached-submit + resumable poll**, not a single blocking wait:

1. **Submit detached.** `xcrun notarytool submit <artifact> --no-wait -f json` returns immediately with a submission `id`. `--no-wait` is `notarytool`'s default; the flag is passed explicitly so the shape survives a future default change.
2. **Persist the submission id.** The `id` is written to the release job's artifact store keyed by `{version, platform, artifact}` before the job may exit. This is what makes the poll resumable: a CI job that ends — timeout, runner eviction, deliberate release-engineer detach — loses nothing, because Apple's Notary service keeps processing regardless. `notarytool --timeout` is a **polling** limit, not a submission cancel: per the tool's own help text, "The Notary service will continue processing even if the timeout is reached."
3. **Poll in bounded increments.** Each poll invocation runs `xcrun notarytool wait <id> --timeout 30m -f json` (or `notarytool info <id>` for a non-blocking status read). A poll that returns `In Progress` re-queues rather than failing the release. No single CI job is held open for days.
4. **Overall abandonment ceiling: 168 hours (7 days)** from first submission, accumulated across resumed polls. The ceiling sits above the spec's documented 120-hour upper bound with headroom, so it is an abandonment signal for a genuinely stuck submission — never a resubmission trigger for a merely slow queue. Resubmission is a manual release-engineering decision after the ceiling trips, because Apple's queue treats a resubmission as new work and a resubmit-on-timeout loop is self-defeating.
5. **Terminal handling.** `Accepted` → `xcrun stapler staple <artifact>` and the artifact is releasable. `Invalid` / `Rejected` → `xcrun notarytool log <id>` is captured into the release record and the artifact is not distributed.

Pinned tool version: `notarytool` 1.1.2 (41), the version shipped with the CI image's Xcode command-line tools; the detached-submit + `info` / `wait` / `log`-by-submission-id surface this design depends on is verified against it. A change in that surface shows as a failing notarization step and is investigated when seen.

### Test

- `apps/desktop/tests/e2e/` — **created.** Playwright `_electron` test files, each `*.test.ts`.
  - `launch.test.ts` — asserts app launches, main window appears, renderer bundle loads via custom protocol, `window.desktopBridge` is defined, `window.require` is `undefined`.
  - `fuses.test.ts` — asserts `@electron/fuses.getCurrentFuseWire()` on the packaged binary returns the posture from Spec-021.
  - `bridge-surface.test.ts` — asserts the `window.desktopBridge` surface matches the desktop's preload API type, `PreloadApi`, in `apps/desktop/src/shared/preload-api.ts`; asserts no property name containing `token` / `dpop` / `secret` is exposed.
  - The `sidekicks://` link end-to-end test — asserts a valid `sidekicks://session/<id>` reaches the rendered session and a valid `sidekicks://workflow-run/<runId>` the run's page, that a malformed address is refused at the parser boundary with nothing routed, that a link to a session the list does not hold answers `That session is not here.` only once the session list has loaded, and that a link handed to the app at a cold launch takes that same path.

- `apps/desktop/tests/` — **already created; this plan owns what each tier must prove.** One directory per tier of [Desktop App Implementation Notes §Console Test Tiers](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#console-test-tiers) plus the harnesses they share in `tests/helpers/` (the Electron launcher, the app harness, the scenario clock, the fixture bundle). What follows is the console's acceptance list: a console surface is done when its build has proved these, in this order.

**Unit — the pure derivations.** The ship strip's next and alternate action from the uncommitted, ahead, pushed and pull-request facts, from a half-finished merge, rebase or bisect that takes `Commit` away, and from a failed read that leaves both actions absent. The live clock's text on both sides of 60 s. The depth-first child tree and the ancestry chain. The activity string per child state. The child state machine's transitions and their side effects — freeze, held, un-held — and the per-identifier fan-out of `Interrupt everything` and `Stop all running` over the daemon's own tree. The fork's title and worktree-name derivation, and worktree-name validation. The markdown serializer behind Copy. The console word table: each word with its caught spellings and the control it presses, the argument each control takes, and the three cases sent as typed with nothing pressed — an argument the control cannot take, a control with nothing to act on, and a word that cannot run right now — the three configuration keys the console catches against every other key forwarded as typed, and the review word's target words. On the settings side: the route grammar over its three arms, including a selection a page's vocabulary does not know; the page list, exactly and in order; the text-size step to root-font-size mapping; each of the two retention controls' ranges against the figures the corpus fixes; the chord-conflict refusal, including the reserved chord; and the billing-mode total that spans two modes.

**Component — every state from fixture data.** Each region renders every state the surface set names and every state in the scenario list of [Console Fixture Scenarios](../architecture/console-fixture-scenarios.md), from fixture data alone, including the cases where a control is absent rather than disabled on a chat session. Each settings page renders over its three read arms — in flight, refused, served — the refused arm asserting the service's own words on screen. The Projects draft over `Save`, `Revert` and close-while-editing. The accounts list over loading, empty, failure, no-default, login-expired and cannot-tell, and `Sign out` against `Remove`: the first leaves the row listed, the second is refused while a run holds the account. The token field asserted write-only — never read back, never held in serializable state, cleared on submit. The tool-server page over its degraded arm, asserting that the controls missing input are absent and every other control is still offered. The Notifications page over its switches, the kinds beneath the second (grayed with `Turned off above.` while it is off), the web address and the email digest.

**Browser tier.** Headless, never a visible window, never taking focus, at 1440 × 900 and again at a window narrow enough to fold the screen to one column: every scenario in the list of [Console Fixture Scenarios](../architecture/console-fixture-scenarios.md), plus the terminal pane, pane drag, pane keyboard resize, a pane moved by its header and by Alt+Shift with an arrow, the pane row scrolling sideways with a newly opened pane in view, double-click reset, width persistence, minimum and maximum clamping, full-width mode and its Escape, the compact word, the color-scheme cycle, the sessions search, `Open folder…` and the `Fast` toggle. Every run requires zero page errors and zero console errors, and asserts zero horizontal overflow in every pane on every screen. Settings runs its own pass per page state: list, page, change a control, refusal, rollback.

**Screen tier — the structural readings.** The built Settings screen proves the entries in order, one page drawn at a time, one row per control the page set names, the Notifications page's rows as the page set lists them and no sound control, Appearance's two theme swatches, Meridian and Graphite, with the default one marked, the transcript-width slider under `Text size` at its derived default with its floor, its ceiling, its one-root step, its readout following the knob and read again at a new text size, and its double-click reset, the Keyboard page's groups over its rows and keys with its lines and with `Rebind` and `Reset` absent until a row is hovered or focused, the Providers page's single `Default` mark per provider with its per-account freshness switch and the window-starting switch nested under it and the one-time memory import settling from its button through its progress to its line, the MCP page's masked value fields with no read-back, the three-line delete confirmation word for word, the two environment lists with a project's row overriding the one above it and a credential-shaped name turned away with nothing written, and the output-style and advisor rows under Claude Code and under no other provider. The type-scale rule and the derived-size rule are swept over the whole screen and proved by doubling the root font size.

**Screen sweeps, run over every state.** No runtime error; the word `sidekick` where the generic word would be; no sentence beginning with `You`; and no wire spelling on screen. The banned-word set is a review obligation rather than a tier, because no tier reads source, configuration or documentation text.

**Tripwires that must never regress.** The session-row status dots step and never ease into a smooth pulse — a rule about those dots alone and never a global one. No status dot in the session header, and no sessions control in it where the two columns are side by side; the one-column layout's back control, a left chevron and the word `Sessions`, is the one thing the header gains where the list is not on screen. No goal element and no goal action in the More menu. No in-repo worktree path string. No overflow menu on a child row. No "via the parent" text. No eyebrow above a user turn naming the user. No standing cost or quota figure outside the inspector's `Cost` section and the agents pane's per-agent spend.

**Color.** Every color-asserting check runs in all four theme-by-scheme cells; layout checks run once, because layout is theme-invariant.

**Rules no screen check can reach.** A rule whose subject is the daemon's or the main process's rather than the screen's is proved by the tier that owns it, and so is the half of the browser rule no one screen can stand in for: the saved site-data set, Settings › Browser and its per-site `Forget`, and the process inventory. On the settings side there are four: status arriving on a live stream, subscribing before the list is read, a binding never written to a wire, and the theme restored before the first paint. Where a rule is partly the screen's and partly the build's — what the next session starts with, the limits read on each provider's own cadence and what it never does, a value never held in serializable state, the setup steps running with the repository's own git config — the screen's half is asserted on screen and the build's half is named as the build's.

**Provider parity, live, run by hand**, like the live provider smoke suite, on both providers with the same expected end state. The undo scenario: a file the agent added is gone, a file it changed is back, and the file a shell command wrote is back — then the same again with a background child's edit. The Codex pause as the boundary interrupt: the hook holds the next call, the turn interrupt ends the turn, the hook denies the parked call, and the next turn start continues, with the line reading `Pausing · a command still running` until the command ends. The `Interrupt` press with a command running, the line's state slot reading `Interrupted · a command still running` on Codex until the daemon has ended it, and the row settling as `Stopped by you` on both. A foreground child surviving a Claude Code interrupt. The retry, the exhausted retry and the compaction, from proxy error injection on Claude Code. The limit row from Codex's rate-limit frame. A child re-attached by its persisted identifier after a daemon restart. Claude Code's interrupt ends the operating-system process of the lead's own foreground command within 41 ms, and closing the command-line tool's standard input never orphans a command because the tool waits for it: this run re-checks that rather than measures it.

**Several windows, Electron tier, headless, never a visible window.** Two views in one window and a view in a window of its own: a row press on a session on screen elsewhere brings that view forward and switches nothing; `⌘`-click, `Open beside`, `Open in new window` and a dragged title each land the session in one place only; a `sidekicks://` address, `sidekicks open` and a notification click route to the session's view where it is on screen and otherwise to the focused view of the window used last; a pane's own window stays when the window it came from closes and returns to its session's view, or opens it in the window used last; every window, its views and every pane window come back after a restart; closing every window stops no session. A view's title dragged onto another view in its window swaps the two; dragged over another window it shows that window's drop place and, released, joins that window's row there; dragged past every window it becomes a window of its own that follows the pointer and stays where it is released. The same undo run with two sessions in one folder puts back only its own session's files and lists the other's as `also changed by <session>`. The memory and processor budget of four windows showing eight sessions, two of them streaming a turn, is measured against one window showing one session, for resident memory, processor time while idle and while streaming, and a view's switch time.

**Drags, on real input.** Every drag test drives the pointer as trusted input, never a synthetic DOM event. A drag inside a window runs in the browser tier, its input sent through the browser's own input pipeline, in the test page's document and again in a second document the page draws into, the same one-script, many-documents shape as the console's windows. A drag between windows and the live tear-off run in the Electron tier through the Playwright `_electron` harness, in the first window and a second window the console opened. Each checks: the dragged item stays under the pointer while it moves, its neighbors glide apart, the drop glides into place and commits the new order once, Escape leaves everything as it was, and the keyboard path makes the same change with its live-region announcement. It runs for the terminal's tabs, Preview's tabs, the waiting messages, the pane row and the session views' titles.

**Fixture launch.** Every tier that plays a scenario starts the app with `--fixture <scenario>` (and `--session <session-id>` where it opens one) and nothing else; a test proves that an unknown scenario, a missing value and a session the scenario does not hold each stop startup with an error naming it, and that a release build refuses `--fixture` at startup.

**What the Preview build must prove on its own.**

- **Unit.** The address parser over every accepted shape and every refused one, including a path on the current page. The mark hit-test against the accessibility snapshot's element boxes. The renumbering that skips strokes. The page-budget eviction order — oldest idle first, address kept. The cookie adapter between Playwright's record and Electron's, including the `expires` to `expirationDate` rename. The two provider row shapes normalizing to one row.
- **Component.** Each of the four body states — the host rectangle, the live picture, the server rows or the one `No dev server is running in <project>` line, and the load error with `Try again` — the frozen-marking state, the chip's three texts, the tools at rest and pressed, the hue slider appearing only under Pen, and the attachment chip from zero marks to many — all from fixture data.
- **Electron tier, headless, never a visible window.** The dual-client case end to end: two clients attached to one page through the daemon's relay, driving it from both, with exact counter arithmetic and zero detach events. `capturePage` on a hidden view, asserting the scale is read from the returned image and not from `getScaleFactors()`. The screencast's acknowledgment loop, asserting the stream does not stall at three frames. `connectOverCDP(transport)` over the relay listing exactly the open Preview pages with the console absent, a page session's `Target.getTargets` and its attach to any other target refused, and no debug port open on the machine — the regression that catches the relay's filter going missing or a `BrowserWindow` sneaking back in. Preview's scenarios drive this relay path and never a port.
- **Process inventory.** `app.getAppMetrics()` asserting one renderer per open page, one browser per machine, and no per-session process beyond that; the release of the oldest idle page when `process.availableMemory()` falls below the headroom, its renderer gone from `getAppMetrics()` within 1.5 s, and the ten-minute releases.
- **Provider round trip.** One browser call on Claude Code and one on Codex producing the same flow row; one marks attachment reaching each provider in its own image shape.
- **Tripwires.** No browser-style tab strip in the pane chrome. No devtools control. No search. No download list. No dialog, overlay, backdrop or scrim in the pane. No picture in place of a real page on the machine that runs the session, except the still picture laid in the page's slot while its pane moves (T-020r-8-6).
- **Run by hand, never in continuous integration.** The provider round trip runs live, like the live provider smoke suite; the dual-client case, the hidden-view capture and the screencast's acknowledgment loop are the headless Electron tier's above. The app works with whatever provider, Electron or Playwright version is installed, and nothing is re-checked on a version or pin bump; a changed behavior shows in the daemon's logs, the transcript or a failing test, and is investigated when seen. Two claims a build must not take from third-party write-ups: that `getScaleFactors()` gives the device pixel ratio, which returns `[1]` against a real two-times capture, and that private memory can be read on macOS, which is `undefined` at this Electron.

## Data And Storage Changes

No Postgres or Durable Object schema changes, and no entry in the operating system's keystore: this machine's DPoP key, the hosted account's tokens, this machine's identity key and every other secret are the daemon's, and the local daemon session token is read from its file at every connect and never cached, because the daemon makes a new one at every restart. The desktop app stores only:

### Main-process-local config

- Main's window-place file under `<app-user-data>` — one entry per window, keyed by the window id the renderer's kept layout shares: its place, size, display and maximized or fullscreen state, and a pane's own window's place and size per pane kind. Put back before a window is built; kept beside the appearance record whose kept background paints its first frame. Written whole and atomically; an empty or unreadable file brings the app up at the display's default size.
- `<app-user-data>/appearance.json` — the appearance record: the theme, the color scheme, the text size, the transcript width, and the current theme's light and dark ground colors, every value an enum or a number. Main reads it to paint the first frame before the page loads; `window.setAppearance` writes it. A missing or broken file reads as the defaults and main repairs it; an unreadable file or a failed repair reads as the defaults and is recorded in main's diagnostic log.
- `<app-user-data>/keyboard-map.json` — the keyboard map: only the overridden rows, keyed by act, read and written through `keyboardMap.read()` / `keyboardMap.write()`. A missing or broken file reads as the chords the app ships with, main repairs it, and the Keyboard page says both happened.
- `<app-user-data>/updater-state.json` — the last release version seen and nothing else, which the next check's refusal of an older release reads; a staged update is read from `electron-updater`'s own cache. Managed by the updater. Main reads and writes it whether or not the service runs, and no wire writes it; a missing or broken file reads as the installed version.

`<app-user-data>` resolves per Electron `app.getPath('userData')`: `~/Library/Application Support/AI Sidekicks` (macOS), `%APPDATA%\AI Sidekicks` (Windows), `~/.config/AI Sidekicks` (Linux).

Each of these files is readable and writable only by the person, written whole and atomically over a schema-checked parse, and never written to a wire.

**The machine's settings file is not the app's.** `<home>/.ai-sidekicks/machine-settings.json` holds the machine-wide preferences — the auto-update preference, `Keep crash reports`, the editor, Claude Code's advisor default, the default checkout, the new-session and keep-awake switches, the notification preferences other than their secrets, the browser's switches, the screen-reader switch, the project environment rows, the backup switch and folder, the folder new clones go into, the branch-name pattern and voice's settings. The background service is its only writer: main writes a change through `machineSettings.write(change)`, which hands it to the service's `daemon.machineSettingsUpdate {change}`, and every window reads it through `machineSettings.read()` and `machineSettings.subscribe()`, which carry `daemon.machineSettingsRead` and `daemon.machineSettingsSubscribe`. Main reads the file directly only before the service first answers, and only the auto-update preference and `Keep crash reports`, which it needs at start; the renderer never reads it.

### Build-time asset

## API And Transport Changes

### Preload bridge contract (new, owned by this plan)

The full `PlatformBridge` interface from [Preload Bridge Contract](../architecture/contracts/preload-bridge-contract.md) lives in the renderer's `services/platform/` with `PlatformBridgeProvider`; the desktop's implementation of it reads `window.desktopBridge`, whose type is the desktop's preload API type, `PreloadApi`, in `apps/desktop/src/shared/preload-api.ts`. Key requirements:

- All methods return `Promise<T>` or a synchronous primitive; no callbacks (`ipcRenderer`-style pub-sub uses the `subscribe(...): Unsubscribe` pattern).
- No property or method signature references `Token`, `DPoP` or `SessionToken` in its type or return shape.
- `FilePathRef` is an opaque branded type (`string & { __brand: 'FilePathRef' }`); the renderer cannot dereference a filesystem path — dereferencing requires a second bridge round trip.

### Daemon JSON-RPC forwarding

- Wire format: Content-Length-framed JSON-RPC 2.0 per Spec-006 / ADR-009.
- Transport: Unix domain socket (macOS / Linux) or named pipe (Windows) per Spec-006. The main process instantiates the client from Plan-005's shared client SDK.
- The main process presents the daemon session token as the first message on its connection, in `daemon.hello`; the renderer never sees the token.

### Control-plane tRPC + WebSocket forwarding

- Path: main forwards each renderer control-plane call to the background service over its own connection to the daemon; the service speaks tRPC v11 over HTTPS for request/response and the Spec-027 binary wire frames on the relay WSS connection; the control plane carries no presence events (ADR-013, Spec-027).
- Main attaches no control-plane credential and holds none. The service presents this machine's access token under `Authorization: DPoP` with a fresh DPoP proof on every outbound HTTP request, and authenticates the relay WSS connection with the short-lived relay `connectionToken` over the `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` subprotocol. The desktop app reaches the person's other machines as its machine, never as a linked device: the service negotiates and authenticates each channel to another machine with this machine's credentials.
- Response sanitization: the main process strips `set-cookie` headers and any upstream auth-material-carrying headers before returning the response body to the renderer.

### Deep-link transport

- Protocol: `sidekicks://<action>/<...args>`. Actions: `session/<id>` and `workflow-run/<runId>`. The form is one shared contract: `packages/contracts` exports the one function that composes an address and the one that parses it, and `Copy link`, main's handler and `sidekicks open` all use them, so an address copied from the app opens the same place from the command line or the operating system.
- URL arrival:
  - macOS: `app.on('open-url', (event, url) => ...)` handler.
  - Windows / Linux: `app.on('second-instance', (event, argv) => argv.find(a => a.startsWith('sidekicks://')))` handler; the URL is the last `argv` entry on secondary-instance launch.

### Update feed transport

- Transport: HTTPS GET against the project's GitHub Releases.
- Update format: `electron-builder`'s update files (`latest.yml` and its per-platform siblings), read by `electron-updater`'s stock GitHub provider, which checks the SHA-512 they list on each download. The release manifest beside them lists each artifact's SHA-256 for the service's own update; the app's updater does not read it.
- Artifact format: `.dmg` to install and `.zip` for updates (macOS), NSIS `.exe` for x64 and arm64 (Windows), AppImage (Linux). A `.deb` or `.rpm` install updates through the apt or dnf repository and the package manager, never through the app.
- Blockmap delta downloads where the platform has one: NSIS, the macOS zip once a previous update is cached, and AppImage.

## Invariants

These give the plan's behaviors a named `I-020-N` namespace, so every remainder task's `Verifies invariant:` marker resolves against a plan-side id instead of pointing at an unnumbered [Spec-021 §Acceptance Criteria](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#acceptance-criteria) bullet, which a task can only cite by inventing an `AC-N` number the spec does not carry.

| ID | Invariant | Tasks |
| --- | --- | --- |
| I-020-1 | No property or method on the `PlatformBridge` or the desktop's preload API surface carries auth material: no name matches `/token\|dpop\|secret/i` and no return type transitively contains one, except names that carry no credential (token counts and limits, a workflow secret's id, the service's secrets file path, the environment variable a tool server's bearer token is read from, the handle the service mints for a listed folder, a screencast frame's acknowledgment id, a webhook token's dates), a question's masked-answer flag, and the two credentials the design shows once, each only on its own rotate result: the notification web address's signing secret (`attention.webAddressSecretRotate`) and a workflow webhook's token (`workflow.webhookTokenRotate`) ([Preload Bridge Contract](../architecture/contracts/preload-bridge-contract.md), [Spec-021 §Acceptance Criteria](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#acceptance-criteria)). | T-020p-1-4 (partial, type-level); T-020r-2-1, T-020r-2-2, T-020r-2-3, T-020p-1B-3 (project registration; enables the per-module units), T-020r-7-1 |
| I-020-2 | Every window is constructed as a `BaseWindow` and every document it shows as a `WebContentsView` carrying the locked `webPreferences` block, loading over the custom protocol and never `file://`; a `BrowserWindow` is used nowhere ([Spec-021 §Security Hardening Baseline](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#security-hardening-baseline), [ADR-034](../decisions/034-embedded-browser-for-preview.md)). | T-020p-1-3 (partial, the window factory's test); T-020p-1B-1, T-020p-1B-2, T-020p-1B-5 (partial supplement — the load, the one locked constructor and main's registry of windows); T-020r-3-3, T-020r-8-1, T-020r-7-1 |
| I-020-3 | `electron-builder` flips the fuses (its `electronFuses` setting) and embeds the ASAR integrity digest before it signs, so the signature covers both and no step after signing changes the packaged binary, because either change would invalidate the signature ([Desktop App Implementation Notes §Build And Packaging](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#build-and-packaging), [Desktop App Implementation Notes §Code Signing And Notarization](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#code-signing-and-notarization)). | T-020r-6-1, T-020r-6-2 |
| I-020-4 | Test-only probe code is compile-time eliminated from release bundles; the `__SMOKE_BUILD__` define collapses the guard so no probe body survives tree-shaking ([Spec-021 §Pitfalls To Avoid](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#pitfalls-to-avoid)). | T-020p-1-2 |
| I-020-5 | The renderer imports no Node built-in, no Electron module, and nothing under `src/main/` or `src/preload/`; the ESLint restricted-imports rule is a CI gate, not advice ([Spec-021 §Trust Stance](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#trust-stance)). | T-020p-1-6 |
| I-020-6 | The renderer scheme is registered through `protocol.registerSchemesAsPrivileged` as `standard: true` and `secure: true` **before** `app.ready`, so the renderer origin has IndexedDB and `localStorage`; a registration after ready, or a scheme left non-standard, fails the startup-composition test ([Desktop App Implementation Notes §Renderer Bundle](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#renderer-bundle), [Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian)](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#console-design-meridian) §Persistence on the renderer scheme). | T-020p-1B-1, T-020p-1B-3, T-020r-3-3 |
| I-020-7 | Every window is a `BaseWindow` built through the one private locked constructor, and none is the main one. A window that shows the console — the first window, a new window, a session's own window, a side pane's own window — is built by main's window-open handler around a `window.open` child of the hidden console document main builds at start and never shows, the first window a person sees included, which keeps the renderer's sandbox, context isolation and CSP and carries no preload and no bridge of its own, so one renderer and one set of stores and daemon subscriptions drive every window, and no such window is ever in Preview's relay target list ([Spec-021 §Main Process Responsibilities](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#main-process-responsibilities), [Spec-021 §Security Hardening Baseline](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#security-hardening-baseline), [Preload Bridge Contract](../architecture/contracts/preload-bridge-contract.md)). | T-020p-1B-2, T-020p-1B-3, T-020r-3-3, T-020p-1B-5, T-020r-8-5, T-020r-7-1 |
