| I-020-8 | Fixture code stays outside the product. Scenario definitions and scripted data live in the desktop app's `fixtures/` folder; each fixture implementation sits beside the real boundary it substitutes and answers only the operations that boundary's real client exposes; the renderer's fixture composition in `app/` picks them once, at startup, and nothing else checks for fixtures; fixture-only types never reach `packages/contracts`; and only the `development` and `fixtures` builds carry fixture code, so `--fixture` on a release build is a startup error. An unknown scenario, a missing value or a session the scenario does not hold stops startup with an error that names it, and nothing falls back to another scenario ([Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian)](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#console-design-meridian)). | T-020p-1C-1..7, T-020r-5-1 |
| I-020-9 | Every console PR passes the bundle-size budget [Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian)](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#console-design-meridian) §Budgets sets, the tiers [Desktop App Implementation Notes §Console Test Tiers](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#console-test-tiers) registers other than accessibility and endurance, and the three gates the [Desktop App Implementation Notes §Console Libraries](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#console-libraries) structure-enforcement row admits: `structure:dead-code` (no file, export, type, or dependency the entry points do not reach), `structure:layering` (no cycle, no orphan, no process-boundary break, and no upward edge against the renderer import direction), and `lint` (the renderer import bans and the structural rules `apps/desktop/AGENTS.md` enumerates). The accessibility and endurance tiers land after the macOS build is complete (T-020p-1C-8) and gate every PR from then on. The other budgets that section sets — p95 frame time under four streaming lanes, renderer heap at rest and under the `concurrent-streaming` scenario streaming live, idle and streaming CPU, per-terminal memory, and first-transcript-row latency — are measured on their named workload in the PR of the task that builds their subject. The gates run in CI; a budget is raised only in the spec, and an export a document still claims carries a `@consumedBy` tag rather than a `knip` ignore entry. | T-020p-1C-1..8 |
| I-020-10 | One place per session and per destination, and a window seam that runs one way. A session is shown in one view at a time across every window, and each destination (Sidekicks, Skills, Workflows, Settings) in one window at a time, so the only thing a window refuses is a second view of a session or a destination already on screen: asking for it, by any route, brings that view's window forward and focuses it. The sessions feature asks for windows through the renderer's window client over `window.*`, and main's window owner builds, focuses and places the native windows; main never reads or changes the renderer's layout, and the daemon never learns about windows, so another device sees no change ([Spec-021 §Main Process Responsibilities](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#main-process-responsibilities)). | T-020p-1B-5, T-020r-5-3, T-020r-5-13, T-020r-8-5 |

## Cross-Plan Obligations

Plan-020 owns the `apps/desktop/src/{main,preload,renderer}/` **namespaces**, but several files inside them carry other plans' **content** — the per-file content exceptions this section records. The rule this section encodes: Plan-020's remainder work composes, imports, and registers that content; it never rewrites a body it does not own.

- **CP-020-1 — the remainder's registry of main's bridge handlers registers the `daemon.status` handler Plan-025 Phase 1 builds.** [Plan-025](./025-remote-control.md) Phase 1 carries the service's state to the renderer as the `daemon.status` topic on `daemon.subscribe` — the link state from the liveness check and the app-and-service version range — and refuses every renderer-issued mutating call (`daemon.stop`, `daemon.restart`) unless that topic reads connected; Plan-020's registry registers that handler on the `daemon.subscribe` channel and never rewrites its body. This closes the renderer's status path: `daemon.subscribe('daemon.status', …)` has a live backend once this registry and the non-stub preload ship. **Direction:** Consume from [Plan-025](./025-remote-control.md). **Tasks:** T-020r-2-1.

### CP-020-2 — Agent definition editor mount (reciprocal of [Plan-024](./024-agent-definitions-and-peer-invocation.md) CP-024-2)

Plan-024 authors the Sidekicks destination's library, editor and `Browse plugins` view as components in the existing agents feature, `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/features/agents/`; this plan mounts them into the renderer's layout and router at Phase 5 — the library and editor on the `sidekicks` arm over `#/sidekicks`, `#/sidekicks/<definitionId>` and `#/sidekicks/new`, and `Browse plugins` on its own `sidekicks-plugins` arm over `#/sidekicks/plugins`, the rail marking Sidekicks on all four — and owns nothing inside them. The split follows the precedent exactly — Plan-002 authored `run/controls/` and Plan-010 owned the mount — and keeps the editor's eligibility and refusal logic with the plan that owns the daemon contract it projects, rather than duplicating that logic in a host that would then be a second source of truth.

**What the library and the editor read.** The daemon's agent-definition methods: `agent.definitionList`, `agent.definitionCreate`, `agent.definitionUpdate`, `agent.definitionDelete`, `agent.definitionExport` and `agent.definitionImport`, with each definition's `workflowUsage` and `lastUsedAt` on `agent.definitionList`'s rows; the tool-allowlist picker reads `callbackTool.list`, `mcp.list` and the `builtInTools` of `driver.listCapabilities`. A method of that group the daemon has not registered yet is a build gap for its owner to close, and no client refuses on its behalf or stands in for it. The fixture implementation sits beside the definitions client and answers only its operations; the scenarios that drive it and their data live with every other fixture scenario under `apps/desktop/fixtures/`, compiled only into a fixture-capable build, and nothing fixture-only reaches `packages/contracts`.

**Resolution.** Plan-024 Phase 5 is gated on this plan's Phase 5 merged, so the mount point exists before the components that fill it. The reciprocal is Plan-024 CP-024-2. This plan gains no task: the mount is a route-table plus screen registration of the shape Phase 5 already performs for every other plan-owned subtree.

### CP-020-3 — MCP-governance settings page in the Settings page list (reciprocal of [Plan-022](./022-mcp-server-configuration-and-governance.md) CP-022-5)

This plan owns the Settings frame and its page list (T-020p-1C-4), and the page list, `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/features/settings/pages/registry.ts`, already registers the MCP servers page (the `mcp-servers` section, `McpServersPage`). Plan-022 builds the page's body in the settings feature's MCP servers page folder, `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/features/settings/pages/mcp-servers/` (its T22.5.7), and this plan owns nothing inside that folder. The split of authorship is CP-020-2's: the owner authors the components and their eligibility projection, the host provides the frame and the page list entry, and neither edits the other's code. The reason it matters more here than as tidiness is that the MCP Servers View sketch under [Spec-021 §Signature Feature Composition Sketches](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#signature-feature-composition-sketches) requires the page to derive no connection state and no effective idempotency class of its own — an eligibility projection authored in the host, away from the plan that owns the governance rules, is exactly how a second source of truth for those decisions gets written.

**Resolution.** The reciprocal is Plan-022 CP-022-5. This plan gains no task and nothing is mounted at a later phase: the page list entry exists, and Plan-022's own dispatch order is unchanged.

### CP-020-4 — Provider command-and-skill enumeration consumed from [Plan-003](./003-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md)

The composer's command list subscribes to `session.providerCommandsSubscribe` and reads the `provider_commands` and `context_compaction` declarations through the existing `driver.listCapabilities` cache; Plan-003 owns all three and this plan authors none of them. The subscription takes the session alone (`{ sessionId }`): the daemon follows the session's live provider process and sends the whole list again on each change the provider pushes and for each new process — a new session, or a switch of provider, account or worktree at a run boundary — so the composer never names an agent, a run or a binding, which no client-facing read publishes. Each emission carries the `(driverName, providerAccountId)` it was read under, and the composer replaces its list with each emission rather than merging or filtering an older one. That is [Spec-004 §The provider command and skill surface](../specs/004-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md#the-provider-command-and-skill-surface)'s routing invariant landing on the one surface that could violate it — a composer that kept one list across a binding change would offer a Claude command to a Codex agent without any daemon call being wrong. Until the first emission the list has nothing to offer, this being a live read rather than a catalog that outlives a session.

**Resolution.** Plan-003 Phase 4 registers the subscription; this plan's Phase 5 composer consumes it. No reciprocal task is owed on Plan-003 beyond the registration it already carries, so this row is one-directional by design.

### CP-020-5 — Preview's daemon-side dependencies and the page-host namespaces live in the daemon package

[ADR-034](../decisions/034-embedded-browser-for-preview.md) puts these pieces of Preview in the daemon rather than in the main process: the context resolver, the headless browser that stands in where no desktop runs, and the agent's browser tool server hosted in process. Their dependencies are therefore **`packages/runtime-daemon`'s, not `apps/desktop`'s** — `playwright-core` 1.63.0 pinned exactly for the headless fallback and for the tool server, which is that package's own `lib/coreBundle` export (the `@playwright/mcp` shim re-exports it and pins a pre-release pair, so the shim is not taken; a change to the export or the element-reference format shows as a failing test or in the daemon's logs and is investigated when seen), and `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.32.0 as a direct dependency because the in-process hosting pattern binds the connection to a transport the daemon builds. This plan's renderer half adds one dependency of its own for the mark strokes, `perfect-freehand` 1.2.3, and `electron` 44.5.1 is already pinned here. The `preview.*` and `browser.*` method roots those pieces answer on are daemon JSON-RPC roots for the same reason: there are two page hosts and one endpoint, so the verbs sit where the resolver does and nothing above them branches on which host is live.

**Resolution.** §Phase 8 — Preview And Detached Panes builds all of it as this plan's tasks. T-020r-8-1 builds the Electron page host, its debugger link and the `browser` bridge namespace in the main process; T-020r-8-2 builds the daemon's Preview in `packages/runtime-daemon` — the relay with its page-session filter, the context resolver, the headless browser, the in-process tool server, the per-session page store, and the handlers behind `preview.*` and `browser.*`; T-020r-8-3 and T-020r-8-4 build the renderer's pane and Settings › Browser. The daemon's method-namespace registry stays **[Plan-005](./005-local-ipc-and-daemon-control.md)**'s: CP-005-10 registers the daemon's `preview` and `browser` roots and nothing more; T-020r-8-2 builds everything that answers on them and adds its daemon dependencies.

### CP-020-6 — Skills destination routing arm (reciprocal of [Plan-026](./026-skills.md) CP-026-1)

Plan-026 authors the Skills destination as components in its own `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/features/skills/` subtree, which Plan-026 creates; the icon rail, `layout/NavigationRail/destinations.ts` and the routing module are this plan's files. The rail has one owner, this plan, which builds the Skills item and its place in the keyboard order with the rail's five destinations; Plan-026 adds one arm, the destination arm carrying that destination's four addresses. The split is the CP-020-2 shape unchanged: the owner authors the components behind the mount, the host provides the mount, and neither edits the other's subtree.

**Resolution.** Plan-026 Phase 4 is gated on this plan's Phase 5 merged, so the rail, its Skills item and the router exist before the arm lands, and Plan-026 adds exactly that arm and edits nothing else in this plan's files. This plan gains no task: the rail item is part of the rail this plan builds, and the destination arm is the route-table registration Phase 5 already performs for every other plan-owned destination.

### CP-020-7 — The worktree screens over Plan-007's methods (reciprocal of [Plan-007](./007-worktree-lifecycle-and-execution-modes.md) CP-007-9)

The worktree switcher and its setup card on the composer strip (T-020p-1C-5) and Settings › Runtime's worktree and kept-worktree rows (T-020r-7-5) call Plan-007's worktree methods through the preload bridge. Each move, create, removal, `Put back` and `Delete now` fires on an explicit press and fires one call; every row the status read returns is drawn with its state, `failed` included, never filtered; the read lists only standing trees, so a removed row reads `<name> removed · Put back` until the popover closes, and a kept copy is `repo.removedWorktreeList`'s, on Settings › Runtime; and every verdict — cleanliness, occupancy, risks, the name a tree will get — is the daemon's, never computed on screen. The swept sessions' flow row is a transcript row and so [Plan-010](./010-transcript-and-reasoning.md)'s.

**Resolution.** Plan-007 T3.7 to T3.10 build the methods; T-020p-1C-5 and T-020r-7-5 draw them, and neither re-derives a verdict the daemon returns.

## Implementation Steps

1. **Scaffold `apps/desktop/` workspace package.** Create `package.json` with the dependency set declared in §Target Areas. Add to the root workspace `package.json`'s `workspaces` array. Configure TypeScript strict (`"strict": true`, `"noUncheckedIndexedAccess": true`, `"exactOptionalPropertyTypes": true`).
2. **Author the bridge types.** The full `PlatformBridge` interface per Spec-021, with `PlatformBridgeProvider`, in the renderer's `services/platform/`, and the desktop's preload API type, `PreloadApi`, in `apps/desktop/src/shared/preload-api.ts`; both import the Plan-005 daemon types and the Plan-025 control-plane types from `packages/contracts`. Type-only, no runtime, except the provider. Author the conditional-type negative test (no member of the preload API type or of `PlatformBridge` includes `'token' | 'dpop' | 'secret'`) in `apps/desktop/src/shared/preload-api.test-d.ts`, beside the preload API type.
3. **Author `electron.vite.config.ts`.** `electron-vite` config with three entries (main / preload / renderer). Per-target module-system shape: main emits ESM (`build.rollupOptions.output.format: 'es'`); preload emits CJS via `build.rollupOptions.output.format: 'cjs'` + an explicit `.cjs` output extension (Electron's sandboxed preload runtime is CJS-only — an ESM preload fails to register with `SyntaxError: Cannot use import statement outside a module`, and the `.cjs` extension overrides the package-level `"type": "module"` so Node loads the file as CJS regardless of the package field); renderer emits browser-ESM. Renderer loads via custom protocol, not `file://`. Enable `build.sourcemap: 'hidden'` (sourcemap emitted but not referenced from the bundle, so they stay with the build for reading a stack trace and are not shipped to end users). The `apps/desktop` `build` script uses `electron-vite build` (not `tsc -b`); this is what enables both the per-target output shape above AND the smoke-mode `define` substitution described next. **Smoke-mode gate:** declare a compile-time-static identifier `__SMOKE_BUILD__` via Vite's `define` plugin — substituted with the literal `false` in default `electron-vite build` and with the literal `true` in `electron-vite build --mode=smoke`. Test-only probe code paths gate on this identifier in an outer `if (__SMOKE_BUILD__ && <runtime-opt-in>)`; Rollup's tree-shaker collapses the `if (false && expr)` branch in release bundles, physically eliminating the probe body from the shipped artifact (the "test machinery never embedded in production binaries" property — see [Spec-021 §Pitfalls To Avoid](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#pitfalls-to-avoid)). The inner runtime opt-in remains as defense-in-depth so the probe never auto-runs even in a smoke bundle without explicit invocation.
4. **Author the window factory** in `main/windows/` — one private `constructLockedWindow` builds every window, the hidden console window at start and every window a person sees around the hidden console document's own `window.open` child, as a `BaseWindow` hosting the renderer as a `WebContentsView` with the `webPreferences` block from [Spec-021 §Security Hardening Baseline](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#security-hardening-baseline). Add the two ESLint selectors to `apps/desktop/eslint.config.mjs` — one refusing a `BaseWindow`, `BrowserWindow` or `WebContentsView` built outside the window factory, one refusing `contextIsolation`, `sandbox`, `webSecurity`, `nodeIntegration` or `nodeIntegrationInWorker` written as anything but its hardened literal — and write `factory.test.ts`, which checks the factory builds every window through the locked block.
5. **Author the preload bridge.** `apps/desktop/src/preload/index.ts` — single `contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld('desktopBridge', bridge)` call. Each bridge method delegates to `ipcRenderer.invoke('<channel>', ...)`; no direct Node API use. The bridge object must be structurally typed as the desktop's preload API type from step 2.
6. **Wire the IPC handlers.** `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/install-handlers.ts` + the per-namespace bridge handlers §Target Areas Main process lists (the daemon, control-plane, native and update handlers, and the `window`, machine-settings, keyboard-map, app and `browser` handlers). One `ipcMain.handle('<channel>', handler)` per method. Each handler validates input against the Spec-021 contract; auth material is attached server-side; responses are sanitized before return.
7. **Author the auto-update state machine.** `apps/desktop/src/main/services/updater.ts`. `autoUpdater` (from `electron-updater`) with its stock GitHub provider on a 4-hour cadence + startup + explicit check, with `autoDownload` and `autoInstallOnAppQuit` off and `allowDowngrade` off. At check time main refuses a release whose version is not above the last one seen in `updater-state.json`; the cycle is skipped and logged. A download starts only on the person's press, and `electron-updater` checks its SHA-512 against the release's update file (and on Windows the installer's publisher) before `update-downloaded`; a mismatch discards the artifact, logs and refuses. `on('update-downloaded')` handler: stage artifact at `apps/desktop/<staging-dir>`; `Restart to finish` applies it through `app.relaunch() + app.quit()`. After relaunch main handshakes with the service through `daemon.hello` as at every start; a handshake that completes with the service outside the accepted version range makes the console read-only and names the side that is behind. The app does not roll itself back; a bad release is fixed by the next one.
8. **Author the crash reporter** in the main process — called **first** in main's entry point before any other module loads. Starts Crashpad with `crashReporter.start({ uploadToServer: false })` while `Keep crash reports` is on, reading that one value straight from the machine settings file because the service has not answered yet. Crashpad covers the main process and every renderer; nothing is uploaded and no crash SDK is initialized anywhere. The service turns each dump into the stripped, symbolicated report it keeps and serves through `daemon.crashList`.
9. **Author the deep-link handler.** `apps/desktop/src/main/services/deep-link.ts`. `app.setAsDefaultProtocolClient('sidekicks')` at startup. `open-url` (macOS) and `second-instance` (Windows / Linux) handlers parse the URL in the main process with the one `sidekicks://` parser `packages/contracts` exports and hand the renderer the parsed request — `session/<id>` or `workflow-run/<runId>` — through `window.subscribeToNavigationRequest`; a malformed URL is refused at the parser boundary and nothing is routed.
10. **Author the renderer's layout and composer.** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/app/App.tsx` (top-level router + layout) plus `layout/` (the layout chrome) and `features/composer/` (the composer). This step authors **no feature-view body**: each Signature Feature view is created by its owning plan in that plan's own `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/` subtree, and this step only mounts it as a route and holds the composition points.
11. **Author the build pipeline.** `electron-builder.yml`'s `electronFuses` setting and its ASAR integrity embed, + `code-sign.ts`. `electron-builder` packages, flips the fuses and embeds the digest, and then signs (per-platform); fuse-flipping and digest-embedding precede signing because both invalidate any prior signature.
12. **Author the release manifest**, a desktop release script — writes the release manifest (a monotonic `version`, `released_at` and each artifact's SHA-256) and publishes it to the GitHub Release beside `electron-builder`'s update files.
13. **Author the renderer ESLint config.** `apps/desktop/eslint.config.mjs` — `no-restricted-imports` rule banning `electron` / `node:*` / `fs` / `child_process` / `net` / `os` / `path` / `process` / `./src/main/**` / `./src/preload/**` from `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/**`. CI runs this lint; any renderer-side Node import fails the build.
14. **Author the Playwright `_electron` E2E suite.** `apps/desktop/tests/e2e/*.test.ts` per §Target Areas Test section. The `_electron` namespace is marked experimental in the [Playwright class-electron docs](https://playwright.dev/docs/api/class-electron), but it is the only production-viable harness (Spectron is archived). Tests must tolerate minor API churn between Playwright releases.
15. **Author the CI gate scripts.** The desktop's gate is `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, run on every pull request to `develop` or `main`. Its `test` job runs the full-repo lint and format check, the build, the typecheck and each package's lint. Its `desktop` job, which runs when `apps/desktop/**`, `packages/**`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `turbo.json` or the workflow itself changed, runs the package's `test` chain ([§Testing posture](#testing-posture)) one group per matrix leg, plus `structure:layering`, and, once T-020p-1C-8 lands them, the accessibility and endurance tiers; its `desktop-slow` job, under the same check names and path filter, runs the E2E suite of step 14 on the nightly run, on pull requests into `main`, and on a manual run; the `ci-gate` job aggregates every job and is the one check `main` requires. A per-platform release job signs + notarizes in `.github/workflows/release.yml`, the repository's one release workflow, which gains a trigger on tag pushes beside its `workflow_dispatch`.
16. **Document release runbook.** `apps/desktop/RELEASE.md` — per-platform signing prerequisites (the self-signed code-signing identity on macOS until the Developer ID certificate exists; the SignPath Foundation project on Windows; no key on Linux: Cloudsmith's per-repository key signs each RPM on upload and the apt and dnf metadata) and the notarization retry procedure.
17. **Author Preview and a side pane's own window.** The Preview page host in the main process, the daemon's Preview in `packages/runtime-daemon`, the renderer's Preview pane and Settings › Browser, and a side pane in a window of its own ([§Phase 8 — Preview And Detached Panes](#phase-8--preview-and-detached-panes)).

## Parallelization Notes

- Step 1 (scaffold) + step 2 (bridge type) must land first; everything else orbits them.
- Steps 3 (vite config) + 4 (window factory) + 5 (preload) can run in parallel; they have no cross-file dependencies.
- Step 6 (IPC handlers) follows steps 1–5.
- Step 7 (auto-update) depends on [Plan-025](./025-remote-control.md) Phase 1's connection to the service, which carries the service's own update and restore that main relays.
- Step 8 (crash reporter) has no dependency and should be authored early so it runs first at startup.
- Step 10 (renderer layout and composer) depends on step 2 (bridge type) — can start in parallel with steps 6–9 since it consumes the bridge-shape, not the backing implementations. Its router leg additionally depends on the Plan-005 / Plan-025 request channel in §Preconditions.
- Step 11 (build pipeline) depends on everything compiling; orchestrates tail-end of release flow.
- Steps 13 (eslint) + 14 (E2E) + 15 (CI) + 16 (runbook) are independent tail-end tasks.
- Step 17 (Preview and a side pane's own window) depends on step 4's locked constructor and main's registry of windows (T-020p-1B-5), on the renderer's view registry (T-020r-5-3), and on the service's socket [Plan-025](./025-remote-control.md) Phase 1's watch reaches; it follows step 14, whose E2E suite and two-client harness it is tested on.

## Partial PR Sequence

The partial slice lands as **1 small PR** following the control-plane bootstrap precedent (one cohesive substrate slice). The carve-out exists so later desktop work lands on an existing `apps/desktop/` tree; Plan-001's sidecar drain (CP-001-1) runs in the daemon's stop sequence and its tests sit in the daemon package, so neither waits on it, per the substrate-vs-namespace carve-out in [§Delivery Split](#delivery-split). The remainder retains the full Implementation Steps 1–17 surface that depends on Plan-005 / Plan-015 / Plan-025 / Plan-021.

### Phase 1: Workspace Package Substrate (apps/desktop/ tree + minimal entrypoints)

**Goal:** `apps/desktop/` workspace package compiles; minimal Electron app launches end-to-end. Directory tree (`apps/desktop/src/main/`, `apps/desktop/src/preload/`, `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/`) exists; `electron.vite.config.ts` builds all three targets; preload exposes a stub `window.desktopBridge` typed against the desktop's preload API type, `PreloadApi`, in `apps/desktop/src/shared/preload-api.ts`; renderer mounts a placeholder React tree. No start of the service, no auto-update, no crash reporter, no deep-link — those stay in the remainder.

**Spec-021 AC coverage.** Phase 1 ships no remainder _behavior_ — no start of the service, auto-update, crash reporting, or deep-link — and in exactly that behavioral sense the substrate is pre-behavior plumbing. It does, however, land three durable enforcement surfaces that partially close [Spec-021 §Acceptance Criteria](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#acceptance-criteria) bullets: the locked `webPreferences` block's checks ([Spec-021 §Security Hardening Baseline](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#security-hardening-baseline), the two ESLint selectors and `factory.test.ts`), the conditional-type negative contract test for the no-auth-material bullet (`apps/desktop/src/shared/preload-api.test-d.ts`, beside the preload API type), and the launch smoke test's renderer-isolation assertions (`window.require` / `process` / `global` all `undefined`). The remaining [Spec-021 §Acceptance Criteria](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#acceptance-criteria) bullets (fuses, signing, auto-update, deep-link token containment) all require the remainder surfaces.

**Precondition:** None at the plan level. Partial entry point. The placeholder entry file Plan-001 Phase 1 shipped is repositioned during this PR per its forward-declaration comment ("split into `apps/desktop/src/{main,preload,renderer}/` per the electron-vite zero-config convention").

- `apps/desktop/package.json` — minimal dependency set: `electron` (44.x, exact-pinned by T-020p-1B-4), `electron-vite@6.0.0-beta.5` (exact), `react@^19`, `react-dom@^19`, `@ai-sidekicks/contracts` (workspace), `@ai-sidekicks/client-sdk` (workspace). Heavy deps (`electron-builder`, `electron-updater`, `@electron/fuses`, `@playwright/test`) land with the remainder.
- `apps/desktop/electron.vite.config.ts` — `electron-vite` config with three targets (`main` / `preload` / `renderer`). Per-target module shape: main emits ESM; preload emits CJS via an explicit `.cjs` output extension (Electron's sandboxed preload runtime is CJS-only); renderer emits browser-ESM. The `apps/desktop` `build` script uses `electron-vite build` (not `tsc -b`) to support both the per-target output shape and the `__SMOKE_BUILD__` compile-time `define` substitution (Implementation Step 3) that gates test-only probe code paths so Rollup tree-shakes them out of release bundles. Strict TypeScript; HMR on dev. (Implementation Step 3 substrate.)
- `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts` — minimal main entrypoint: `app.requestSingleInstanceLock()` + `app.whenReady()` → `openRendererWindow()`. No crash reporter, no protocol handler, no start of the service, no deep-link handler in the partial. (Implementation Step 1 substrate fragment.)
- `apps/desktop/src/main/windows/factory.ts` — `openRendererWindow()` factory returning a `BaseWindow` hosting the renderer as a `WebContentsView` with the `webPreferences` block from [Spec-021 §Security Hardening Baseline](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#security-hardening-baseline) (`contextIsolation: true`, `sandbox: true`, `nodeIntegration: false`, `webSecurity: true`, preload script, custom-protocol load — not `file://`). The two ESLint selectors and `factory.test.ts` ship in the partial to lock in the security posture from Step 1. (Implementation Step 4 substrate.)
- `apps/desktop/src/preload/index.ts` — single `contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld('desktopBridge', bridge)` call. The `bridge` object is typed by the desktop's preload API type, `PreloadApi`, in `apps/desktop/src/shared/preload-api.ts`; each method (`daemon.call`, `controlPlane.call`, etc.) is carried over IPC, and one whose main handler is not built yet rejects with Electron's own no-handler error. (Implementation Step 5 substrate fragment.)
- `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/main.tsx` — minimal React entrypoint: `createRoot(document.getElementById('root')!).render(<App />)`. (Implementation Step 10 substrate fragment.)
- `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/app/App.tsx` — placeholder `<App />` returning a minimal tree (e.g. a single `<div>AI Sidekicks Desktop (partial substrate)</div>`). The remainder's router, the `layout/` chrome and `features/composer/`, and the mounting of each owning plan's feature view defer to the remainder. (Implementation Step 10 substrate fragment.)
- `apps/desktop/src/shared/preload-api.ts` — `PreloadApi`, the desktop's preload API type, importing the Plan-005 `DaemonMethod` / `DaemonParams` / `DaemonResult` / `DaemonEvent` / `DaemonEventPayload` typed unions from `packages/contracts` and declaring the bridge surface method signatures (only the type contract), and the conditional-type negative test beside it (`apps/desktop/src/shared/preload-api.test-d.ts`), which locks in the no-auth-material invariant from day one; `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/services/platform/` — the `PlatformBridge` interface and `PlatformBridgeProvider`. (Implementation Step 2 substrate.)
- `apps/desktop/eslint.config.mjs` — flat-config `no-restricted-imports` rule banning `electron` / `node:*` / `fs` / `child_process` / `net` / `os` / `path` / `process` / `./src/main/**` / `./src/preload/**` from `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/**`. CI runs this lint. (Implementation Step 13.)

#### Tasks

- **T-020p-1-1** (Files: `apps/desktop/package.json` (REPLACE the Plan-001 Phase 1 placeholder scaffold) + root `package.json` workspaces no-op; Verifies invariant: none — substrate scaffold) — Replace the Plan-001 Phase 1 placeholder `apps/desktop/package.json` with the minimal dependency set above; remove the placeholder entry file. Pin `electron-vite` at `6.0.0-beta.5` exactly, per §Risks And Blockers. The Plan-001 Phase 1 placeholder forward-declaration ("split into `apps/desktop/src/{main,preload,renderer}/`") is satisfied by this task.
- **T-020p-1-2** (Files: `apps/desktop/electron.vite.config.ts` (CREATE) + `apps/desktop/tsconfig.json` (REPLACE existing scaffold — main, preload and shared, everything under `src/` but the renderer) + `apps/desktop/src/renderer/tsconfig.json` (CREATE); Verifies invariant: I-020-4 (the define collapses the guard in the release build)) — `electron-vite` configuration with three build targets and the `__SMOKE_BUILD__` `define` of Implementation Step 3; one `tsconfig.json` for the two Node targets and one for the renderer, each extending the root TS strict + `isolatedDeclarations: true` posture. Build the package: `pnpm --filter @ai-sidekicks/desktop build` succeeds.
- **T-020p-1-3** (Files: `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts` (CREATE) + `apps/desktop/src/main/windows/factory.ts` (CREATE) + `factory.test.ts` (CREATE) + `apps/desktop/eslint.config.mjs` (EXTEND — the two window selectors); Verifies invariant: Spec-021 §Security Hardening Baseline `webPreferences` lock-in) — Minimal main entrypoint + `openRendererWindow()` factory with the locked `webPreferences` block. The two ESLint selectors refuse a window built outside the factory and a hardened key written as anything but its hardened literal, and `factory.test.ts` checks the factory builds through the locked block. **No start of the service / no crash reporter / no protocol-handler in the partial** — those wire in at the remainder against the same `index.ts`.
- **T-020p-1-4** (Files: `apps/desktop/src/preload/index.ts` (CREATE) + the desktop's preload API type, `PreloadApi`, in `apps/desktop/src/shared/preload-api.ts` and its conditional-type test `apps/desktop/src/shared/preload-api.test-d.ts` (CREATE) + the `PlatformBridge` interface and `PlatformBridgeProvider` in `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/services/platform/` (CREATE); Verifies invariant: Spec-021 §Acceptance Criteria "No auth material on `window.desktopBridge`" — typed enforcement) — the preload API type importing the Plan-005 daemon types from `packages/contracts`; bridge methods declared (no implementations); single `contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld` call in preload. Conditional-type test asserts the preload API type refuses any property name matching `/token|dpop|secret/i`. A method whose main handler is not built yet rejects with Electron's own no-handler error.
- **T-020p-1-5** (Files: `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/main.tsx` (CREATE) + `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/app/App.tsx` (CREATE) + `apps/desktop/src/renderer/index.html` (CREATE); Verifies invariant: none — substrate scaffold) — Minimal React + Vite renderer mount. Renderer must not import any Node API (enforced by T-020p-1-6 ESLint rule).
- **T-020p-1-6** (Files: `apps/desktop/eslint.config.mjs` (CREATE); Verifies invariant: Spec-021 §Trust Stance — renderer-untrusted enforcement at the import boundary) — Flat-config ESLint with `no-restricted-imports` banning `electron` / `node:*` / `fs` / `child_process` / `net` / `os` / `path` / `process` / `./src/main/**` / `./src/preload/**` from `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/**`. CI gate.
- **T-020p-1-7** (Files: `apps/desktop/tests/launch.smoke.test.ts` (CREATE — Vitest, not Playwright, in the partial; Playwright `_electron` E2E suite ships in the remainder per Implementation Step 14); Verifies invariant: substrate boots) — Vitest smoke test that programmatically spawns `electron .` against the built bundle, asserts the main window appears within 5 s, asserts `window.desktopBridge` is defined as an object, asserts `window.require` is `undefined`. This is the partial's unblock proof.

Plan-020's remainder picks up Implementation Steps 6–12 (step 10 reduced to the router + layout subtree per §Renderer) + 14–17 against the substrate established here, decomposed into Phases 2–8 by [§Remainder PR Sequence](#remainder-pr-sequence).

The renderer-load legs — the privileged scheme, the bundle handler, the window's `loadURL`, the one locked window constructor, and the unit project their tests run in — sit in the partial supplement Phase 1B below rather than in Phase 3, because none of them consumes a daemon contract and the Phase 1C console cannot run in the app without them.

### Phase 1B — Renderer Load Substrate

**Goal:** Every Phase 1C console PR needs a renderer that actually runs in the app, with a real origin, and nothing in that need touches the daemon. This phase ships the renderer-load substrate in the partial: the scheme registered privileged before ready (I-020-6), the `sidekicks-renderer://` handler serving the built bundle with a root-containment guard and the [Spec-021 §Security Hardening Baseline](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#security-hardening-baseline) CSP on every response, the hidden console window's load through the one locked constructor (I-020-7), the platform's own application menu, the `main-unit` Vitest project that makes their units execute, and the Electron-44 / `better-sqlite3`-13.0.3 pin move ADR-015 and ADR-021 decided. Phase 3's T-020r-3-3 then **composes** these legs into the full startup order and adds nothing to them.

**Precondition:** Phase 1 merged. No upstream plan gate — no leg consumes a daemon, control-plane or update contract, and every file this phase touches is Plan-020-owned with one named exception class: T-020p-1B-4 also edits `packages/runtime-daemon/package.json` for the `better-sqlite3` move [ADR-021 §Decision](../decisions/021-v1-toolchain-selection.md#decision) mandates alongside the Electron pin — a dependency bump no plan owns, recorded here rather than routed to a second PR because ADR-021 forbids the Electron pin moving first — and, because that binding's Node-API-10 prebuild needs Node 22.14.0 or later and the packages' floor is `>=24.21.0`, the `engines.node` floor line in every package manifest, the CI Node pins, and the contributor-facing floor sentences the task enumerates: toolchain configuration no plan owns, moved in one commit with the binding so no interval exists in which the pinned binding and the declared floor disagree.

- `apps/desktop/src/main/services/renderer/protocol.ts` — the registration and the handler, exported so the startup order is asserted rather than assumed: `RendererSchemeRegistration`'s `register()` wraps `protocol.registerSchemesAsPrivileged([{ scheme: 'sidekicks-renderer', privileges: { standard: true, secure: true, supportFetchAPI: true } }])` and throws on a second call, because Electron accepts exactly one registration per process and a silent second call would hide a startup-order regression; `installRendererProtocol(rendererRoot, rootStamp)` wraps `protocol.handle('sidekicks-renderer', …)` and is called inside `whenReady()` **before** any window is constructed. The handler resolves `sidekicks-renderer://app/<path>` under the built `out/renderer` tree through one pure function, `resolveRendererAsset(rendererRoot, url)`, that decodes and normalizes the pathname, refuses any resolution that escapes the root (`..`, percent-encoded separators, backslashes, absolute paths, and symlinks — checked through `fs.realpath` against the root's own realpath), refuses any host other than `app`, and answers a miss with 404 and an escape with 403 — both with an empty body and no path echoed, so a probe learns nothing about the tree. There is no `index.html` fallback: the console routes by hash, so every navigable URL is `index.html` plus a fragment and a fallback would only turn typos into a served page. One reserved path, `/-/load-failure`, is answered from a document GENERATED in the main process, reading `The app's window could not load.` over the (escaped, bounded) failure reason and `Quit the app and open it again. If it still does not load, reinstall the app.` under it — never read from the tree, because the failure it reports is that the tree could not be loaded and a fallback living there is missing exactly when it is needed; it is matched on the exact decoded path so nothing under it falls through, touches no filesystem call so containment is trivially true rather than checked, and carries no script, link, or retry control. Bodies stream through `net.fetch(pathToFileURL(resolved))` — the pattern Electron's own `protocol.handle` docs give — so nothing is buffered in main-process memory; the `Content-Type` comes from a closed extension map (`html`, `js`, `mjs`, `css`, `json`, `svg`, `png`, `webp`, `woff2`, `wasm`) with `application/octet-stream` for anything else — except `.map`, which answers 404 unconditionally: the build emits hidden source maps that are not shipped, so the handler refuses them even while they sit beside the bundle in a dev tree, and Phase 4's builder `files` filter excludes `out/**/*.map` from the package; and every response carries the `Content-Security-Policy` header [Spec-021 §Security Hardening Baseline](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#security-hardening-baseline) sets, which is the CSP's **only** carrier — the shipped `index.html` deliberately has no meta tag.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/windows/factory.ts` — The hidden console window gains its load. One private `constructLockedWindow(options)` owns the `webPreferences` literal, and nothing outside the window factory's module calls it, so `factory.test.ts` covers every window through a single block and the ESLint selector refuses a window built anywhere else. The hidden console window loads `sidekicks-renderer://app/index.html`; under `electron-vite dev` (`!app.isPackaged && process.env.ELECTRON_RENDERER_URL`) it loads the dev-server URL so HMR works, and that branch is the only place the variable is read. The dev origin differs from the packaged scheme origin, so IndexedDB partitions differ between `electron-vite dev` and the built bundle — accepted and stated, because the console's persistence layer keys nothing on origin and every console test tier runs the built bundle. Every window a person sees — the first, a new window, a session's own window, a side pane's own window — is built later by main's window-open handler around the hidden console document's own `window.open` child (T-020p-1B-5 and T-020r-8-5), with no preload and no bridge of its own, so one renderer drives every window. Every locked window also carries the **navigation policy** (`apps/desktop/src/main/windows/navigation.ts`) installed by the same private function as the locked block: in-window navigation is confined to the renderer scheme's own scheme-and-host pair (plus the dev origin under the same two-condition dev branch), every popup is denied, including a same-origin one, except the renderer's own `window.open` that main's window-open handler answers once T-020p-1B-5 lands, and an `http(s)` target is handed to the OS browser through a single main-owned `shell.openExternal` call behind a closed scheme allowlist while every other scheme is refused with no side effect — the locked `webPreferences` block governs what the renderer may DO and said nothing about where it may GO, and a top-level navigation to a remote origin would put attacker-served content behind the same preload and the same partition. A **rejected `loadURL`** leaves no live blank window: the window loads the generated load-failure document instead (see the `renderer/protocol.ts` bullet), and when even that cannot be served the window is destroyed — the app exiting non-zero with the diagnostic when it was the hidden console window, rather than sitting there as an invisible placeholder a harness can only detect by timing out. A gone renderer (`render-process-gone`) is logged with its reason and exit code and handled on a later task per [Desktop App Implementation Notes §Repeated renderer crashes](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#repeated-renderer-crashes).
- The application menu, at the root of `main/` — one static template built once with the platform's own roles, Electron's `{ role: "windowMenu" }` among them, whose platform verbs list every open window by its title. No module registers a section into it and it opens no route of its own: every chord the console answers is a row on the Keyboard page of [Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian)](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#console-design-meridian), and none is a menu accelerator minted here. The rest of the template — the Edit roles, the File menu's `New window` with the live `New window` binding as its accelerator, the View menu's color-scheme choice and the macOS menu-bar icon — lands with the window registry at T-020p-1B-5, and the macOS app menu and About on every platform at T-020p-1B-5 EXTEND.
- `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts` — imports `protocol.ts` first and registers the scheme (`new RendererSchemeRegistration().register()`) at module top level, ahead of every `whenReady()` consumer, then keys the profile to the install, opens main's log and starts the crash reporter (T-020r-3-1), in that order, ahead of everything else; inside `whenReady()` the order is `installRendererProtocol` → `installApplicationMenu` → the first window. The smoke probe loads no `about:blank`: it waits for the real bundle's `did-finish-load`, and the `__SMOKE_BUILD__` define gates only the probe's assertions, so the smoke test proves the bundle is served, not merely that a window exists.
- `apps/desktop/vitest.config.ts` + `apps/desktop/turbo.json` + `apps/desktop/package.json` — the `main-unit` Vitest project, the one §Testing posture names for the units under `src/main/**` and `build/**`.
- `apps/desktop/package.json` + `packages/runtime-daemon/package.json` + `pnpm-lock.yaml` — the pin move: `electron` to the 44.x line ADR-015 fixes, `better-sqlite3` to **13.0.3** exact per ADR-021 (the Electron pin may not move first, so both move in this one task), with the Electron-41 stamps swept from the shipped partial comments, the `package.json` description, and `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s sandbox comment, ADR-023's `lifecycle.gc.test.ts` re-run under 44 (its own tripwire), the [Console Sources §Console Design Sources](../architecture/console-sources.md#console-design-sources) platform-feature probe re-run on the 44 binary, and the [Spec-013 §Driver Pin](../specs/013-persistence-and-recovery.md#driver-pin) change riding with the re-verification its upgrade policy demands.

#### Tasks

- **T-020p-1B-1 — Renderer scheme registration and bundle handler.** `RendererSchemeRegistration`'s `register()` at module top level, `installRendererProtocol(rendererRoot, rootStamp)` inside ready before any window; `resolveRendererAsset` as a pure function with the containment guard, the closed content-type map, and the fixed CSP header; streaming bodies through `net.fetch`; 403 / 404 with empty bodies. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/services/renderer/protocol.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/services/renderer/assets.ts` (CREATE — `resolveRendererAsset`), `apps/desktop/src/main/services/renderer/assets.test.ts` (CREATE — `main-unit`: the traversal arms `..`, `%2e%2e`, `%2F`, `%5C`, an absolute path, and a symlink planted outside a temp root all answer `forbidden` with no path in the result; every mapped extension yields its type and an unmapped one yields `application/octet-stream`), `apps/desktop/src/main/services/renderer/protocol.test.ts` (CREATE — `main-unit`: a `.map` planted beside the bundle answers 404 with an empty body; a second `register()` on one `RendererSchemeRegistration` throws), `apps/desktop/src/main/index.test.ts` (CREATE — `main-unit`: importing `main/index.ts` under a mocked `electron` records `registerSchemesAsPrivileged` before the first `whenReady()` and `protocol.handle` before the first window construction; reordering either fails the test), `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts` (EXTEND) **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Security Hardening Baseline (the bundle is served over a custom protocol, never `file://`; the CSP header on every renderer document); Desktop App Implementation Notes §Renderer Bundle (the before-ready privileged registration) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-2 (the load is over the custom protocol), I-020-6 (registration precedes ready and carries `standard: true` / `secure: true`)
- **T-020p-1B-2 — Window load, the locked constructor, and the application menu.** `constructLockedWindow` as the single owner of the `webPreferences` literal, called from nowhere outside the window factory's module; the hidden console window loading the scheme URL (dev-server URL only under the dev branch); `render-process-gone` logged with its reason and exit code and acted on a later task, every window's place kept as it stands, the hidden console window built again to reload the renderer and the windows that lost their documents closed without pruning a place, so the reloaded renderer reopens every window from its kept layout at its kept place, and on the third crash in a row the reload is a safe start instead, the count clearing after five minutes with no crash ([Desktop App Implementation Notes §Repeated renderer crashes](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#repeated-renderer-crashes)); the application menu as one static template with the platform's own roles and `{ role: "windowMenu" }`, and no section registry; the navigation policy (`will-navigate` confinement, popup denial except the renderer's own `window.open` that main's window-open handler answers, main-owned allowlisted `shell.openExternal`) installed by the same private function that owns the locked block; a rejected `loadURL` serving the generated failure document and, when even that fails, destroying the window and exiting non-zero when it was the hidden console window; the smoke probe moved onto the real bundle. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/windows/factory.ts` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/src/main/menu.ts` (CREATE), the desktop's `shared/` folder (CREATE — code both processes import, which `apps/desktop/eslint.config.mjs` (EXTEND) holds to the renderer's own import ban because every byte of it is bundled into the renderer, and which `apps/desktop/src/renderer/tsconfig.json` (EXTEND) lists and widens `rootDir` for — the package-root config already covers the Node-context side, so the orphan `apps/desktop/src/main/tsconfig.json` + `apps/desktop/src/preload/tsconfig.json` (DELETE), which no script and no `references` entry ever reached, are removed rather than widened), `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/lib/wire/errors.ts` (CREATE — `readWireErrorEnvelope` / `readWireErrorEnvelopeWithCode` / `lossyStringify`, with `normalizeWireRejection` beside it in `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/lib/wire/rejection.ts`, the one wire-error normalizer every renderer caller imports, which answers a refusal for every rejection and never throws, and takes an optional caller-written fallback for a rejection that carries no code of its own), `apps/desktop/src/main/windows/navigation.ts` (CREATE — the pure navigation classifier and the single `shell.openExternal` call site), `apps/desktop/src/main/windows/navigation.test.ts` (CREATE — `main-unit`: the renderer scheme and its hash routes are in-window; a DIFFERENT non-special scheme is refused even though `URL.origin` is `"null"` for both, which is the false-PASS a `.origin` comparison would take; `file:` / `javascript:` / `data:` / `blob:` / an OS-handler scheme are refused; a credentialed authority is refused; and `openExternalUrl` re-checks the scheme itself, so a direct call with a refused target opens nothing), `apps/desktop/src/main/services/renderer/protocol.ts` (EXTEND — serving the reserved `/-/load-failure` document, which `apps/desktop/src/main/windows/load-failure/document.ts` generates) + `apps/desktop/src/main/services/renderer/protocol.test.ts` (EXTEND — it is served with the locked headers and is matched on the exact path so a prefix falls through to a 403) + `apps/desktop/src/main/windows/load-failure/document.test.ts` (it escapes a markup reason and bounds a long one), `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts` (EXTEND — menu install, the two probe gates, and the persistence comment corrected to [Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian)](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#console-design-meridian) §Persistence on the renderer scheme: layout / scroll / selection / pins / expansion sets persist and drafts never do) + `apps/desktop/src/main/probes/smoke.ts` + `apps/desktop/src/main/probes/gc.ts` (CREATE — the two probe bodies lifted out of the entrypoint so it reads as the startup ORDER it is asserted for; the ADR-023 probe's `window-all-closed` flag becomes a private field of a `GcProbe` class and its deferral is authored beside it, so the scheduled callback closes over the probe alone and cannot root the window it measures; release elimination is strengthened rather than preserved — with nothing referencing either module and no top-level side effect in either, Rollup drops both whole instead of only the gated branch), the application menu's test (CREATE — `main-unit`, the shared `electron` mock: the template carries the platform's own roles with `{ role: "windowMenu" }`, and no module can add a section to it), the window factory's test (CREATE — `main-unit`, mocked `electron`: the hidden console window loads `sidekicks-renderer://app/index.html`; the dev URL is loaded only when `isPackaged` is false **and** the variable is set, and never when either condition fails; the pre-load hook the constructor takes runs — with the constructed window — strictly ahead of `loadURL` in one ordered operation log, so a load-lifecycle listener is on time by construction rather than because Electron emits on a later tick, a hook that throws destroying the window instead of leaving a live blank one;
