the suites that STATICALLY import their module under test keep a local stub because a top-level mock instance would still be in its temporal dead zone when the hoisted factory ran, and neither constructs a window), `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/main.tsx` (EXTEND — the `#root` non-null assertion becomes a named `MissingRootElementError` naming both the id and the document that was supposed to declare it, because React's own "Target container is not a DOM element" names neither in a window whose only other symptom is a white rectangle), `apps/desktop/tsconfig.build.json` + `apps/desktop/turbo.json` + `apps/desktop/package.json` (EXTEND — the `scripts/` directory joins the typecheck and lint inputs, `tests/helpers/**` joins the `main-unit` inputs, and the package `lint` script widens to `build scripts src tests`) **Tests (the crash steps):** a renderer that crashes on every load is reloaded twice from the kept layout and the third time as a safe start, with the window-place file and the kept layout unchanged; no reload starts inside the `render-process-gone` handler; five minutes without a crash clears the count. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Main Process Responsibilities (window management — the one locked constructor, the platform menu); Desktop App Implementation Notes §Repeated renderer crashes; Spec-021 §Security Hardening Baseline (every window carries the locked block) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-2 (every window this phase constructs carries the locked block and loads over the custom protocol), I-020-7 (the one private constructor is the only place a window is built)
- **T-020p-1B-2 EXTEND — developer tools only in a development build.** `constructLockedWindow` sets `devTools` from the build: on in a development build and off in every other, so a shipped window has no developer tools to open by chord, by menu or through `webContents.openDevTools()`, the same discipline Preview follows in refusing one, and the application menu carries no developer-tools row outside a development build. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/windows/factory.ts` and its test (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/src/main/menu.ts` (EXTEND) **Tests:** a window built outside a development build reports its developer tools unavailable, and `openDevTools()` on it opens nothing. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Rules every console surface obeys (nothing forces a state by hand: the developer tools only in a development build) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020p-1B-3 — `main-unit` Vitest project registration.** The `main-unit` Vitest project — named `main-unit` because `main` is the smoke project — node environment, `include: ["src/main/**/*.test.ts", "src/preload/**/*.test.ts", "src/shared/**/*.test.ts", "build/**/*.test.ts", "scripts/**/*.test.ts", "tests/helpers/**/*.test.ts"]`, excluding the renderer's tests outside the source tree (`RENDERER_TESTS_OUTSIDE_SOURCE` in `vitest/tier-projects.ts`), disjoint from every other project's globs so the posture's no-double-discovery property holds; the paired `"test:main-unit": "vitest run --project=main-unit"` script and Turbo task with `dependsOn: ["^build"]` and **not** `build:smoke` (plain-TypeScript units need no `electron-vite` bundle, and hanging them off the smoke build would re-impose the ~25-30 s cost the posture exists to avoid), `inputs` of `src/**`, `fixtures/**`, `build/**`, `scripts/**`, `tests/budget/document.json`, `tests/helpers/**`, `vitest.config.*`, `vitest/**`, the root `vitest.shared.ts`, `tsconfig*.json` and `package.json`, covering every tree the `include` discovers tests in and every file those tests read (the fixture-launch unit reads the scenario catalog in `fixtures/`, whose scenarios import renderer modules, and the budget scripts' tests read `tests/budget/document.json`), and `cache: false` like every test task; the package-level `test` script runs `test:main-unit` in its first group, with `test:renderer` (the chain is in [§Testing posture](#testing-posture)). Lands in the same PR as T-020p-1B-1, which authors the first `src/main/**` unit. **Files:** `apps/desktop/vitest.config.ts` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/turbo.json` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/package.json` (EXTEND) **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Main Process Responsibilities (the main-process behavior these units are the only automated check on); Spec-021 §Acceptance Criteria (the criteria whose per-module verification is unit-level rather than E2E) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-1, I-020-6, I-020-7 (load-bearingly — this registration is what makes every `src/main/**` assertion for those invariants execute at all; without it each unit is inert and the invariants are asserted only by the far slower E2E suite)
- **T-020p-1B-4 — Electron 44 and `better-sqlite3` 13.0.3 pin move.** `apps/desktop/package.json` moves `electron` to the 44.x line (`44.5.1` **exact**, saved with `--save-exact` — the plan's own exact-pin rule, so a lockfile refresh cannot move the runtime on its own; a later 44.x release is a one-line bump once the workspace's `minimumReleaseAge: 1440` admits it, and a `minimumReleaseAgeExclude` entry for `electron` stays rejected as a permanent, unconditional weakening of the guard for exactly the dependency class it exists for ([ADR-021 §Decision](../decisions/021-v1-toolchain-selection.md#decision)); the `electron-builder` / `electron-updater` set lands with Phases 4 and 6) and `packages/runtime-daemon/package.json` moves `better-sqlite3` to `13.0.3` exact in the same commit, because [ADR-021 §Decision](../decisions/021-v1-toolchain-selection.md#decision) forbids the Electron pin moving ahead of the binding whose 12.x line has no ABI-149 prebuild, and **the Node floor is `>=24.21.0`, above the 22.14.0 the binding needs** — the 13.x prebuilds are Node-API 10 (declared `NAPI_VERSION=10` in the package's own `binding.gyp`), and a Node-API-9 host (22.12, 22.13) was **observed** to segfault with no diagnostic at the first `new Database()` — Node's _documented_ refusal for a too-new Node-API module is a load-time throw from `ThrowNodeApiVersionError`, so both grades are stated in [Spec-013 §Driver Pin](../specs/013-persistence-and-recovery.md#driver-pin) and the floor turns on neither — across every package `engines.node` (`packages/contracts`, `packages/runtime-daemon`, `packages/client-sdk`, `packages/crypto-paseto`, `apps/desktop`), the Node pins in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `.github/workflows/docs-corpus.yml`, and the floor sentences in `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, ADR-023's ADR-021 reference line, and Plan-024's CI-pinned-tool-versions note (the `Node 22.12` code comments that record where a behavior was measured are provenance and stay); `pnpm install` must resolve the daemon's binding from a bundled Node-API prebuild with no source compile, asserted by a CI provenance check with two conjuncts — the binding the daemon resolves (the path `lib/binding.js` selects) lives under the tarball's `prebuilds/<platform>-<arch>.node`, and the package's `build/Release/` holds no `.node` — sound because the 13.x line carries no `prebuild-install` (the 12.x line downloaded its prebuild into `build/Release/`, which is why an absence check alone was ambiguous there) and a source build is the only remaining writer of `build/Release/better_sqlite3.node`; `node-gyp` never runs for it at all — 13.0.3 declares no install script, so the only candidate is the implicit `node-gyp rebuild` npm synthesizes for any package shipping a `binding.gyp`, and `pnpm-workspace.yaml` denies it (`allowBuilds: better-sqlite3: false`): upstream writes that `binding.gyp` to no-op when a host prebuild exists, but configure runs before gyp evaluates the condition, and on `windows-latest` node-gyp 11.5.0 cannot parse the image's Visual Studio 18 install, so approving the build fails `pnpm install` outright on a platform whose prebuild is sitting in the tarball unused — measured on the matrix below, four targets green with the build approved and `win32-x64` red, all five green with it denied — and denial is what makes the second conjunct absolute, since `build/Release/` cannot hold a `.node` if no build ever runs. The task sweeps every Electron-41 stamp that is an instruction rather than provenance — `src/main/windows/factory.ts`, `electron.vite.config.ts`, the shipped test files, the package description, and the `.github/workflows/ci.yml` SUID-sandbox comment, whose step also stops working on 44: that line publishes **no install script at all** — a **42.0** breaking change 44 inherits rather than one 44 introduced (`docs/breaking-changes.md` §Breaking API Changes (42.0): `electron` "no longer downloads itself via `postinstall` script", and `ELECTRON_SKIP_BINARY_DOWNLOAD` "is no longer supported"; 41.6.1 published `postinstall: node install.js`, and every manifest from 42 through 45 carries no `scripts` field) — and moved binary acquisition to module scope in `npm/index.js`, reached by every `require('electron')`, so `pnpm install` leaves no `dist/`. **Materialized at install time:** `apps/desktop` carries an idempotent `postinstall` running `apps/desktop/scripts/materialize-electron.ts` (CREATE — the `scripts/` + `.ts` home this task's own **Files** list names), which invokes Electron's own install entry point when the binary is absent, skips when it is present (mirroring upstream's `isInstalled()` — `dist/version` equal to the package version, `path.txt` present, `dist/<path.txt>` on disk), fails closed if the binary is still absent afterwards, and honors `ELECTRON_SKIP_BINARY_DOWNLOAD` — an escape 42.0 dropped — 44.5.1 still publishes an `install.js`, and it no longer reads the variable — reinstated by this wrapper on its own terms — so a leg that never launches Electron stays fast. The seam is measured, not assumed: a full workspace install runs a workspace project's `postinstall` with that project as cwd, a scoped `--filter` install that excludes the project does not, and `pnpm-workspace.yaml`'s `allowBuilds` gates DEPENDENCY scripts only and never a workspace project's own — which is why the root `prepare` step was rejected (it runs on every install, including the legs that must not download). The smoke-test job runs that same script rather than a second acquisition path and takes the sandbox helper from beside the executable the resolved module will spawn rather than searching `.pnpm` for one; every other CI install sets `ELECTRON_SKIP_BINARY_DOWNLOAD` — the renderer-only coverage job, the control-plane Node-24 job and the docs-corpus job, none of which launches Electron (`electron-vite build` needs only the package's `version` field, verified by building with `dist/` absent), while the `native-prebuilds` job's scoped `--filter` never reaches the project at all; the `test:smoke` script runs it before the timed spawn; and `apps/desktop/tests/launch.smoke.test.ts` refuses BEFORE spawning with `Electron binary not materialized; run pnpm install` instead of paying the 120–160 MB platform download (130 MB `darwin-arm64`, 158 MB `win32-x64` at 44.5.1) inside the spawn deadline and reporting a timeout — runs ADR-023's `lifecycle.gc.test.ts` under 44 (a failure is a Plan-020 §Risks item, not a skip), re-runs the [Console Sources §Console Design Sources](../architecture/console-sources.md#console-design-sources) platform-feature probe on the 44 binary and re-grades its two ❌ cells where they flip, and carries the [Spec-013 §Driver Pin](../specs/013-persistence-and-recovery.md#driver-pin) change in-swap (flip-and-restore): the pin sentence moves to 13.0.3 only with the daemon's pragma-readback tests green under 13.0.3 and the `.backup()` contract re-probed against it (the daemon ships no backup caller yet, so Spec-013's backup-policy atomicity contract has no suite until the backup writer lands — the direct probe stands in), re-running on the pinned tree the scratch-tree re-evaluation ADR-021's entry records: suite identical under both pins, #1507 reproduced on 13.0.1 and closed on 13.0.3 under this repo's own worker-terminate shape (so 13.0.2 is the floor beneath the pin), and `@types/better-sqlite3` staying at its pin because no 13.x types exist. **Provenance on every target:** the check runs in a dedicated `native-prebuilds` CI job over a hosted-runner matrix covering the five ADR-021 targets — `ubuntu-latest` (linux-x64), `ubuntu-24.04-arm` (linux-arm64), `macos-latest` (darwin-arm64), `macos-15-intel` (darwin-x64), `windows-latest` (win32-x64) — each installing the narrowest filter that resolves `better-sqlite3`, asserting the two-conjunct provenance, opening a database, and reading back the WAL-reopen pragma on the 24.21 floor; a target whose hosted label is unavailable is recorded on this task with the closest label used, never dropped silently. **Files:** `apps/desktop/package.json` (EXTEND), `packages/runtime-daemon/package.json` (EXTEND — the one cross-package touch, named in this phase's gate), `pnpm-lock.yaml` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/src/main/windows/factory.ts` + `apps/desktop/electron.vite.config.ts` + `apps/desktop/tests/launch.smoke.test.ts` + `apps/desktop/tests/lifecycle.gc.test.ts` (EXTEND — comment sweep, and the two spawn harnesses' `ix` / `cmd` / `args` renamed to `probeTagIndex` / `spawnCommand` / `spawnArguments`), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (EXTEND — comment sweep, the Node pins, the prebuild assertion, and the Electron-binary materialization the 44 line's dropped install script forces), `apps/desktop/scripts/materialize-electron.ts` (CREATE — the install-time materializer both the `postinstall` and CI call, in `scripts/` because it is invoked BY NAME from a package script rather than run DURING `pnpm build`, and TypeScript under `node --experimental-strip-types` so the package has one script language and no `.mjs` + hand-written `.d.mts` pair), `apps/desktop/tests/launch.smoke.test.ts` (EXTEND — the pre-spawn refusal), `.nvmrc` (EXTEND — the last remaining `22.12` pin in the tree; every other occurrence is a code comment recording where a behavior was measured, which is provenance and stays), `.github/workflows/docs-corpus.yml` (EXTEND — the Node pins and the install-time download skip), `packages/contracts/package.json` + `packages/client-sdk/package.json` + `packages/crypto-paseto/package.json` (EXTEND — `engines.node`; the root `package.json` carries no `engines` install gate), `CONTRIBUTING.md` + `CLAUDE.md` + `docs/decisions/023-electron-main-process-window-retention.md` + `docs/plans/024-agent-definitions-and-peer-invocation.md` (EXTEND — floor sentences), `docs/specs/013-persistence-and-recovery.md` + `docs/decisions/021-v1-toolchain-selection.md` (EXTEND — the in-swap edits), `pnpm-workspace.yaml` + `.npmrc` (EXTEND — the `allowBuilds` denial and the install-policy comments it corrects) **Spec coverage:** Desktop App Implementation Notes §Electron Version And Support Window (the 44.x target); Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian) (the `browser` pane kind's detached-`WebContentsView` dependency on the 42+ bounds fix) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-2 (the locked window still builds and loads under the new runtime), I-020-6 (the privileged scheme survives the runtime move — the startup-order test re-runs on 44)
- **T-020p-1B-5 — Main's registry of windows.** Every window is alike and none is the main one. Main keeps the registry: the windows' focus order (the window used last), each one's place in main's window-place file, which keeps an entry only for each console window open now and the last one closed, and one place per pane kind that no close or quit drops, and, at start, the hidden console window — never shown, its background throttling off, kept until quit — whose document opens the window used last first and the rest from its kept layout, so closing any window a person sees leaves the others running. Every window a person sees is built by main's `setWindowOpenHandler` answering the hidden console document's own `window.open` with `{ action: 'allow', createWindow }`: main builds its own `BaseWindow` through the locked constructor from its own options and the place the frame name's window id keys, adopts the handed `webContents` in a `WebContentsView` — so the child keeps the renderer's sandbox, context isolation and CSP, with no preload and no bridge of its own — pairs the two closes both ways, and mirrors each window document's title onto its `BaseWindow`: `<session title> — <project>`, or `— Chat` for a chat, and a pane's own window `<Pane> — <session title>`. No window it builds is ever in Preview's relay target list. The window-place file keeps each window's place, size, display and maximized or fullscreen state, puts it back before the window is built so no window first appears at another size, pulls a rectangle off every attached display back on screen, and keeps a pane's own window's place and size per pane kind. On macOS there is no system title strip: the hidden-inset title style with the window-controls overlay on (`titleBarOverlay: true`), so the window's document reads where content may start past the traffic lights as `env(titlebar-area-x)` and their area's depth as `env(titlebar-area-height)`; the frame's rail track is the larger of the rail's own width and the first and the rail's top padding is the second, so the traffic lights sit at the top of the rail with nothing to the right of it under them, a width and an inset that are gone in fullscreen and back on leaving it with no message from main, and the console filling the window, the rail and each session header's empty space being the window's drag regions (`app-region: drag`) with every control inside them and the session's title cut out (`no-drag`), and a view's title dragging the view; every overlay that can cover a drag region — a dialog and its backdrop, the command palette and its backdrop — is cut out too; on Windows and Linux the system's own strip stays and the rail keeps its own width with no inset. The application menu gains the rest of its one static template: the Edit roles, the File menu's `New window`, whose accelerator is the live `New window` binding and is rebuilt when that binding changes, the View menu's `System · Light · Dark` color-scheme choice with the one in force ticked, and, on macOS, the menu-bar icon (`Tray`) that stays when the last window closes, built on its idle face, `apps/desktop/resources/menu-bar-idleTemplate.png` with its `@2x` and the `menu-bar-idle.svg` it is drawn from, copied unchanged from the approved icon set in place of `trayTemplate.png` and its `@2x`, which go; T-020r-5-17 adds the other two faces and keeps the face live. Closing the last window is platform-correct: on macOS the app keeps running with its menu bar and menu-bar icon, and a click on the dock icon or the menu-bar icon (`activate`) opens a window on the session used last; on Windows and Linux closing the last window quits the app after the same flush a quit runs (T-020r-3-3). Closing every window stops no session. The hidden window never closes, so `window-all-closed` never fires: main counts the windows a person sees, and the last of them closing drives the last-window behavior above. **Files:** main's `windows/` (EXTEND — the registry, the window-open handler and the window-place file beside the window factory), the application menu at the root of `main/` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/resources/` (EXTEND — `menu-bar-idleTemplate.png`, its `@2x` and `menu-bar-idle.svg` added, `trayTemplate.png` and its `@2x` removed) with `apps/desktop/AGENTS.md`'s line on `resources/` (EXTEND), the stylesheets of the rail, the session header, the dialog and the command palette in the renderer (EXTEND — the inset and the drag regions), the end-to-end title-bar test (CREATE), and their tests **Tests:** on macOS closing the last window leaves the app running and `activate` opens a window on the session used last; on Windows and Linux closing the last window flushes and quits; closing the first window a person opened and minimizing a second leaves a third drawing at full rate; a control inside a drag region still takes its press; in a real macOS window the rail's first destination starts below the traffic lights' reported area, the inset is gone in fullscreen and back on leaving it, and off macOS no overlay is reported and the rail has no inset; the rail and the session header compute as drag regions and the rail's buttons and the session's title as cut out. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Main Process Responsibilities (window management; the platform menu) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-2 (every window it builds carries the locked block), I-020-7 (every console window is a renderer child with no preload of its own), I-020-10 (main builds, focuses and places windows and reads nothing of the renderer's layout)
- **T-020p-1B-5 EXTEND — the app menu and About.** On macOS the template opens with the app menu under the app's name, holding the platform's own `about`, `hide`, `hideOthers`, `unhide` and `quit` roles, which macOS shows as About, Hide, Hide Others, Show All and Quit; on Windows and Linux it ends with a Help menu whose one row is the `about` role, `About AI Sidekicks`. Before it installs the menu, main calls `app.setAboutPanelOptions` once with `applicationName` from `app.getName()` and `applicationVersion` from `app.getVersion()`, the same facts General's first row reads; on macOS `version`, the same version, which the panel shows in parentheses in place of the development bundle's Electron build number; and on Windows and Linux `iconPath`, the app icon as a 64-pixel PNG exported from `build/icon.icon`'s default appearance, because Linux shows no value that is not set and Windows has no About panel of its own, so Electron draws a message box with that icon. macOS takes the icon from the bundle. The console builds no About page, window or dialog. **Provides:** About on every platform — the system's About panel on macOS, the desktop's about dialog on Linux, Electron's message box on Windows — each showing the app icon, the app's name and its version; the macOS app menu's Hide, Hide Others, Show All and Quit. **Consumes:** T-020p-1B-2's static template and T-020p-1B-5's rest of it; Electron's `about` role and `app.setAboutPanelOptions`. **Files:** the application menu at the root of `main/`, `menu.ts` (EXTEND — the About panel filled before the menu is installed), `apps/desktop/resources/icon.png` (CREATE), the application menu's test (EXTEND) **Tests:** `main-unit`, the shared `electron` mock: on `darwin` the first menu holds the five app-menu roles in order and no Help menu is built, and on `win32` and `linux` the Help menu's one row has the `about` role and no app menu is built; `app.setAboutPanelOptions` is called once, before `Menu.setApplicationMenu`, with the app's name and version, on `darwin` the same version as `version`, and on `win32` and `linux` an `iconPath` that names an existing file, or in an installed app `icon.png` in `process.resourcesPath`. **Spec coverage:** [Spec-021 §Main Process Responsibilities](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#main-process-responsibilities) (window size, menus and title — the app menu and About) **Verifies invariant:** none

After Phase 1B merges, Phase 1C's console has a served origin with IndexedDB on the 44.x runtime, and Phase 3's T-020r-3-3 composes these legs behind the crash reporter and the single-instance lock without re-authoring any of them.

### Phase 1C — The Console

**Goal:** Build the whole Meridian console [Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian)](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#console-design-meridian) fixes — the app frame and icon rail, the all-sessions list, the row of session views a window holds (each view's header, transcript, composer and pane layout), every pane kind, the composer at its pinned home, the settings pages, the palette and keybinding grammar, and the fixture scenarios the app starts on with `--fixture <scenario>` — over the shipped partial renderer and the Phase-1B origin, against a fixture implementation at the real boundary for every wire not yet built and against the live bridge for every wire that is. The console is a projection over already-registered bridge namespaces inside the spec envelope: a task here mints no wire method, event type, error code, table, or column, and one it needs is stated in its owning spec and plan first; a surface whose wire is not yet built runs over its boundary's fixture implementation in the fixture builds and reads the wire live in the PR that builds it. Eight tasks, one per delivery PR, the feature tasks (T-020p-1C-2..7) dispatching in parallel after T-020p-1C-1 and T-020p-1C-8 landing last, after the macOS build is complete. Every one of them passes the bundle-size budget and the tiers [Desktop App Implementation Notes §Console Test Tiers](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#console-test-tiers) registers other than accessibility and endurance. **Budgets are per task.** T-020p-1C-1 lands the bundle-size budget in CI and every later console PR gates on it; a budget whose subject a feature task builds is measured on its named workload in that task's PR — T-020p-1C-2 the frame time, the renderer heap, streaming CPU and first-transcript-row latency on the `concurrent-streaming` scenario, and the heap at rest and idle CPU with one session open and nothing streaming; T-020p-1C-7 the per-terminal memory; and T-020p-1C-8 lands the accessibility and endurance tiers, which gate every PR from then on (I-020-9).

**Precondition:** Phase 1B merged (the served origin, the 44.x runtime, the `main-unit` project). No upstream plan gate — beyond what the shipped partial bridge types already declare; the live wiring of any surface waits on the plan or remainder phase that builds its wire, and Phase 5 **adopts** the result (T-020r-5-1 EXTEND).

- The console's folders under `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/` that §Target Areas Renderer registers, laid out by concern: `lib/` (the DAG floor — the runtime tripwire registry, the keyed registry, the single refusal constructor, the emitter, and the clock), `styles/` (the Meridian tokens and the twelve-step OKLCH hue system), `routing/` (the route vocabulary with its parser, formatter, and rail destination map), `components/` and `hooks/` (the transcript row, chips, the three refusal shapes, the five kinds of nothing, and the chord formatting the palette consumes), `layout/` and `app/` (icon rail, hash router, app frame, error boundaries), `store/` (one zustand store per open session, partitioned entity maps, the single apply chokepoint with per-frame coalescing, per-row selectors), `store/persistence/` (`idb` under an own per-session partition with LRU trim and the quota gauge, and the in-memory adapter that runs and says so when the scheme is not privileged), `services/` (the live clients; the fixture scenarios sit outside the tree in the desktop app's `fixtures/` folder, each fixture implementation beside the boundary it substitutes, and none of it is in a release bundle), `layout/CommandPalette/`, `registries/commands/` and `registries/keybindings/` (the command palette, the when-scoped chord grammar, the keybinding service), `features/sessions/` (the sessions list, the row of session views and each view's pane layout), `registries/` (the pane registry and its single mount point, and the other registration mechanisms), `features/transcript/`, each feature's `contributions/` (its pane registrations only; a pane body lives in its owning feature, and `app/` makes the registration calls), `features/agents/`, `features/repos/`, `features/workflows/`, `features/preview/`, `features/terminal/`, and `features/settings/`. Console tests are co-located as `**/*.test.{ts,tsx}` with no `__tests__/` directory; the browser-mode, accessibility, budget, and end-to-end tiers live under `apps/desktop/tests/<tier>/`.
- `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/features/composer/` — the composer at its pinned home, authored by T-020p-1C-3 and wired live by T-020r-5-2; the composer's folder is this plan's own, and the console's never-touch rule covers only the bodies other plans author in it and beside it (Plan-010's transcript rows and its context ring, Plan-002's run controls).
- `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/app/App.tsx` — mounts the console's app frame from T-020p-1C-1 on; Phase 5 later reduces `AppShell` (`layout/AppShell/`) to the thin host (T-020r-5-1 EXTEND).

#### Tasks

- **T-020p-1C-1 — Console substrate.** The app frame, icon rail, and hash router, with the screen view one generic `ScreenView<S extends AnyScreen>` over a typed `Screen<Id, Params>` union, each case naming a rail destination and the parameters it opens with, so the compiler checks that whatever opens a screen hands it the right parameters; the Meridian tokens, the twelve-step hue system (which step each agent wears is [Plan-013](./013-multi-agent-orchestration.md) T4.1's rule) and shared components. **The tokens:** both themes, Meridian and Graphite, each in a light and a dark scheme, each scheme's palette declared once and the scheme in force marked on the root, under follow-the-system too; the type scale's base steps `--xs` to `--xl`, 11, 13, 14, 16 and 20 px at the default root, with the other sizes the screen uses, while the corner radii, 3, 6 and 10 px, the hairlines and the 2.25 px glyph stroke on the 14 px glyph grid stay in pixels and never grow with `Text size`; four letter-spacing steps, each from a published type system: `tracking-heading` 0.14 em and `tracking-label` 0.12 em inside Material's uppercase range of 0.089 to 0.167 em, `tracking-caps` 0.09 em from Material's button (1.25 px over 14 px), and `tracking-mixed` 0.02 em between Carbon's 0.027 em at 12 px and 0.011 em at 14 px; the run-time contrast check over all four renderings, naming the rendering, the pair and the ratio on a miss; the one focus mark the browser's own `:focus-visible`, no control setting a ring of its own, drawn just inside the edge of a control its container would clip; no status indicator and no cursor that ever blinks; and every live clock's value held in the model and never read back out of the page, so a re-render or a re-mount never resets it, every clock figure and every date written from the machine's own settings the bridge's `app` facts carry (T-020r-2-9 EXTEND); the overlay scrollbar every scroller but the conversation draws (`overlayscrollbars` with its React binding, the `os-theme-meridian` class reading the tokens, `autoHide: 'move'`, and the 500 ms rest as one named duration in the motion module); the glyph set, with `bellOff` for a muted session's row; the refusal words, one code-to-words mapper behind the refusal card that every screen uses, which reads one of three sentences of the app's own, never the method's name, where the app itself fails a call — `This request could not be sent to the background service.` when it cannot build a request the service would accept, `The background service's reply could not be read.` when the answer does not parse, and `This read was closed before it finished.` when a read nobody waits for any more is closed; the stores and the apply chokepoint; the persistence layer with both adapters (UI state only — its value classes declared once and enforced at the write chokepoint; a draft is the daemon's and is never written to window or renderer storage); the fixture scenarios in the desktop app's `fixtures/` folder — `first-run`, `concurrent-streaming`, `multi-session-streaming`, `transcript-states`, `empty-session`, `waiting-for-input`, `approval-request` and `terminal-lease`, with `multi-session-streaming` holding the full dataset of several sessions across more than one project, one streaming a scripted reply word by word, any of them opened with `--session` — the renderer's fixture composition, and main's fixture launch, which stops startup with an error on an unknown scenario, a missing value or an unknown session and never falls back; the palette and keybinding grammar, the palette listing every match and drawing only the rows in view, the palette's acts — the mute's row and `Copy link` drawn from the session's one actions list, `Copy link`'s row and `Copy conversation`'s flashing before the palette closes — and find; the error boundaries, each reading `<Region> stopped rendering. Retry`; the one try-again control in `components/`, a faint clickable word at the right end of the line it belongs to reading `Retry` or `Try again`, with no box, border or icon, which every retry on every screen draws — the catch-up line's and the run page's among them — rather than an outlined button of its own; the one line every live stream that reopens on its own draws while it is down, `Live updates paused · Reconnecting…`, gone the moment the stream is open again — the session's own streams, Providers' account stream, General's settings stream, MCP servers' status stream and the Runs tab's run subscription — a lost connection to the background service drawing the working line's `Connection lost` instead; the string table every component reads its words from, so no component writes its words inline; the tripwires; and the console's test projects — the `renderer` unit project over the tests beside their source under `src/renderer/src/`, the browser-mode project, the accessibility, budget, and end-to-end tiers under `apps/desktop/tests/<tier>/`, each with a glob no other project reaches; and the bundle-size budget in CI through `size-limit` 13.x (`apps/desktop/tests/budget/` — the brotli and gzip sizes of the built `out/renderer` assets, budgets in its one config — on the tier-1 desktop job; a maintained library measures the same budget, so the project keeps no own-built test for it). **Tests:** a live clock re-mounted mid-run reads the elapsed figure it had, not zero; with the bridge reporting a twelve-hour cycle a clock figure reads `2:20 PM` and with a twenty-four-hour one `14:20`; and the contrast check fails, naming the rendering, on a Graphite dark token pair dropped below its floor. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{lib,styles,routing,components,hooks,store,services,registries,layout,app}/` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/app/App.tsx` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/vitest.config.ts` + `apps/desktop/turbo.json` + `apps/desktop/package.json` (EXTEND — the console dependencies §Target Areas Renderer lists and the test projects), the desktop app's `tests/` folder (CREATE), its `fixtures/` folder (CREATE — the scenarios, their shared data and the catalog), main's fixture launch at the root of `main/` (CREATE), the desktop app's `electron.vite.config.ts` (EXTEND — the fixture gate, on in the `development` and `fixtures` modes and in no release build, beside the smoke one; the `dev` script is `electron-vite dev`), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (EXTEND — the bundle-size gate) **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian) (§Meridian, the design language, §Rules every console surface obeys, §The fixture bridge, §Budgets, §Persistence on the renderer scheme); Desktop App Implementation Notes §Console Libraries (every ADOPT row's constraints enforced where the library lands); Desktop App Implementation Notes §Console Test Tiers (every tier registered) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-6 (the persistence layer runs on IndexedDB when the scheme is privileged and on the in-memory adapter, visibly, when it is not), I-020-8 (fixture code stays outside the product, and a bad `--fixture` launch stops with an error), I-020-9 (the budget and test tiers gate this and every later console task)
- **T-020p-1C-2 — The transcript.** The transcript **frame** — the virtualized scroll chokepoint, reading anchor, reveal engine, run groups, and system messages — the streaming-markdown and tool-row **primitives** (a tool call is a line, never a card), the `concurrent-streaming` scenario, and the build-only flag, off by default and visible only to whoever sets it, that logs the middle and the slow cases of what drawing the live rows costs in a running session; the `transcript` pane's chrome and its typed row mount point. **The rows are Plan-010's.** The row vocabulary of [Spec-011 §Transcript Entry Types](../specs/011-transcript-and-reasoning.md#transcript-entry-types) — every kind-narrowed row body over `TranscriptEventRow`, the superseded treatment, the provider-switch lines, the input-ask card — is authored by Plan-010 Phase 4 in the transcript feature's rows folder, `features/transcript/rows/`, and **absorbed by import** into the row mount point when those rows land (the absorb-by-import rule §Target Areas Renderer states; no CP is minted because the import direction is the console's own); until then the mount point renders fixture rows from scenario data through the same primitives, and the fixture rows are deleted in the PR that lands Plan-010's rows. This plan authors no row body for a Spec-011 entry type, and one authored here is a review rejection. **What the frame does while a turn is streaming — four rules, each of them a thing the frame either does or visibly fails at.** **(1) Only the tail re-renders while following.** A row grows at its tail, so the tail block is the unit of change: settled text is never re-parsed or re-rendered, and one row is re-measured rather than the list. The retained window and the sub-row reading anchor stay this frame's own, but the end anchor and follow-on-append are **the virtualizer's**, taken as its own options rather than rebuilt here — both ship on the build the [Desktop App Implementation Notes §Console Libraries](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#console-libraries) verdict row already pins, so the pin is the thing to re-read at a bump, not this sentence. The conversation scroller keeps the browser's own scroll anchoring off (`overflow-anchor: none`) at all times, following or not, because the browser's mechanism and this one cannot both be driving the same scroller, and a picture or a diagram that typesets three screens up must not rock the column under the reader. **(2) The reveal runs at the pace the words arrive.** The rate is measured off the wire rather than set to a constant, with two gears and hysteresis for a backlog — catch up when the queue is deep or stale, drop back only after a calm stretch — and the frame budget as the ceiling, so the reveal neither trails the wire by more than about a breath nor idles on a slow one. **(3) Reduced motion reaches the reveal engine itself**, not only the stylesheet: the engine reads the machine's setting and resolves a stream straight to its full text, so the rule and the behavior cannot disagree. **(4) The client takes the daemon's batched frames and repairs a gap by snapshot.** It consumes the frame [Plan-005](./005-local-ipc-and-daemon-control.md) T-005p-2D-1 emits — changes carrying their cursors — and on a frame flagging a gap it repairs from the record by cursor, re-opening `session.subscribe` after the last position it kept, and past a gap of 1,024 events takes a snapshot read instead of a fill, so the transcript is never silently short; while the repair runs, `Catching up…` under the header is the one visible sign, with no second notice for missing entries. **Three checks, because these are the failures that cost:** a burst leaves the follow neither dumping the person out of the tail nor drifting off it, asserted on the scroll position across the burst rather than on a render count; a flagged gap is repaired on **both its arms** — by a fill where the gap is within 1,024 events and by a snapshot read past it — and on each the client that fell behind ends holding exactly what a client that never fell behind holds, with `Catching up…` the only notice while it runs, since a silently short transcript is what this whole path exists to prevent; and with reduced motion set the reveal engine emits no animation at all, asserted on the engine rather than on the stylesheet, which is the arm a stylesheet-only rule passes and the engine fails. **The frame's other duties.** The ask rail rides the transcript's right edge inside its scroller, pointer-only: invisible at rest and fading in once the pointer rests in its edge zone, hidden from assistive technology with no focusable control, absent while the session holds fewer than two asks and while the column is at its floor; one hairline tick per message the person sent, the tick of the ask nearest the middle of the visible band filled in the accent, a hovered tick showing the ask's first words and a pressed one scrolling there, with no preview card, no widening and no arrow (the two chords are T-020r-5-6's). Every one-shot read a pane makes takes a cancel signal, canceled when the pane closes or a newer read replaces it; Review, Preview and the terminal load their bodies the first time they open and stay loaded, a body that fails reading one line naming that pane with `Retry`. A selection dragged into a band a line height or two deep at the top or bottom of the conversation scrolls it, faster the further in, until the button is released. A session long enough to reflow is held blank while its row heights settle and shown in one piece, with a failsafe so it is never left blank and a short session shown at once; a session opened before mounts at the heights its rows had last time, a row with none taking its kind's estimate until it measures. A step of `Text size` or a move of `Transcript width` re-measures every drawn row and drops the kept height of every row that is not drawn, the row being read staying under the eye. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/features/{transcript,sessions}/` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian) (§The surface set — the session screen; §Meridian, the design language — transcript rows, motion, density); [Spec-011 §Flow Row Kinds](../specs/011-transcript-and-reasoning.md#flow-row-kinds) (Agent prose — the markdown, math, diagram, footnote, dead-link, admonition, code-block and table primitives); Spec-021 §Signature Feature Composition Sketches (Transcript / Runs View, mounted as this pane's Plan-010 legs land) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-8, I-020-9
- **T-020p-1C-3 — Composer and inspector.** The composer at `features/composer/` (the working line, one card at a time, the command list with its discovery-only autocomplete — its Sidekicks group drawing the one agent-identity row in `components/` that Plan-024 T5.1 builds and the workflow node's chooser also draws — the attachment row, the draft field with its send control, the toolbar, the session strip, and the draft's place, which the daemon holds), the inspector, the edit-and-resend affordance's mount point (the affordance itself is Plan-002 T4.6), the input-ask card's mount point in the composer card (the card itself is Plan-010's, absorbed by import under T-020p-1C-2's ownership rule). The attachment row mounts the held-notes chip and its discard that [Plan-008](./008-gitflow-pr-and-diff-attribution.md) T8.10 builds and draws no notes chip of its own. While a drag is over the card the card carries a tint and nothing else, and a drop anywhere else on the session column does nothing and draws nothing. The level list's one-line note on `Sandboxed` reads that it never asks and writes only inside the worktree and the repository's git folder, whose hooks and config stay closed. The composer's own surfaces — the model, effort, speed, mode, level and tool-server lists, the folded-chips menu, the file picker and the draft's command and mention lists — hand the caret back to the message box when they close. The inspector holds only the facts of the session on screen and re-keys when its view switches session; Identity's facts read `Repo`, `Path`, `Branch`, `Against base`, `Mount`, `Created`, `Provider`, `Account`, `Notifications` and `Address`; and `Cost` draws no input, output, cache, reasoning, per-turn or per-agent line, a long account name keeping its line with its figures dropped to the next, right-aligned; `Runs here`, on every session, carries the count in its header and one row per workflow run in this folder, its name and when it ran, `· running` after the time while it runs, or `No runs yet.` where there are none. **Tests:** an inspector open while its view switches session draws the second session's facts and none of the first's; a folder with one finished run and one running renders `2` in the `Runs here` header and `· running` after the second run's time only, and a folder with none renders `No runs yet.`; closing the model list by Escape or by a pick leaves the caret in the message box. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/features/composer/` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/features/inspector/` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/services/{driver-capabilities,provider-accounts,wire-reads}/` (EXTEND — the composer's and inspector's daemon reads inside the `services/` folder T-020p-1C-1 creates), with the account fold and notification hold in `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/store/provider-accounts/`, the approval flow projection in `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/store/session/events/` and the queue's reading in `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/features/transcript/queue/` (EXTEND) **Spec coverage:** [Spec-021 §Session Composer — the chrome every session view carries](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#session-composer--the-chrome-every-session-view-carries--plan-020-desktop-app-and-renderer) (one agent, one composer; the card's parts in order; the autocomplete); Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian) (§The surface set — the composer, the inspector pane kind) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-8, I-020-9
- **T-020p-1C-3 EXTEND — one addressee: what the composer card and the working line need is hoisted to the surfaces that draw it.** The one waiting approval, an agent's question, a plan waiting for its verdict and a run halted on the restart question (T-020r-5-19, not built) are each **the single card in the composer**, one at a time; pausing and interrupting a run are the last pressable words of the **working line** at the head of that card: while a turn runs `Interrupt` stays there and reaches only the agent in view, and after the interrupt, while agents the session dispatched are still live, the line ends in `Interrupt everything`; and what is queued is the transcript's own last rows, each offering move, edit, remove and send-now. The composer addresses the session's own agent, and the one other thing it can address is another session, which an `@` at the head of the draft names — so its **send router**, its **target chip** and its **posture chip** go: there is no target picker, no machine picker and no posture control anywhere on the card, and addressing another session is the draft's own text rather than a control. **The hoist comes first, and it is not optional:** the approval card with its always-allow control, the question card (`QuestionCard`), the run controls' own reading and dispatch, the steer box, and the queue rows are all bodies the composer card, the working line and the transcript still draw, and `feature-isolation` in `apps/desktop/.dependency-cruiser.mjs` makes one feature importing another an error — what two features share goes through `registries/`, or down to `components/`, `hooks/`, `store/` or `lib/`. So each of those moves to the surface that draws it — the cards and the run controls into the composer feature, the queue rows to the transcript feature, which owns the pending rows — and nothing forks a copy. Two bodies leave the composer, each placed by what it does rather than by its old folder: the approval rules, each with `Revoke`, move to the inspector's Rules section, and the callback-tool configuration moves to the agent definitions, whose tool allowlist covers the callback tools. There is no goal card: a goal the person sets is a system message in the transcript, never the session's label. The plan card's `Fresh session with` list carries, on a provider that cannot give the session's level, the line `<Level> isn't available on this <Provider> account` (or `<Level> isn't available on <Provider> on Windows`), and picking that row opens the level list in place with that provider's levels and the nearest more careful one marked; the fresh session starts at the level picked, over the daemon side [Plan-009](./009-approvals-permissions-and-trust-boundaries.md) T3.11 builds. - **T-020p-1C-4 — Agents and the settings frame.** The `agents` pane; the settings frame and its ten pages — General, Providers, MCP servers, Projects, Browser, Keyboard, Appearance, Notifications, Runtime, Devices — each page a projection that derives no eligibility of its own. Each page opens with its `Kept on …` line: Appearance, Keyboard, Notifications and General's first block (the install's facts, its update block and `Keep crash reports`) are the device's own, `Kept on this device.`; General's second block, Providers, MCP servers, Projects, Browser and Runtime are the machine's, `Kept on <machine>.`; and Devices is the account's, the same list on every machine and every device. Projects draws `Branch names` under `Every project` and `Branch names for this project` on an opened project, and the Keyboard page holds forty-six rows over forty-seven keys ([Spec-021 §Settings](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#settings)), `Search sent messages` on `⌃R` in `Sessions` among them, and `Open Workflows` (`⌘⇧W`, which opens the Workflows screen and never toggles it), `Next waiting` (`⌥⌘N`) and `Answer this run` (`⌥⌘↩`) in `App`, the last two inert while a text field has focus. A session renames in place: a double-click on the header's title or on its list row turns it into one field, Enter saving through `session.rename`, Escape canceling, and an empty name restoring the first-message title, and `Rename session…` on the actions list opens the same field. **The settings frame's behaviors.** The page list's cursor opens the page it lands on — an arrow, `Home` or `End` draws that page at once and the cursor wraps at both ends, in the order the list draws. The one box above the page list finds controls: every typed word must appear in a control's label, the heading it sits under or its one-line hint, hits ranked label, then heading, then hint, ties in page order, each reading its label over `Page › Heading`, Enter landing on the control, and a term nothing matches reading `No setting matches “<term>”.` Arriving on a page from a link, a search hit or another surface opens any folded section holding the named control, scrolls the control into the middle of the view and lights it briefly, the address's third part naming the control, and a control the page is not drawing leaves the person on the page. Closing Settings commits the field being edited and never the Projects draft, which waits behind its own `Save`; a control whose value differs from its default carries a mark; every `Reading …` line appears only after the one short fixed delay and only while nothing it stands for is drawn; a failed write rolls the control back to the value it had and says why in an inline strip on the page with `Try again` at its end, and no toast appears; and a window too narrow for the list and the page side by side shows one at a time, the page carrying `‹ Settings`, at the width the two panes' own content needs. General opens under its page note, `What this install is, how it updates itself, and what a new session starts from.` Projects draws its whole body as [Spec-021 §Settings](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#settings) gives it — `Clone new repositories into` with `Change…`, the `Every project` section's environment rows and `Branch names`, then one row per attached project opening in place on `After creating a worktree`, that project's environment rows and `Branch names for this project`, the environment lists' refusals at save and the four actions on the attachment — over the daemon verbs [Plan-006](./006-repo-attachment-and-workspace-binding.md) builds. **Owner bodies are not authored here.** Four bodies these features host belong to other plans: the agent-definition editor (Plan-024, mounted through CP-020-2), the MCP Servers page body (Plan-022, under CP-022-5 and CP-020-3), the Providers page body (Plan-023, over Plan-013's committed-spend read), and the Devices page body (Remote Control's, [Plan-025](./025-remote-control.md) Phase 7, in `features/remote-control/`, contributed through the settings page registry). For the editor, this task ships the pane **chrome only** — route, header, search registration, the empty and error shapes — plus a typed owner mount point (in `features/<feature>/<kind>/`) that the owning subtree mounts into on the CP-020-2 host-mounts shape. For the three pages, it ships the Settings frame and the page list, `features/settings/pages/registry.ts`, whose `providers`, `mcp-servers` and `devices` entries register each page; the owning plan builds the page's body — Providers and MCP servers in that page's folder under `features/settings/pages/` (`providers/`, `mcp-servers/`), Devices through the settings page registry from `features/remote-control/` — and nothing is mounted at a later phase. Until the owner's body lands, each of the three pages renders an empty frame under its heading. Each page's fixture body sits in the page's `fixtures/` folder (`pages/mcp-servers/fixtures/McpFixtureBody.tsx`, `pages/providers/fixtures/AccountsFixtureBody.tsx`): it takes its calls as arguments, is mounted only once a composition has calls to give it, and exists only in the fixture build, because the release build drops every `features/**/fixtures/` folder by path. The fixture body is deleted in the PR that lands the owner's body; from then on the fixture build draws the owner's real body over a fixture implementation of that feature's desktop-facing boundary. The create form's base list draws Review's base picker, the one branch-choosing control [Plan-008](./008-gitflow-pr-and-diff-attribution.md) T8.10 builds, adding only the graying of a branch another worktree holds, and builds no menu of its own. **The setup card** carries an `Edit setup steps` link that opens Settings › Projects on that project. **Clone:** the new-session picker's `Clone repository…` foot (the `Repository URL` field with where the clone will go, `Change…` and `Clone`) and, in the composer card where the setup card runs, the clone card — `Cloning <owner>/<repository>` with git's own progress words and `Cancel`; git's question as one input line under the progress, masked for a password or passphrase; a failure keeping git's last error line, the `Repository URL` field and `Clone`; `Clone canceled` and `Cloned`; and, where Git LFS is missing, its line with `Get large files` — live on `repo.clone`, `repo.cloneSubscribe`, `repo.cloneAnswer`, `repo.cloneCancel` and `repo.largeFilesPull`, which [Plan-006](./006-repo-attachment-and-workspace-binding.md) builds. **Opening by kind:** a file chip under a sent user turn and a staged chip that is not a picture open Review scoped to that file (on a chat, the chat's file pane), the staged one handing focus back to the draft on close; a picture or a PDF opens the quick look; and a changed picture's row in Review opens the quick look with `before` and `after`, which [Plan-008](./008-gitflow-pr-and-diff-attribution.md) T8.10 draws. Every file header in Review and in the file pane carries `Copy path` beside `Open in editor`. Pictures in the flow, under a turn or named in prose, are loaded and drawn by [Plan-010](./010-transcript-and-reasoning.md) T4.19. The screen caches are each a share of physical memory handed to the renderer once at start: loaded pictures at decoded size, 1/64 and at most 256 MiB; cached files at two bytes a character, 1/512, between 16 and 64 MiB; color spans packed, 1/2048, between 4 and 16 MiB. At a budget the least recently used clean entry goes first, a picture's decoded copy is let go once nothing on screen draws it (the quick look closed, its session switched away) and decoded again from the file on return, and the trim runs only while nothing is happening on screen and no turn is live. **The quick look** is a dialog on a light scrim carrying the file's own name for assistive technology: the picture at its natural size capped to the session column, the name on one line beneath it, and a PDF as its first page with `Page 1 of <n>` under the name. Its faint `×` takes focus when it opens, the transcript, the composer and the inspector keep running and stay usable under it, and it closes on a press on the scrim, on the `×`, on Escape and on a session switch, putting focus back on whatever opened it — except after a session switch, which leaves focus where the switch put it. A pinch, or `⌘`-wheel (`Ctrl`-wheel on Windows and Linux), zooms the picture about the pointer, a drag pans a picture larger than the surface, `Home` or a double press returns it to its fitted size, and closing forgets the zoom; there are no arrows between pictures, no zoom control and no gallery. The quick look carries `Cover part of it` over a staged picture, and the agent receives only the covered copy, whose hidden pixels are gone, never the original. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/features/repos/` (CREATE) **Tests:** the quick look closed by Escape puts focus back on the chip that opened it, and the next picture opens at its fitted size whatever the last one was zoomed to; a trim never runs while a turn is live, and at a budget it evicts the least recently used clean entry and never a dirty one; a picture whose session is switched away has its decoded copy let go at the next idle moment and is decoded again on return; a question git asks during a clone reaches git as the typed answer and nowhere else. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian) (§The surface set — the worktree switcher and its setup card; the `diff` pane kind); Spec-021 §Renderer Responsibilities (the repo and diff features); [Spec-008 §Required Behavior](../specs/008-worktree-lifecycle-and-execution-modes.md#required-behavior) (the switcher's pending move, removal and put-back) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-8, I-020-9

So they move to their owner, the inspector feature, and every other consumer reaches them by one of those routes; nothing forks a copy. **Files:** the artifact pane's bodies (MOVE, into `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/features/inspector/artifacts/`), `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/features/repos/index.ts` + `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/features/repos/contributions/` (EXTEND — the `artifact` pane registration and its lazy body go with the folder) **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian) (§The surface set — the pane kinds: no artifact pane, and the three surfaces that hold what it drew) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-8, I-020-9

No workflow screen is a pane. Until Plan-014's body is supplied on the host-mounts shape, each mount point renders an empty frame: `features/workflows/components/EngineMountPoint.tsx` draws the frame and mounts a body only once it has one. The human form is the one exception: a step waiting on a form is answered in the run page's step panel by `StepForm` in `features/workflows/runs/page/step/components/StepForm.tsx`, which reads, saves and submits it through `useStepForm` in `features/workflows/runs/page/step/hooks/useStepForm.ts` and draws it with `ParamForm` in `features/workflows/param-form/ParamForm.tsx`, the same generic form renderer the builder's node inspector draws its parameters with — the node's prompt over one field per entry of its input schema, and `Submit` — rather than drawn a second way, and that form ships. The workflows feature has no fixture body; once Plan-014's body is supplied, the fixture build draws that real body over a fixture implementation of the workflows feature's desktop-facing boundary. Beyond the human form, a body authored here for any of the five is a review rejection. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/features/workflows/` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian) (§The surface set — the Workflows rail destination and its screens); Spec-021 §Renderer Responsibilities (the workflow-viewer feature) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-8, I-020-9
- **T-020p-1C-7 — Preview and terminal panes.** The `browser` pane's registration, and the `terminal` pane with its lease line, built here and wired live on its Spec-002 shared-terminal lease obligations; it consumes no `browser.*` wire. **The shell tabs:** closing the pane hides its shells and ends none, and the pane comes back to them; a right-click on a tab raises `Close`, `Close others` and `Close to the right`, the last two grayed with their reason in their own tooltip where there is no such tab, and no close-everything row; a tab whose command ended while another tab was on screen carries a small quiet mark until it is selected; a shell that will not open keeps its tab with the reason in words on its first line, and a shell that ends keeps its tab with its exit line until its own `×` clears it, and clearing the last tab leaves the pane open, reading `No active terminal.` above its `+`, with focus on the `+`; and a tab drags to reorder within the strip through `pty.reorder`, staying the active one, on the console's own pointer drag (T-020r-5-11). A middle-click on a tab closes it exactly as its `×` does, with nothing drawn for the gesture; a tab activates on a press and, while it has focus, on Enter and on Space, the strip holding no chord of its own; and a shell opened from another device lands as a tab without taking focus, becoming the active tab only when none was active. **The body.** Its accessible name is `Shell output`. It paints on the pane's own ground from the theme's tokens, never a dark box: its ground, text, cursor and sixteen named colors read the tokens and change in place with the theme or the scheme, the wider color cube is derived from them with its gray ramp mirrored in the light scheme, and a color a program states outright is drawn as stated. Selection works as it does anywhere — a drag after a little travel, a double press for a word, a third for whole lines, Shift extending — holds its text as output scrolls, scrolls the history when dragged into the body's top or bottom, and is washed with the accent, stronger while the shell has focus. The cursor is a filled block with focus and its outline without, and never blinks. While the body has focus only the platform-modifier chords reach the keybinding service, and every other key a terminal can be sent goes to the shell — printable characters as the input method produces them, Enter, Tab and Shift-Tab, Backspace, Escape, the arrows and Home and End in either cursor mode, Insert, Delete, the paging keys, the function keys, control combinations, and Alt as an escape prefix. `Find in this session` pressed with focus in the shell opens the find box over that tab's output and history, with the transcript's count, chevrons and `×`; the palette's `Clear the terminal` empties the active shell's view and its history on screen and sends nothing to the shell, and is absent while the session has no shell. **A paste** reaches the shell whole: the pane hands the daemon the whole paste, wrapped in the bracketed-paste start and end marks before any split when the program in the shell asked for them, an end marker inside the pasted text stripped, and the daemon writes it in pieces under its own write bound ([Plan-021](./021-rust-pty-sidecar.md) T-021-3B-8); the pane holds no size figure and refuses nothing. Preview's pane body, its page host, the `browser` bridge namespace and Settings › Browser are §Phase 8's (T-020r-8-1 to T-020r-8-4), so this task mints no main-process `WebContentsView` host and no `browser.*` namespace. **The terminal's flow control reaches the process behind it.** The terminal emulator's own flow control is propagated back to the PTY, so a shell writing faster than the renderer can paint is slowed at the shell rather than filling the renderer; the write buffer's high-water mark stays a safety net and never becomes the mechanism, because a watermark that is the mechanism has already dropped or stalled by the time it fires. **This is one leg of a pair and the other is not this plan's:** the sidecar end of the same back-pressure is [Plan-021](./021-rust-pty-sidecar.md)'s, and this pane is built against it rather than reimplementing it — until that leg lands the pane runs over fixtures like the rest of this task, and a renderer-side throttle standing in for the sidecar's is a review rejection. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/features/{preview,terminal}/` (CREATE) **Tests:** with the body focused, Escape, Tab and Alt with a letter reach the shell as their terminal sequences and `⌘K` reaches the console; a middle-click on a tab ends that tab's shell as its `×` does; `Clear the terminal` writes nothing to the shell. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian) (§The surface set — the two kinds built now and wired live once their wires are registered) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-8 (the terminal's fixture implementation sits beside its boundary), I-020-9 (the per-terminal budget)
- **T-020p-1C-8 — Polish.** Built and first run after the macOS build is complete: the accessibility sweeps across every route and dialog, and the endurance reference run, both landing as tiers that gate every PR from then on. **Files:** `apps/desktop/tests/` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/package.json` (EXTEND — the accessibility and endurance tiers back in the aggregate `test` script), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (EXTEND — the accessibility and endurance gates) **Spec coverage:** Desktop App Implementation Notes §Console Test Tiers (the accessibility and endurance tiers); Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian) (§Budgets) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-9 (the accessibility and endurance tiers gate every PR once they land)

After Phase 1C merges, the console is the front end the remainder phases wire live: each surface reads its wire live in the PR that builds that wire, and Phase 5 adopts the tree by mount-and-move (T-020r-5-1 EXTEND).

## Remainder PR Sequence

Phase numbering continues from the partial's Phase 1; the remainder's task ids use the `T-020r-<phase>-<n>` remainder namespace (the partial used `T-020p-1-<n>`).

**Phase ordering is the §Parallelization Notes dependency shape, serialized.** Each phase's precondition names its predecessor, and every upstream-plan item in §Preconditions is named on **each** phase it gates, not on one phase alone.

### Phase 2 — IPC Bridge Registry And Per-Namespace Handlers

**Goal:** Implementation Step 6 in full — the registry of main's bridge handlers plus the per-namespace handler files; this phase's main-process units execute in the `main-unit` Vitest project Phase 1B registers (T-020p-1B-3). Every preload-bridge method from [Preload Bridge Contract](../architecture/contracts/preload-bridge-contract.md) gets exactly one `ipcMain.handle`; auth material is attached server-side and stripped from responses; the partial's `NotImplementedError` stubs are replaced by real forwarding.

**Preconditions.** Consumes Plan-005's daemon client and Plan-025 Phase 1's `daemon.status` handler (CP-020-1), Plan-025's tRPC + relay-WSS clients in the background service, which carries main's control-plane calls, the Plan-005-owned contracts type exports, and the deferred per-subscription request channel. Phase 2 also waits on the `**(Remainder only.)**` items in §Preconditions. Phases 1 and 1B must have merged, and so must Plan-005 Phase R3; Phase 1B is needed because the bridge's `window` members and every handler that answers a window act on main's registry of windows (T-020p-1B-5). T-020r-2-11 also waits on [Plan-005](./005-local-ipc-and-daemon-control.md) T-005r-4-4 and T-005r-4-14.

#### Tasks

- **T-020r-2-1 — Bridge registry.** `bridge/install-handlers.ts` registers one `ipcMain.handle('<channel>', handler)` per contract method and **registers Plan-025 Phase 1's `daemon.status` handler on the `daemon.subscribe` channel without rewriting its body** (CP-020-1). Registration is exhaustive over the contract's method union, so a method added to the desktop's preload API type without a handler fails the build rather than surfacing at runtime. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/install-handlers.ts` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/install-handlers.test.ts` (EXTEND) **Spec coverage:** Preload Bridge Contract (one handler per bridge method); Spec-021 §Main Process Responsibilities (IPC handler registration is main-process work) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-1 (the registry exposes no channel whose name or payload carries auth material)
- **T-020r-2-2 — Daemon forwarding handler.** `bridge/daemon.ts` — `daemon.call` / `daemon.subscribe` forwarding to the Plan-005 daemon client over the main process's own connection, whose `daemon.hello` presented the daemon session token read from its file at that connect. Subscriptions use the parameterized signature §Preconditions waits on, so `session.subscribe` carries `sessionId` and `afterCursor`. A subscription that ends while the page holds it — the daemon completes or refuses it, or the link fails under it — is told to the page once as `completed`, `refused` with the wire error, or `failed`, and the window opens it again from the last cursor it kept, drawing nothing; only the `daemon.status` topic says the service cannot be reached. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/daemon.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/daemon.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Preload Bridge Contract (`daemon.call` / `daemon.subscribe` shapes; the deferred request channel); Spec-021 §Renderer → Main Process → Daemon (via Preload Bridge) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-1 (the daemon session token never appears in a value returned across the bridge)
- **T-020r-2-3 — Control-plane forwarding handler.** Main's control-plane handler behind the preload bridge — `controlPlane.call` forwards the renderer's request, unchanged, to the background service's own `controlPlane.call` over main's own connection to the daemon; main attaches no `Authorization` or `DPoP` header and holds no control-plane credential, and the service sends the call under this machine's access token and a fresh DPoP proof. The service takes the call as `controlPlane.call {procedure, input}` on its local socket; `procedure` is one of `device.list`, `device.linkStart`, `device.linkRedeem`, `device.link`, `device.linkCancel`, `device.rename`, `device.revoke`, `device.forget`, `device.statementList`, `device.pushAddressSet`, `runtimenode.rename` and `runtimenode.remove`, and the service refuses relay negotiation or any other procedure. Responses are sanitized — `set-cookie` and every upstream auth-material-carrying header stripped — before the body returns to the renderer. The anonymous arm is asserted, not assumed: a test drives an anonymous procedure while the service holds no credential and asserts the request is **still issued**, rather than refused for want of one. The credential-present arm is deliberately unpinned — anonymous means authentication is not required, not that it is forbidden, and the relay counts its sign-in routes per source address ([Spec-019 §Canonical Endpoint Group Registry](../specs/019-rate-limiting-policy.md#canonical-endpoint-group-registry)), so a present credential is permitted and immaterial to admission. **Files:** main's control-plane handler behind the preload bridge (CREATE), with its test beside it (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Renderer → Main Process → Control Plane (via Preload Bridge); Spec-021 §Acceptance Criteria (no auth material on `window.desktopBridge`) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-1 (response sanitization is asserted, not assumed — a planted `set-cookie` and a planted bearer header must both be absent from the returned body)
- **T-020r-2-4 — Native handler.** The modules in `bridge/native/` — dialogs, notifications, clipboard, reveal-in-file-explorer, and `openExternal` behind an allowlist. Filesystem paths cross the bridge only as opaque branded `FilePathRef` tokens; dereferencing requires a second round trip through the main process. **Files:** the native handler modules in `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/native/` (EXTEND — `open-dialog.ts`, `clipboard.ts`, `file-intake.ts`, `notification-permission.ts`, `editors/`), each with its test beside it, and one module beside them, with its test, for each handler not yet built (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Preload Bridge Contract (`FilePathRef` is opaque; the renderer cannot dereference a path); Spec-021 §Trust Stance (renderer-supplied input is untrusted) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-1 (no `native` return value carries a raw filesystem path or auth material)
- **T-020r-2-4 EXTEND — the rest of the `native` namespace.** The native handler also answers: `native.showOpenDialog` for Attach — files only, several at once, returning tokens and never a path; `native.getDroppedFileRef(file)` for a file dropped on the composer — the preload reads its path with `webUtils.getPathForFile` and main mints the token, so page code never holds a path; `native.savePastedImage(bytes)` — main writes a pasted picture to a temporary file and mints its token, and that file is deleted once `session.attachmentAdd` has copied the picture into the store; `native.openInEditor(ref, line?)` in the editor Settings names, opening in the system default when that editor is no longer found; `native.openInTerminal({sessionId})`; `native.revealInFileExplorer(ref)`; `native.openExternal(url)` behind the scheme allowlist; `native.copyToClipboard({text, html?})`, which main writes in one `clipboard.write` so a reply's Copy, a partial selection included, carries the markdown and a formatted flavor beside it while a person's own message copies as plain text, and `⌘C` with nothing selected in the message box copies what is selected in the conversation, joined in the order it is read, while with something selected in the box it copies that; `native.listEditors()`, the editors installed on this machine — on Linux, desktop entries matched by desktop id, a JetBrains entry's `StartupWMClass` or `Exec=` program, the id match winning and a `NoDisplay` or `Terminal` entry skipped while another matches, and launcher paths for an install with no entry, with Devin Desktop one entry found under its own and its Windsurf identifiers and reading `Devin Desktop`, and detection on Windows measured there before it ships, `native.openInEditor` there opening with an absolute `Exec=` program or the first launcher path that exists, never a bare name; and `native.getNotificationPermission()`, the operating system's notification permission. `native.openInEditor` and `native.revealInFileExplorer` also serve `Open` on a path the daemon served — a memory path, a hook's file, a definition's or a skill's file. The token comes from the call's own answer: the preload carries `fileRefs` past `settleDaemonCall`, and `DaemonReply`'s served arm holds it beside `value`. **Files:** the native handler and its test (EXTEND), the desktop's preload API type, `PreloadApi`, in `shared/preload-api.ts` and the `PlatformBridge` interface in the renderer's `services/platform/` (EXTEND) **Tests:** `⌘C` with a selection in the conversation and none in the message box writes the selection joined in the order it is read; with a selection in the message box it writes that one. A served `session.memoryRead` reaches the page with a token per memory path, and `Open` hands that token, never the path. **Spec coverage:** Preload Bridge Contract (the `native` namespace; `FilePathRef` is opaque) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-1 (a dropped or pasted file reaches the page only as a token)
- **T-020r-2-5 — Update handler surface, and the `webAuthn` namespace removed.** The update handler (`getState` / `subscribe` / `requestCheck` / `requestDownload` / `requestRestart`, forwarding to the Phase-4 state machine behind a seam). **The shipped partial `webAuthn` namespace is removed:** the preload API type and the `PlatformBridge` interface declare it as `createCredential(options)` / `getAssertion(options)` / `deriveKeyMaterial(input)`, and the desktop app carries no WebAuthn, so this task deletes the namespace, its `createStubBridge` arm, the `PrfInput` type and the `PublicKeyCredentialCreationOptions` / `PublicKeyCredentialRequestOptions` / `PublicKeyCredential` stubs that only typed it — which also removes the remainder `lib: ["dom"]` decision those stubs deferred. **Files:** main's update handler behind the preload bridge (CREATE), the desktop's preload API type, `PreloadApi`, in `shared/preload-api.ts` and the `PlatformBridge` interface in the renderer's `services/platform/` (EXTEND — the `webAuthn` namespace and its stubs removed) **Spec coverage:** Preload Bridge Contract (the update method group, and the remainder namespace rule) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-1 (no member of the update surface carries auth material)
- **T-020r-2-6 — Bridge adversarial-boundary arms.** The intake-validation-parity and mutation-isolation-symmetry arms of §Adversarial-Tampering Boundary: each handler's own validation is asserted at least as strict as the contract schema (a payload the schema rejects must not pass the handler), and structured-clone discipline is asserted in both directions — a renderer-held reference cannot mutate main-process state, and a renderer-supplied object is cloned on the way in. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge/adversarial-boundary.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Trust Stance (every renderer-originated payload is untrusted input); Preload Bridge Contract (the contract schema is the validation floor) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-1 (the parity arm is what keeps a lax handler from re-admitting a shape the contract forbids)
- **T-020r-2-7 — The `window` namespace and the appearance record.** `window.lastUsedWindowId` is the window used last at the last quit, or a new id on a first launch, handed to the preload as a launch argument beside the app facts; the hidden console document opens it first and keys its kept layout by window ids, and the renderer reads each window's id from that window's frame name, so nothing is handed to a preload per window. The bridge's `window` handler answers `window.setAppearance({theme, scheme, textSize, transcriptWidth}, grounds: {light, dark})`, which writes main's appearance record (`<app-user-data>/appearance.json`) first and, only once it is written, tells the platform the window's appearance, ticks the View menu's color scheme, repaints the grounds and pushes the record to the pages — a failed write is refused and changes nothing, a color scheme chosen in a window whose write fails is not applied and that window's banner reads `Could not save the color scheme, so it did not change.`, a View-menu pick whose write fails puts the tick back and is logged, and changes arriving during a write are written once, as the latest — so the next start paints its first frame in the right colors before the page loads, with main's document handler stamping the appearance on the served page's root element.
