Before `ready`, main reads the appearance record and gives each window it builds, and that window's console view, the kept background before the view loads, so the first frame is never the wrong color and nothing shows white; `window.subscribeAppearance(handler)`, which carries the View menu's scheme pick and the record once the page finishes loading; `window.setMinimumSize(windowId, size)`; `window.setDefaultSizes({paneWidths})`, each pane kind's default width, the width a pane's own window with no kept place opens at, handed before the first window opens and again when the text size changes, so main writes no size figure: a window of session views with no kept place fills the display's work area but for one cascade step on every side, and a pane's own window takes its pane's width at the work area's full height; the minimum size recomputed when the text size changes from the floor the renderer sums and never set past the work area of the display the window is on; `window.endSafeStart()`, which the console document calls once `Restore windows` reopened the kept windows, main keeping no window's place until then; `window.subscribeToReopenRequest(handler)`, through which main asks the hidden console document, by window id, to open the window used last again when no window a person sees is open — a Dock click or a second launch; and `window.subscribeToNavigationRequest(handler)`, through which main's deep-link handler, `sidekicks open` and a notification click hand the renderer a parsed request. **Files:** the bridge's `window` handler and its test (CREATE), the desktop's preload API type, `PreloadApi`, in `shared/preload-api.ts` and the `PlatformBridge` interface in the renderer's `services/platform/` (EXTEND) **Spec coverage:** Preload Bridge Contract (the `window` namespace); Spec-021 §Main Process Responsibilities (window management) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-1, I-020-10 (the renderer asks and main acts; no member reads the renderer's layout)
- **T-020r-2-8 — The machine settings members.** `machineSettings.read()`, `machineSettings.write(change)` and `machineSettings.subscribe(handler)`, carrying the service's `daemon.machineSettingsRead`, `daemon.machineSettingsUpdate {change}` (answered with the file as written) and `daemon.machineSettingsSubscribe`. The service is the file's one writer, so a change made from another device reaches it as surely as one made here. One `MachineSettings` schema in `packages/contracts` describes the file for every reader; its keys include the notification switches with the four kinds (`notifications.kinds.waitingOnYou`, `.finished`, `.failed` and `.notifyStep`, each `true` by default and when missing), the screen-reader switch the service reads when it starts a Terminal pane's shell, and voice's `voice.mode` (`hold` or `tap`, default `hold`) and `voice.callVoice`. Main reads the file itself only before the service first answers, for the auto-update preference and `Keep crash reports`; the renderer never reads it, and no `browser.settingsUpdate` exists. **Files:** the bridge's machine-settings handler and its test (CREATE); the desktop's preload API type, `PreloadApi`, in `shared/preload-api.ts` and the `PlatformBridge` interface in the renderer's `services/platform/` (EXTEND — the members, whose signatures refer to the contracts' `MachineSettings`); the `MachineSettings` schema and the `daemon.machineSettingsRead`, `daemon.machineSettingsUpdate` and `daemon.machineSettingsSubscribe` shapes beside it in `packages/contracts` (EXTEND) **Spec coverage:** Preload Bridge Contract **Verifies invariant:** I-020-1 (no credential is ever a machine-settings value)
- **T-020r-2-9 — The keyboard map, the app's facts, and the credential behind the bridge.** `keyboardMap.read()` / `keyboardMap.write()` over main's `<app-user-data>/keyboard-map.json` — only the overridden rows, keyed by act, written whole and atomically, `write()` returning the map as stored — with no subscription, because one renderer drives every window and holds the map. `app.version`, `.platform`, `.arch` and `.locale`, the real facts the General page's version row reads. Every credential behind `daemon.call` and `controlPlane.call` is attached outside the renderer: main presents the daemon session token on its own connection to the daemon, and the control-plane credential is attached by the process that sends the call (T-020r-2-3); neither the page nor the preload ever holds a credential, and none is ever put in an address. **Files:** the bridge's keyboard-map and app handlers and their tests (CREATE), the preload (EXTEND), the desktop's preload API type, `PreloadApi`, in `shared/preload-api.ts` and the `PlatformBridge` interface in the renderer's `services/platform/` (EXTEND) **Spec coverage:** Preload Bridge Contract **Verifies invariant:** I-020-1 (the credential is reachable through no bridge member)
- **T-020r-2-9 EXTEND — the machine's clock and region.** The bridge's `app` facts gain two members, read by main from the machine's own settings on each platform: `regionLocale: string`, the locale the machine writes dates in, and `hourCycle: "h12" | "h23"`, its 12- or 24-hour clock, the region's own cycle where the person changed nothing, because the UI language `app.locale` carries no hour cycle. The source of each is measured on each platform before this task is built — on macOS the region and the 12- or 24-hour switch in System Settings, on Windows its Region settings, on Linux the system locale — and the call that reads it is named from that measurement, never assumed. The figure formatter (`lib/wire/figures.ts`) writes every clock figure in that cycle and every date in that locale. **Files:** the bridge's app handler and its test (EXTEND), the preload (EXTEND), the desktop's preload API type, `PreloadApi`, in `shared/preload-api.ts` and the `PlatformBridge` interface in the renderer's `services/platform/` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/lib/wire/figures.ts` (EXTEND) **Tests:** with the platform set to a 24-hour clock under an English UI language, `app.hourCycle` reads `h23` and a clock figure reads `14:20`. **Spec coverage:** Preload Bridge Contract (the `app` namespace); Spec-021 §Meridian, the design language (item 4: clock figures and dates) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-2-10 — Microphone permission.** The console's session answers a media request with a permission handler: audio alone, asked by the console's own origin, is granted, and video, every other permission and every other origin are refused. On macOS, when `systemPreferences.getMediaAccessStatus('microphone')` reads `not-determined`, the first recording's request waits on `systemPreferences.askForMediaAccess('microphone')`, the system's own prompt, for however long the person takes to answer, with no timer; a refusal, then or later, reaches the page as the capture's refusal, which the strip shows as `Microphone access is off for this app.` with `Open settings`, opening the system's microphone privacy page through `native.openExternal`, whose scheme allowlist admits that page's address on macOS and Windows; where the platform has no such page, `Open settings` is absent. The macOS build carries the microphone usage string (`NSMicrophoneUsageDescription` through `mac.extendInfo`) and the hardened runtime's audio-input entitlement, without which the prompt never appears. **Files:** main's media permission handler and its test (CREATE); `apps/desktop/electron-builder.yml` (EXTEND — the usage string and the entitlement); the native handler (EXTEND — the privacy page's address on the `openExternal` allowlist) **Tests:** the handler grants audio to the console origin and refuses video, another permission and another origin; a request made while the status is `not-determined` stays pending until the prompt's answer resolves, with no timer able to settle it; a `denied` status answers the request with a refusal. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Session Composer (voice waits for the person's answer to the system microphone prompt however long it takes; voice's refusals) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-2-11 — The service's place and move members.** The five members of the `daemon` namespace [Preload Bridge Contract](../architecture/contracts/preload-bridge-contract.md) lists for a Windows computer with WSL 2 — `daemon.listPlaces()`, `daemon.subscribePlaces(handler)`, `daemon.requestMove(place)`, `daemon.cancelMove()` and `daemon.subscribeMove(handler)` — each registered once in the bridge registry and answered by main's service supervisor, which reads the places and runs the move ([Plan-005](./005-local-ipc-and-daemon-control.md) T-005r-4-4 and T-005r-4-14), so the list answers before any service exists; Settings › Providers reads and presses them through the preload. **Files:** the bridge's place and move handler (CREATE), the preload (EXTEND), the desktop's preload API type, `PreloadApi`, in `shared/preload-api.ts` and the `PlatformBridge` interface in the renderer's `services/platform/` (EXTEND — the five members, typed by the preload contract's `ServicePlace` and `ServiceMoveState`, whose shapes Spec-006 gives) **Spec coverage:** Preload Bridge Contract (the `daemon` namespace's places and move); [Spec-006 §Interfaces And Contracts](../specs/006-local-ipc-and-daemon-control.md#interfaces-and-contracts) (the places) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-1 (no place or move member carries auth material)

### Phase 3 — Crash Reporter And Main Startup Composition

**Goal:** Implementation Step 8, plus main's startup composition, its entry point, that orders it and [Plan-025](./025-remote-control.md) Phase 1's start and watch of the service, over main's registry of windows from Phase 1B. Crash reporting initializes first; the service's start and watch run after `app.whenReady()`.

**Preconditions.** Phase 2 merged: its registry, carrying [Plan-025](./025-remote-control.md) Phase 1's `daemon.status` handler (CP-020-1), must exist before the service's watch has a bridge surface to reach. Consumes Plan-025 Phase 1's start and watch of the service and Plan-005's `DaemonHello` readiness signal it handshakes on — the §Preconditions item that names it gates this phase as well as Phase 2. Phase 1B must also have merged: T-020r-3-3's composition-only EXTEND orders the renderer protocol, the application menu, and the locked window constructor that supplement creates.

#### Tasks

- **T-020r-3-1 — Crash reporter.** The crash reporter starts Crashpad with `crashReporter.start({ uploadToServer: false })` while `Keep crash reports` is on, its default, reading that one value straight from the machine settings file because it starts before the service first answers. It is called first among the startup steps in main's entry point — ahead of the single-instance lock, the protocol handler install, every window, and the service's start and watch — with three named steps before it at module top level: Phase 1B's scheme registration (`RendererSchemeRegistration`'s `register()`), which Electron requires before `app.ready` and which touches no network, no file, and no crash-relevant state, so a fault inside it is a startup-order regression the entry point's startup-order test catches, not a report the reporter would have caught; keying the profile to the install, because the crash reports live under the profile folder; and opening main's log, because the reporter records in it a settings file it could not read. Crashpad writes each crash of the main process and every renderer to this machine only: nothing is uploaded, no crash SDK is initialized in any process, and this module scrubs nothing, because a dump is not a report — the service builds the stripped report from it on the machine, symbolicates it there, deletes it with the diagnostic logs, and serves the kept reports through `daemon.crashList` to Remote Control and `sidekicks crash list`. **Files:** the crash reporter in the main process (CREATE), with its test beside it (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Desktop App Implementation Notes §Crash Reporting (Crashpad on the machine only; the `Keep crash reports` switch); Spec-021 §Acceptance Criteria (crash reports carry no tokens, session ids, file paths, or content payloads) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-1 (no crash leaves the machine, so auth material cannot leave by the crash channel)
- **T-020r-3-2 — Crash-report exfiltration arm.** The §Adversarial-Tampering Boundary crash-exfiltration arm, driven through the service's report builder (Plan-005 T-005r-1-14): a crash dump planted with a PASETO token, a DPoP proof, a session id, an absolute file path, and a content payload yields a kept report in which every one is absent — the process type, the app and service versions, the platform and the crashing thread's stack remain, file paths cut to their names and session ids replaced by stable hashes — and a crash with the network recorded sends nothing off the machine. **Files:** the crash-exfiltration test beside the crash reporter (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Desktop App Implementation Notes §Crash Reporting (reports stay on the machine, stripped); Spec-021 §Pitfalls To Avoid (heap-resident secrets reaching an upload endpoint) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-1 (a negative test, so the barrier is proven rather than asserted)
- **T-020r-3-3 — Main startup composition.** Grows `main/index.ts` into the full startup sequence: scheme registration at module top level (Phase 1B, the one step Electron pins before ready), then crash reporter, single-instance lock, protocol handler install, application menu, the hidden console window, whose document opens the window used last (T-020p-1B-5), the start and watch of the service ([Plan-025](./025-remote-control.md) Phase 1). With `--banner`, main builds no window, takes the single-instance lock or exits, and hands to the notifications service's banner mode; a `second-instance` launch meanwhile turns it into the windowed app. A quit sends `daemon.flush` and exits when the flush answers or after 10 seconds, whichever comes first, on every platform, leaving the service, every run and every shell running; the sidecar drains when the service stops ([Plan-001](./001-session-core.md) CP-001-1), and nothing in main's quit reaches it. Window construction and retention follow ADR-023's reachability mechanism so the window is not collected mid-session. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/src/main/index.test.ts` (CREATE) **Tests:** a quit whose flush never answers exits at 10 seconds, and one whose flush answers exits at once. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Main Process Responsibilities (single-instance lock; startup ordering; protocol registration); Spec-021 §Acceptance Criteria (the main-process window-retention bullet, whose mechanism ADR-023 supplies) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-2 (every window this sequence creates carries the locked `webPreferences` block and loads over the custom protocol)
- **T-020r-3-3 EXTEND — composing the Phase 1B renderer-load legs.** The privileged scheme registration, the bundle handler, the hidden console window's load, the locked window constructor, and the application menu ship in the partial at T-020p-1B-1 / T-020p-1B-2, because none of them consumes a daemon contract. This composition **inherits** them and adds nothing to them: the full order is scheme registration (module top level, ahead of every `whenReady()` consumer — the one exception T-020r-3-1's crash-first rule names) → crash reporter → single-instance lock → `installRendererProtocol` → application menu → the hidden console window (its document opens the window used last) → the service's start and watch, and `index.test.ts` asserts that the registration still precedes ready and the handler still precedes the hidden console window's load after the Phase-3 growth — a diff that reorders either, or re-authors `protocol.ts` / `factory.ts` / `menu.ts` here, is a review rejection. Every further window is built by main's window-open handler (T-020p-1B-5). **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts` (EXTEND) and `index.test.ts` (EXTEND) only. **Verifies invariant:** I-020-6 (the order survives composition), I-020-7 (the locked constructor is the one Phase 1B shipped).

### Phase 4 — Auto-Updater And Deep-Link Handler

**Goal:** Implementation Steps 7 and 9 — the two surfaces that mutate the installed application or admit an external URL.

**Preconditions.** Phase 3 must have merged. Main relays the service's own update and restore over [Plan-025](./025-remote-control.md) Phase 1's connection to the service. Consumes Plan-025's control-plane client. The deep-link tasks build with the phase; T-020r-4-1 and T-020r-4-2, the update path, are built in the Release group ([cross-plan-dependencies.md §Platform order](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#platform-order)).

#### Tasks

- **T-020r-4-1 — Auto-update state machine.** `updater.ts` — `electron-updater` 6.8.10 with its stock GitHub provider (T-020r-4-2), checking at startup and every 4 hours only while `Check for updates automatically` is on (main reads the switch from the machine settings file and follows its changes), and on an explicit request, with `autoDownload` and `autoInstallOnAppQuit` off: a download starts on the person's press, is a blockmap delta where the platform has one (NSIS, the macOS zip once a previous update is cached, and AppImage), and Squirrel.Mac is handed an update only at `Restart to finish`. The state `update.getState()` and `update.subscribe()` carry holds each step — `Checked`, the download's progress, `Checking the signature…`, `Ready to install`, `Restart to finish` — a found update with its `What changed`, an update a previous run staged and never applied, a failed check (`The update could not be verified and was not installed.`), an updater that cannot be reached (`Could not check for updates.`, `Could not download the update.`, `Could not restart to install the update.` and `Could not read the update status.`), and an install that cannot update itself; `update.requestCheck()`, `update.requestDownload()` and `update.requestRestart()` drive it. **The install kind** comes from electron-builder's `resources/package-type` (`deb`, `rpm`), `APPIMAGE` and the platform's own markers: a `.deb` or `.rpm` install never runs the updater's own Debian or RPM installer, which would bypass the repository's signature, and names the package manager's own command instead — `This install cannot update itself. Run sudo apt update && sudo apt upgrade to get the newest version.`, or `sudo dnf upgrade` on an RPM install — and a Mac copy running outside a writable folder reads `This install cannot update itself. Move the app to Applications, then check again.` **The service's own update and restore** are main's to relay: `daemon.requestUpdate()`, `daemon.cancelUpdate()` and `daemon.subscribeUpdate(handler)` over the service's self-update path (Plan-005 T-005r-3-6, which also owns the release manifest's schema), which waits for running work to finish and never stops work to update: main relays the steps, the cancel window (`Cancel` until the old service is asked to stop) and the settled line, naming both versions and the reason where the update rolled back, as that path reports them, and `daemon.requestRestore(backupId)`, which replaces the service's data with a backup's. After relaunch main handshakes with the service through `daemon.hello`; a completed handshake with the service outside the accepted range makes the console read-only per Spec-021 §Daemon Supervision Lifecycle. The app does not roll itself back; a bad release is fixed by the next one. **Files:** the updater in the main process (CREATE), with its test beside it (CREATE), the bridge's update handler (EXTEND — `update.requestDownload()`), the desktop's preload API type, `PreloadApi`, in `shared/preload-api.ts` and the `PlatformBridge` interface in the renderer's `services/platform/` (EXTEND — `update.requestDownload()` and the service's update and restore members) **Tests:** with `Check for updates automatically` off, neither the startup check nor the 4-hour check runs, and an explicit request still checks. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Auto-Update Flow (staged apply; the handshake after the relaunch); Desktop App Implementation Notes §Auto-Updater **Verifies invariant:** I-020-1 (updater state persisted to disk, `updater-state.json`, carries no auth material)
- **T-020r-4-2 — The update checks.** Leg 1, at check time: main reads the last version seen from `updater-state.json` and refuses a release whose version is not above it, with `allowDowngrade` off; the cycle is skipped and logged, and nothing from that release reaches a download. Leg 2, on download: `electron-updater`'s stock GitHub provider checks the downloaded file's SHA-512 against the release's update file, and on a Windows build signed through a certificate also checks the publisher (`verifyUpdateCodeSignature`); on macOS Squirrel.Mac takes the update only when it meets the running app's designated requirement. A mismatch discards the artifact, logs, and refuses the update. Both legs are fail-closed with no state mutation on refusal. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/services/updater.ts` (EXTEND), with its test beside it (EXTEND) **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Main Process ↔ Auto-Update Feed (the SHA-512 check and the operating system's code signature); Spec-021 §Auto-Update Flow (the check precedes staging; a failed check discards, logs, and re-attempts next cycle)
- **T-020r-4-3 — Deep-link handler.** `deep-link.ts` — `app.setAsDefaultProtocolClient('sidekicks')`; `open-url` on macOS and `second-instance` on Windows / Linux. URL parse happens in the main process through the one `sidekicks://` parser `packages/contracts` exports — the same form `Copy link` copies and `sidekicks open` sends — which accepts `session/<id>` and `workflow-run/<runId>`, the latter opening the run's page; the renderer receives only the parsed request, through `window.subscribeToNavigationRequest`, and places it: the view already showing that session is brought forward, otherwise the focused view of the window used last switches to it, and with no window open a window opens on it. A notification click and a line in the bell's list arrive the same way. A link to a session this install's list does not hold waits until the session list has loaded and then answers in one line, `That session is not here.`; a link that launches the app is held through the cold launch and takes the same path once the window is up. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/services/deep-link.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/src/main/services/deep-link.test.ts` (CREATE) **Tests:** a link to an unknown id answers only after the session list resolves, and a link received before the first window exists is acted on once it is. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Main Process Responsibilities (main-process-only URL parsing) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-4-4 — Deep-link parser adversarial arms.** The §Adversarial-Tampering Boundary canonicalization-round-trip and empty-segment arms, on the plan's only untrusted-input parser: a `sidekicks://` URL must survive parse → normalize → re-serialize with no action or argument reinterpretation, and `sidekicks://session/` (empty id) plus `sidekicks://session//<id>` (empty leading segment) must both be **rejected at the parser boundary**, never coerced into a valid action. Percent-encoded separators and trailing separators are covered by the same arm. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/main/services/deep-link.parser-boundary.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Main Process Responsibilities (the `sidekicks://<action>/<...args>` grammar the parser must hold exactly); Spec-021 §Trust Stance (an inbound URL is untrusted input regardless of which OS event delivered it) **Verifies invariant:** none

### Phase 5 — Renderer Layout, Router, And Composer

**Goal:** Implementation Step 10 as reduced by §Renderer — `App.tsx` routing plus the `layout/` chrome and the `features/composer/` composer. This phase authors **no feature-view body**; each Signature Feature view is created by its owning plan in that plan's own subtree and mounted here.

**Precondition:** Phase 4's deep-link tasks merged; Plan-005 Phase 2D merged — the router's parameterized subscriptions need the request channel §Preconditions names, and resume by cursor on the batched frame Plan-005 Phase 2D builds. Single tasks also wait on the verbs they call: T-020r-5-2's voice and running-commands list on [Plan-003](./003-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md) T3.32 and T3.28, T-020r-5-5 on [Plan-001](./001-session-core.md) T6.15, T-020r-5-8 on Plan-003 T3.31, T-020r-5-10 on Plan-003 T3.29, T3.41 and T3.43 and on [Plan-006](./006-repo-attachment-and-workspace-binding.md) T3.10, T-020r-5-14 on Plan-006 T3.1 and T3.9, T-020r-5-15 on Plan-001 T6.7, and T-020r-5-17 on [Plan-016](./016-notifications-and-attention-model.md) T3.1, Plan-001 T6.2 and [Plan-014](./014-workflow-authoring-and-execution.md) T1.5 and T5.12.

T-020r-5-1 adopts the console Phase 1C built, by mount-and-move.

#### Tasks

- **T-020r-5-1 — Router and layout chrome.** `App.tsx` grows from the partial's placeholder into the top-level router plus layout; `layout/` holds the chrome. Each Signature Feature view is mounted as a route against the owning plan's own subtree — this task creates a mount point and a route, never a view body. Where an owning plan's view has not landed, its route draws only the chrome this plan owns around an empty mount point — no placeholder, no status word, no sample data and no local reimplementation — and the owner's body fills that mount point in the change that lands it. The Workflows Builder's address, `#/workflows/builder[/<definitionId>]`, and the removal of the `workflow-builder` pane kind from `routing/panes/kinds.ts`, `routing/panes/address.ts` and `components/PaneFrame/PaneFrame.tsx` land together in [Plan-014](./014-workflow-authoring-and-execution.md) T5.4, which owns those routing changes; this task registers no pane kind for a workflow screen. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/app/App.tsx` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/layout/AppShell/AppShell.tsx` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/layout/AppShell/AppShell.test.tsx` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Signature Feature Composition Sketches (each sketch names its owning plan; this plan composes rather than implements); Spec-021 §Renderer Responsibilities (the renderer is a thin projection over the bridge) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-5 (the layout imports nothing under `src/main/` or `src/preload/` and no Node built-in)
- **T-020r-5-1 EXTEND — adopting the console.** By the time this task dispatches, the console's folders §Target Areas Renderer registers are in-tree from Phase 1C and are already the app frame `App.tsx` mounts. This task therefore **adopts rather than re-authors**: the router is the console's route table (the five rail destinations — Sessions, Sidekicks, Skills, Workflows, Settings, in that order per [ADR-029](../decisions/029-five-rail-destinations.md) — the session views, and the settings pages), the layout chrome is the console's icon rail plus the row of session views, and `AppShell` (`layout/AppShell/`) becomes the thin host that mounts the console and the plan-owned subtrees into it — a diff that re-implements a console surface in `AppShell`, or moves a console file to re-own it, is a review rejection. Where an owning plan's body has not landed, the console draws only its own chrome around that body's empty mount point — no placeholder, no status word, no sample data and no local reimplementation — and the body fills the mount point in the change that lands it. **The rail's own marks.** The Sidekicks glyph is the robot the composer's agents badge draws, the Skills glyph an open book — a stroked outline at the rail's weight in the rail's glyph box, never the robot — and the Workflows glyph the `workflow` pane glyph. The Settings button carries one small dot in a token color, never the attention color and with no count, while `update.subscribe` reports an update staged or ready to install (T-020r-4-1's `staged` member and its ready arm); the dot is hidden from assistive technology with the fact in the button's accessible name, and goes when the update is applied. **Files:** the same, with `AppShell.tsx` reduced to the host. **Tests:** `structure:layering` runs against the adopted tree (no import into a plan-owned subtree except the absorbed components), review checks the single mount point — every pane opens through the pane registry's one mount point, which re-focuses the pane an address already has rather than opening a second, and no surface mounts a pane body of its own — and every surface whose wire has landed reads it live; an update reported staged draws the Settings dot and names it in the button's accessible name, and the dot goes when the report clears.
- **T-020r-5-2 — Session composer.** `features/composer/` — the composer's pinned home, in this plan's own folder and never inside a feature-view tree owned by Plan-010 or Plan-013. The composer is **authored at this pinned home by Phase 1C** (T-020p-1C-3) and carries what [Spec-021 §Signature Feature Composition Sketches](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#signature-feature-composition-sketches) specifies: one card addressing the session's own agent — and, where an `@` at the head of the draft names one, another session — holding the working line, the command list, the attachment row, the draft line with send at its end, and the toolbar's six value controls — model, reasoning effort, output speed, the build-or-plan mode control, the permission level and the tool servers — with the context ring beside them. There is no send router, no target picker and no posture control anywhere on it, and the console never carries a second composer. This task is its **live-wiring leg**: it wires the send path's run-queue create and steer-intervention calls live at that home and re-authors nothing. It also wires the draft to the daemon: `session.draftUpdate` saves it one second after typing pauses and at once when the session is left or the window closes, `session.read` restores it on a switch, a restart and another device, and Send clears it; no draft is written to window storage. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/features/composer/` (EXTEND — created by T-020p-1C-3), the composer's test beside it (CREATE) **Tests:** typing then pausing for one second sends one `session.draftUpdate`, leaving the session before the second is up sends it at once, and Send clears the stored draft. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Renderer Responsibilities (composition surfaces call the bridge; they hold no authority); Spec-021 §Signature Feature Composition Sketches (the composer is chrome shared across views, not a view body) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-5 (the composer reaches every daemon and control-plane surface only through the `PlatformBridge` its provider hands it, which is what makes the never-import-a-transport-client-directly rule mechanically true rather than a convention)
- **T-020r-5-2 EXTEND — the console-parity composer affordances.** The same composer additionally hosts the Session Composer surface under [Spec-021 §Signature Feature Composition Sketches](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#signature-feature-composition-sketches): the command list it **authors** (subscribing to `session.providerCommandsSubscribe` per CP-020-4 and holding only its latest emission), and the composer-area **mount points** for the context ring and the user-triggered compaction popover — both authored by Plan-010 in the composer feature (the ring and its compaction popover in `features/composer/context-ring/`), so the composer mounts them and never authors them. **One rule binds the list.** Each emission of `session.providerCommandsSubscribe` is the whole list of the session's live provider process, carrying the `(driverName, providerAccountId)` it was read under: the composer **replaces** its list with each emission and never merges two, since a merged list is a list whose entries have lost the routing invariant that says which binding may be offered them. **The output-speed control is authored here**, unlike those two, because the Session Composer sub-sketch is this plan's own and the control composes only surfaces already registered elsewhere: it reads the session's own agent's `outputSpeed` from `agent.list`, offers the choices published for the session's model — that model's `outputSpeedLevels` on the `driver.listModels` read where its provider publishes them per model, else the driver's `outputSpeedLevels` on the `driver.listCapabilities` read the other affordances already take — and dispatches `agent.configUpdate` — no new verb, no new subtree, and no obligation minted, the members and the vocabulary being Plan-013's and Plan-003's to define. It shows one value as a word, `Fast` or `Standard`, with no suffix, bracket or mark — `Standard` for the provider's standard level (`off` on Claude Code, `default` on Codex) and `Fast` for the other level the model lists — and reads `Standard` while the provider reports that fast output is not on for a turn that asked for it (`observedOutputSpeed`), until a report says it is on; that report's `session.notice` of kind `fast_output_unavailable` draws one flow row in the words [Spec-021 §Session Composer](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#session-composer--the-chrome-every-session-view-carries--plan-020-desktop-app-and-renderer) holds, a transcript row and so Plan-010 T4.21's. It is drawn on every session and follows the model control: where the chosen model has no fast output it reads `Standard`, lightly grayed, reported as disabled to assistive technology, answers no press, and carries the hover title `<model> has no fast output.`, naming the model, with the same sentence as one small faint line beside the chips that appears and goes as the chip grays and lights and folds away with the chips on a card too narrow to hold them. **A switch that fails leaves the session where it was.** When a provider or account switch cannot be applied, the daemon records `agent.provider_binding_change_failed` — a switch that lands records `agent.provider_binding_changed` — the session stays on its previous binding, and the transcript gains one system message: `Switch to Codex failed · <reason>`, or, for an account whose login expired, `Switch to account <name> failed · login expired · Sign in again`, with `Sign in again` pressable there and nowhere else. **What the list dispatches, and on whose terms.** A console word is intercepted in the client and never forwarded as text: most press a console control, and the few that do work of their own reach the daemon on the provider's own verb ([Spec-015 §Command interception — the reserved `/` prefix (C-18)](../specs/015-workflow-authoring-and-execution.md#command-interception--the-reserved--prefix-c-18)). Three cases are sent as typed instead, pressing nothing, with the provider's answer the only one shown: a console word whose control has nothing to act on in this session, such as `/fork` before anything is said or `/background` with no command waiting; a console word given an argument its control cannot take, such as `/effort turbo` or `/workflow` with an unknown verb; and a console word that cannot run right now, such as `/compact` while a turn is running. The console words the list carries are the thirty-four in [Spec-021 §Session Composer](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#session-composer--the-chrome-every-session-view-carries--plan-020-desktop-app-and-renderer), each with its caught spellings, the control it presses and the argument it takes; once a word that takes a name and a space are typed, a second level offers exactly the names that word's own control offers, read from where the control reads them, filtering as typed, Tab and Enter completing one, and a word whose control offers nothing shows that control's own empty line; `/review` typed with no target completes its three target words. An entry the provider's own enumeration carries is forwarded verbatim through the binding it was read under, which is the user-chosen producer class [Spec-004 §The provider command and skill surface](../specs/004-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md#the-provider-command-and-skill-surface) admits on the `driver_command` arm: it claims no effect, so neither the presence check nor the typed-evidence contract applies to it, and a driver that synthesized a success for such a frame would commit exactly the error the tripwire exists to catch. Only the driver-composed entry — the compaction command, reached through the compaction popover's own wire call rather than through this list — carries both guards. A word no row matches closes the list rather than refusing the word, because that word goes to the provider as typed; there is no literal-slash escape and no unknown-command row. **The card's other states.** The card carries a restart's recovery question with its two named choices ([Spec-013 §Fallback Behavior](../specs/013-persistence-and-recovery.md#fallback-behavior)), and it arms the composer for a cloud task: `Next message runs in <provider>'s cloud` with `Cancel`, and on Codex `Cloud environment` — picking from the labels this account's tasks already name or taking one typed, with the label kept for the project preselected (the labels and the per-project store are [Plan-003](./003-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md) T3.31's) — and `Attempts`, 1 to 4. **The working line's running commands.** While any command runs, the working line's action words are pressable and open, in place above the working line, one entry per running command from `command.list` in the order they started — its name, a live timer and its own `Stop` (`command.stop`) — with a live tail of its output under it, eight lines of its own monospace line height tall, scrolling under the pointer with auto-follow pausing while scrolled up and resuming at the bottom, and a small fold. The list never runs under the session header: it stops short of it and scrolls inside itself when its entries and their tails need more room. A press on the name scrolls the transcript to that command's row and closes the list; one last row, `Stop all commands`, stops every running command in the session and no agent; the list goes when the last command ends. The tail is a window onto the row's output, never a second copy. While a compaction runs, the working line reads `Compacting…` in place of its verb, appearing for the duration on a session with no live turn, and it stays until the compaction is done (the `usage.context_compacted` row) or the provider reports it failed; a bare acknowledgment never clears it, and a failed compaction lands no row and returns the line to its verb. One the person asked for that the provider attempts and fails writes `Compaction didn't run — the conversation is too short to compact`, or the provider's other reason in words, on the one line beside the message box, cleared by the next keystroke. **Voice.** `/voice` is the composer's, the same on both providers and naming neither, over the `voice.*` methods [Plan-003](./003-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md) T3.32 builds. `/voice` turns it on in the session it is typed in, moving it from any other session, which reads `Voice ended`; `/voice` again or `/voice off` turns it off, landing `Voice is already off` when it is off; `/voice hold` and `/voice tap` pick the mode, written to `voice.mode` through `machineSettings.write`, and turn voice on in the session they are typed in; in the session where voice is already on they change the mode with no row. The service holds which session voice is on in. Turning it on lands one quiet row, `Voice started · hold Space to talk` or `Voice started · tap Space to talk`, and turning it off `Voice ended`. While it is on, that session's strip reads `Voice on`, and `Listening…`, beside a mark that swells with the microphone's level, takes its place while the microphone is open; the empty draft's placeholder reads `Hold Space to talk` or `Tap Space to talk`, and `Tap Space to stop` while a tap has the microphone open. Only that session's Space talks: held, the key's repeat takes back the spaces it typed and records until it is let go or the window loses focus; tapped with the draft empty, it opens the microphone until the next tap; on a touch screen, holding the dimmed Send circle is the hold. On Claude Code a recording is dictation: the window captures with echo cancellation, noise suppression and gain control, cuts it to 16 kHz 16-bit mono, and streams the frames through `voice.dictationStart`, `voice.dictationWrite` and `voice.dictationStop`, and the words `voice.dictationSubscribe` returns appear at the caret, dimmed until the service settles them; letting go leaves them in the draft, unsent, and a tap's second press sends the draft when it holds at least three words. On Codex it is Codex's own spoken call with the window as its WebRTC peer, through `voice.callStart`, `voice.callStop` and `voice.callSubscribe`: capture runs through an AudioWorklet at 48 kHz into a media-stream destination whose track the call carries, so the call hears nothing while Space is held, then the whole kept request at the release or on focus loss, at 1.5× with its pitch kept and its pauses cut (the stretch is [Plan-013](./013-multi-agent-orchestration.md)'s D-013-19), a hold keeping at most two minutes before it passes in as if let go; a tap's open microphone goes in live; and what the call hears of the person shows dimmed in the composer. Escape while recording, or while the last words are still arriving, drops the recording and puts the draft back as it was, interrupting nothing; on a Codex hold it drops what the hold has kept. Voice's refusals are the strip's one-line refusal: `Voice isn't available on <account>.`, `Microphone access is off for this app.` with `Open settings` (T-020r-2-10), `No sound reached the microphone. Check the input device.`, `No speech heard.`, `Dictation couldn't be sent.` with `Try again`, and `Dictation couldn't sign in to <account>.` with `Sign in again`. `/voice settings` lists the voices `voice.voiceList` returns in the command list, the one in use marked — the person's pick, or Codex's default until they pick one — writes the pick to `voice.callVoice` through `machineSettings.write`, and lands one quiet row, `Voice set to <voice> · from the next call`. The mark beside `Listening…` reads one level every 50 milliseconds from the samples' root mean square in decibels, `x = clamp((dB + 70) / 60, 0, 1)`, drawn at `min(1, 1.4 · (max(0, x − 0.2) / 0.8)^0.7)`. On Codex a hold made while an earlier one is still passing in goes in after it, a tap's closing press closes the microphone only once the audio still kept has gone out, there is no `/voice mute`, and a dropped call's `Try again` starts a new call and plays in the hold's request the drop cut off. **The toolbar's lists, live.** The model control's list draws its four states — skeleton rows while the catalog is read, the rows, one failure line with `Try again` that reads the catalog afresh, and, where the provider offers nothing, one line above `Manage in Settings`, which opens Settings › Providers; a session on a model the catalog no longer offers keeps it, the chip reading `<model> no longer offered` over the current catalog's rows, and its turn still sends. The two provider headings follow `provider.list` live ([Plan-023](./023-provider-accounts-and-credential-homes.md) builds it), so a flip of a provider's `Available for new sessions` switch changes every open composer's rows at once with no restart. The tool-servers control counts only the servers switched on for the session and opens the list `session.mcpServerList` returns: the working servers first under their names alone, a faint rule, then the rest dimmed with one faint word, `Needs sign-in` or `Failed`, its whole reason in the word's hover title and the failed ones last, a switch on every row over `session.mcpServerUpdate`, `Attach a resource…` over `session.mcpResourceList`, and one `Manage in Settings` link under the rows, a session with no servers drawing one line saying so above the link ([Plan-022](./022-mcp-server-configuration-and-governance.md) builds the three). **The line beside the message box** carries the console's own answers and nothing else, cleared by the next keystroke: a compaction asked for that did not run, `/output-style` typed with no style and `/advisor` with no model naming the session's choice and the choices, and the provider's own answer word for word after `/output-style <style>` or `/advisor <model>`, with no row added. On Claude Code, `/config` with the model key, the permission-mode key or the auto-compact key presses the model chip, the level chip or the slider, the three spellings read from `/config --help` once when built; every other `/config` key, and `/config` on Codex, goes as typed. **The draft's keys and failures.** While the command list is open Tab completes the highlighted row, the arrows wrap, Enter runs or completes it and Escape closes it, and with it closed Tab and Shift+Tab traverse the screen; the command and mention lists close on blur, on a press outside them and when a card opens over the box, completing and sending nothing. A send that fails puts the words, the staged files and the held notes back in the draft and lands `Send failed · <cause>`, and where the write back into the draft itself fails that row says so. A file the session cannot take — its provider takes no attachments, or a card above the box is waiting — is refused in the attachment row's one-line refusal naming that cause, whether it came by the picker, a paste or a drop. **Budget:** while dictating, the window's capture costs at most 1% of one core and 80 MiB; while a Codex call runs, its audio costs the window at most 1.5% of one core and 80 MiB, and a held request playing in adds at most 1.5% more and holds at most 6.4 MB; with voice off, nothing is held — each measured before the task and after. **Files:** the same two files, EXTENDed. **Tests:** a list emitted under one binding is not offered once an emission under another binding arrives — asserted on the offered set after the second emission, so a composer that merged the two lists fails; and the composer issues no compaction call of its own on any usage threshold. Selecting a console word presses that word's own control and composes no message, a caught spelling presses the same control as its word, and selecting a provider word is forwarded verbatim through the binding it was read under and inserts nothing into the draft — asserted on the draft's value and the call counts together; each of the three sent-as-typed cases sends the text and presses nothing. The working line's `Compacting…` stays through an acknowledged compaction call that brings neither a `usage.context_compacted` row nor a reported failure; the row clears it, and a reported failure clears it with no row added. The speed chip on a model with no fast output reads `Standard`, answers a press with nothing, and carries `<model> has no fast output.`; a report that fast output is not on for a turn that asked for it reads `Standard`. `Stop all commands` stops each running command and no agent; with more running commands than fit above the working line, the list's top edge sits below the session header's bottom edge and the list scrolls inside itself. Space in a session where voice is off types a space; Escape during a recording restores the draft as it was before the recording and interrupts no turn; on a Codex hold, the call's track carries silence until the release; a tap's second press sends a draft of three words and keeps one of two. The model chip on a model the catalog dropped reads `no longer offered` and its send still goes; a `provider.list` emission turning a provider off takes its heading out of an open composer's model list with no reload; with the command list closed, Tab moves focus out of the box and inserts nothing; `/config` with the model key presses the model chip and sends nothing, and an unknown key goes as typed; and a failed send whose write back into the draft fails says so in its row.
- **T-020r-5-3 — Several sessions on screen.** The renderer's session-view registry places a session: in this window's focused view, in a view beside it (`Open beside`, `⌘`-click), or in a window of its own (`Open in new window`, or a view's title dragged into another window or onto the desktop, T-020r-5-13), and the only thing a window refuses is a second view of a session already on screen — asking for it brings that view forward. Each destination — Sidekicks, Skills, Workflows, Settings — shows in one window at a time the same way: asking for it again, from the rail, the palette, a menu or an address, brings the window that shows it forward and focuses it, and nothing draws a second copy. A row press on a session on screen elsewhere brings that view forward and switches nothing, and the sessions list marks each session drawn in another view `On screen in another view`. `⌥⌘←` and `⌥⌘→` (`Focus session on the left`, `Focus session on the right`) move focus between views; `⌘W` and the view header's `Close session view` close the focused session view, and `⌘W` closes the window when it is the only view; `New window` on its binding (`⌘⇧N` until rebound), in the File menu and in the palette opens a window through the renderer's `window.open`. A `sidekicks://` address, `sidekicks open`, a notification click and a bell line go to the session's view where it is on screen and otherwise to the focused view of the window used last. Each window document's title follows its focused view: `<session title> — <project>`, the session named as the session list names it and the project by the `name` on the `repo.projectList` entry whose `repoMountId` the session's `session.list` entry carries, or `<session title> — Chat` for a chat. Each window's frame work runs on that window's own `requestAnimationFrame` (the clock's frame scheduling takes the window it draws in, never the hidden document's global), so a minimized window pauses only itself; closing a window unmounts it and releases its listeners and the store, subscriptions and decoded pictures of any session no other window shows: the renderer keeps, per session, the set of windows showing it, keyed by the window id, and disposes the session's apply queue, subscriptions and picture entries when that set empties, moving its prepared rows into the recent-sessions cache (no subscription; bounded by its memory share, at most twelve sessions, and no session over a quarter of the share; least recently shown leaving first), from which a reopen draws at once while a fresh subscription catches up from the rows' last cursor. A release that let go of decoded pictures calls the bridge's `app.freeUnusedMemory()` — the preload's call of Electron's `webFrame.clearCache()` — once for every release in the same idle period, never on a timer, and nothing forces a garbage collection (Spec-021 §Main Process Responsibilities records the measurement). A development build gains the on-demand memory report [Spec-021 §Fixtures](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#fixtures) describes beside its developer-tools rule. The renderer keeps every window's layout — which views it holds, their order and widths, which had focus, the rail destination, an open palette or flyout — in renderer-local storage keyed by the window id main's window-place file shares, so a restart brings back every window with its views and every pane's own window; closing every window stops no session. **Budget:** four windows showing eight sessions, two of them streaming a turn, against one window showing one session, for resident memory, processor time while idle and while streaming, and a view's switch time — measured before the task and after; the renderer heap each further window adds at rest is recorded in the endurance ledger as Spec-021 §Budgets' per-window figure. On a safe start — the console document's root carries `data-safe-start` — the renderer opens one window on the sessions list, without the kept layout, showing `The app restarted after repeated problems and did not reopen its windows.` and `Restore windows`, which reopens every window from the kept layout ([Desktop App Implementation Notes §Repeated renderer crashes](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#repeated-renderer-crashes)). **Files:** the sessions feature's view registry and the renderer's window client (CREATE), the palette and keyboard rows and the preload's `app` part with `PreloadApi` (EXTEND — `freeUnusedMemory`), and their tests **Tests:** several sessions released in one idle period, their pictures among them, call `app.freeUnusedMemory()` once, and a release with no pictures calls it never; a load marked as a safe start opens one window on the sessions list with the line, and `Restore windows` reopens the kept layout; in the Electron tier, asking for Settings from a second window while the first shows it focuses the first window and opens no second Settings view; in the endurance tier, opening and closing a window fifty times returns the at-rest renderer heap to its starting level beyond what the recent-sessions cache holds within its share, and a heap snapshot holds no closed window's document, listener or session store; a session reopened from the recent-sessions cache draws its rows before the daemon answers and then applies only what arrived after their cursor, and the cache never exceeds its memory share or twelve sessions, and never takes a session larger than a quarter of the share. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian) (the row of session views; windows); Spec-021 §Main Process Responsibilities (window management) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-10
- **T-020r-5-3 EXTEND — the empty view's logo mark.** A session view that holds no session — a new window's one view, the view beside the sessions list after Escape there, on a first run and at a safe start — draws the app's logo mark alone in the middle of its body, and unmounts it the moment a session opens in the view. The mark is `LogoMark`, the renderer's one copy of the app logo's outline: the one-color outline in `apps/desktop/resources/tray.svg`, moved into the component as its inline drawing, since the menu-bar icon draws its own faces and nothing else reads that file. It strokes in the hairline color `edge`, is sized from the type scale, carries `aria-hidden` with no role, title, focus or pointer events, and is kept out of the glyph set an agent's `icon` picks from, so no agent can carry the logo. Nothing else mounts it. **Provides:** `LogoMark` in `components/LogoMark/`; the empty view drawing it. **Consumes:** T-020r-5-3's session-view registry, which says a view holds no session; the `edge` token in `styles/palette.ts`. **Files:** `components/LogoMark/LogoMark.tsx` and `LogoMark.css` (CREATE), the session view's body in `features/sessions/` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/resources/tray.svg` (DELETE), `apps/desktop/AGENTS.md`'s line on `resources/` (EXTEND), and their tests **Tests:** browser tier: a new window's empty view draws the mark and its accessibility tree holds nothing of it; opening a session in that view removes the mark; a view that holds a session never draws it. **Spec coverage:** [Spec-021 §The session screen](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#the-session-screen) (a view with no session) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-5-4 — The inspector's Memory and Hooks, and code colored once.** The inspector gains `Memory` — one row per memory file or folder the provider reported for the session, each with `Open`, under the `Auto memory` switch — read through `session.memoryRead` and switched through `session.autoMemoryUpdate`, and `Hooks` — Codex's `hooks/list {cwds}` with its errors and warnings, and the Claude Code hook files — read through `session.hookList`; `Open` goes through `native.openInEditor` and `native.revealInFileExplorer` (T-020r-2-4 EXTEND). Code in the transcript, the file pane and the definitions paints the spans `highlight.read` returns. The daemon half — the session methods over each provider's own verbs, which Plan-003 owns, and `highlight.read`, which colors code once in the daemon and keeps and sends the color spans packed (offset, length and class, 0.35 to 0.46 bytes per source byte), with its own span cache at 1/2048 of physical memory — is built in `packages/runtime-daemon` with this task. **Files:** the inspector's Memory and Hooks sections (CREATE), the code views' span painter (EXTEND), the daemon's session memory and hook reads and its highlighter (CREATE), and their tests **Spec coverage:** Desktop App Implementation Notes §Console Libraries (one colorer, in the daemon) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-5-5 — Session groups, links and tags on screen.** Over [Plan-001](./001-session-core.md) T6.15's verbs, the sessions list draws a project's session groups at the top of its list, above its loose sessions, as foldable rows each reading its name and count (`auth work · 3`), folding on its own row as a project header does and carrying a project header's rolled-up status dot while folded; a pinned session stays at its group's top, and an archived one keeps its group while its row goes to `Archived`. A group with no live session left hides on its own and comes back when one of its sessions is unarchived. A project session's one actions list carries `Move to group` after `Pin to top` and before `Mute notifications`, opening the project's groups, a rule and `New group…` (`session.groupMove`); `New group…` puts a name field in place where the group's row will be, Enter making the group with this session in it (`session.groupCreate`) and Escape canceling, so no group is ever empty and the project header carries no `New group`. Dragging a session onto a group's row moves it in, and dragging it into the project's loose sessions takes it out; dragging a chat's row onto a project group opens the convert card in place above the working line with that project's path filled in. A group's row renames in place on a double-click (`session.groupRename`), and its actions list is `Rename group…`, `New session in group` (`groupId` on `session.create`) and `Ungroup` (`session.groupUngroup`), which sends its sessions back loose and archives or closes nothing; the list has no multi-select. The list's search keeps a whole group in view when its name matches. A row whose session is trading messages with another session while nobody types in either reads `talking to <name> · <count>` in its second line's slot on both sessions' rows, from the exchange line [Plan-013](./013-multi-agent-orchestration.md) T2.9 puts on `session.list`, with the age still at its right, no cost figure and no attention count, and it goes back to its branch or document count when the exchange ends. The inspector carries `Related` right after Identity, over `session.relatedList`: one line per linked session, highest relevance first, reading the relationship in words (`started by planner`, `copy of builder`) and pressing through to that session as a list row does; `Link a session…` at its foot opens the `@` session picker and adds a `related` link (`session.linkAdd`), a `related` row's actions list carries `Unlink` (`session.linkRemove`), a link the daemon wrote from an event carries none, and with no links the section reads `No related sessions yet` with `Link a session…`. Identity carries a `Tags` line: each tag a chip with a remove mark (`session.tagRemove`), and an `Add tag` field suggesting the tags in use (`session.tagList`) that adds one (`session.tagAdd`), a refused tag answered in the field's one-line refusal. **Files:** the sessions feature's list, its group row and its drag handling, the inspector's `Related` section and Identity's `Tags` line, and their tests (EXTEND) **Tests:** a session whose exchange line is set reads `talking to builder · 14` and enters no attention count, and the row returns to its branch when the line clears; a drop on a group row calls `session.groupMove` with that group and a drop among the loose sessions calls it with none; `New group…` then Enter calls `session.groupCreate` with the session, and Escape calls nothing; `Unlink` is offered on a `related` link and on no other kind; a chat row dropped on a project group opens the convert card holding that project's path. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §The all-sessions list; Spec-021 §Pane kinds (the inspector); Spec-001 §Groups, Links And Tags **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-5-6 — Keyboard, find and draft keys, live.** The find box keeps its text for the session's stay: reopened, it holds the word it held with its text selected, and the find chord pressed while it is open re-focuses the field and selects its text. An Escape that interrupted a turn never counts as the first press of the Escape pair, so the next Escape with the draft empty opens no rewind menu. The `Cycle accounts` key works on a session screen too: while it is held a strip lists that session's provider's saved accounts with a mark on one, each repeat moves the mark, and the account under the mark at release takes `Default` through `providerAccount.setCurrent`, one change per gesture however many accounts it passed. `Next waiting` does what pressing `Next waiting (N)` does and `Answer this run` presses the main answer the open run's blocking step offers, both inert while a text field has focus, and where either cannot act its chord does nothing and raises no banner, its palette row listed with the reason [Plan-014](./014-workflow-authoring-and-execution.md) T5.28 names; `Open Workflows` does what the rail's Workflows button does, and pressed again with that screen open does nothing further; `Open Settings` likewise opens and never toggles, and `Search sessions` opens the sessions track and focuses its search and never closes it. `Next session` and `Previous session` step the focused view through every session the list holds except those on screen in another view, in the list's own order with chats first, whatever is folded or filtered, wrapping at both ends and never opening the track. `Previous ask` and `Next ask` land the reader on the previous or next message the person sent and announce it. `Search all sessions`, from its palette row and its own chord, opens one field over the session column with the hits grouped by session — the session's name, then its own matching messages, matched letters marked as the palette marks them — over the daemon's ranked read ([Plan-001](./001-session-core.md) T6.9), every session included and no ceiling, and choosing a hit switches to that session and lands on that message. `Interrupt everything` answers only with nothing selected and focus outside a terminal: with text selected the key is left to the platform's own copy, in the terminal body it is the shell's, and a second press never quits the app. `Send message` is a row like the others, holding Enter by default: while it holds `⌘Enter`, Enter starts a new line and `⌘Enter` sends. A chord is modifiers plus exactly one key in one spelling, answering only when the modifiers held are exactly its own, and on macOS the letter under Option and the punctuation under `⌘⇧` are recovered from the physical key (`KeyboardEvent.code`). An act's condition is a typed condition tree built in code over a closed set of named contexts, conflicts checked against those trees, so the `when` string grammar goes: `registries/commands/when-clause/parser.ts` and its `parseWhenClause` are deleted with every caller (`registries/keybindings/conflicts.ts`, `registries/commands/when-clause/cache.ts`) moved onto the tree. The palette lists the composer's model, effort and speed controls beside its level and mode, each opening that control's own list, with no chord minted for any of them; the five destinations, the color-scheme and notifications controls and the two searches sit under the heading `Console`. From an empty draft, `↑` and `↓` walk every message sent in the session, with no cap, giving the person's own draft back past the newest, and yield to a question card while one is open; `⌃R` searches the same messages: what is typed after it narrows to the newest sent message containing it, `⌃R` again steps to the next older match, Enter or `→` puts the match in the draft, and Escape gives the draft back as it was; recall and search fill the draft and send nothing. **Files:** the keybinding service, the find box, the composer's key handling and the accounts strip, the box that searches every session, the command registry's conditions (`registries/commands/when-clause/` EXTEND, its `parser.ts` DELETE) and `registries/keybindings/conflicts.ts` (EXTEND), and their tests (EXTEND) **Tests:** an Escape that interrupted a turn, then an Escape with the draft empty, opens no rewind menu; a `Cycle accounts` gesture passing over three accounts calls `providerAccount.setCurrent` once, with the account under the mark at release; `⌃R`, a typed word, `⌃R` and Enter put the second-newest match in the draft and send nothing, and Escape instead restores the draft as it was; `Next session` skips a session on screen in another view and wraps past the last; `⌥⌘A` does not fire while `⌥⌘⇧A` is held; `⌃C` with text selected interrupts nothing, and a second `⌃C` leaves the app running; a hit chosen in `Search all sessions` opens its session scrolled to that message. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Settings (Settings › Keyboard); Spec-021 §Session Composer; Spec-021 §The session screen (searching every session) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-5-7 — Settings › General and Appearance, live.** General's rows read and write the machine settings file through `machineSettings.read`, `machineSettings.write` and `machineSettings.subscribe` (T-020r-2-8), its update block draws T-020r-4-1's state, and its version row reads `app.version` (T-020r-2-9); Appearance writes the appearance record through `window.setAppearance` (T-020r-2-7); `Transcript width` previews in every window while its knob is dragged and writes the record once, on release. `Simplify for a screen reader` draws the console flat while it is on: no decorative borders or washes on the panes, no animation or transition anywhere, a tool call as one plain line of words rather than a glyph, a column and a cell, and the transcript as headings and paragraphs in order; the background service reads the same switch when it starts a Terminal pane's shell. While `Start a new session on the last model and effort used` is on, a new session opens the model and effort controls on the last model and effort the person picked on any session, and carries nothing else; with it off, or on the first session ever, it opens on the first usable signed-in account's provider and that provider's default model. The pair lives in the machine settings file beside the switch, written by the background service each time a pick moves it, and the new-session control and General's line under the switch, `Last used: <model>, at <effort> effort.`, both read it through `machineSettings.read()` and `machineSettings.subscribe()`; `session.create` carries the model and effort the controls hold ([Plan-001](./001-session-core.md) T6.1). The permission level is never remembered: the level control opens at `YOLO` on every new session, whatever the last session was left on. A new project session preselects the checkout `Default checkout for a new project session` names, `A new worktree` by default, and a choice made at creation always wins. **Files:** the General and Appearance pages, the flat drawing over the tokens and the tool row, and the new-session control, and their tests (EXTEND) **Tests:** with the screen-reader switch on, a tool call renders as one line of text and no element runs an animation or transition; a color scheme whose `window.setAppearance` write is refused leaves the root element's scheme as main kept it and raises `Could not save the color scheme, so it did not change.` on the window's banner; with remembering on, a new session's controls read the last pair and `session.create` carries it, and with it off, the first usable signed-in account's provider's default model; a new session's level control opens at `YOLO` whatever the last session was left on; a checkout chosen at creation wins over the default. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Settings; [Spec-001 §Default Behavior](../specs/001-session-core.md#default-behavior) (the last-used pair; the level opens at `YOLO`) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-5-8 — The cloud task row.** Send on a composer armed for the cloud calls `cloud.taskStart {sessionId, prompt, environment?, attempts?}`: the message lands in the transcript as the person's own, the agent never receives it, and the composer disarms. Under that message sits the task's row, from `cloud.taskList` and `cloud.task_updated`, reading `Cloud task · <state>` with the state only what the provider reported, in sentence case whatever the wire spells: on Codex `Pending`, `Ready`, `Applied` or `Failed`, with Codex's own message under a failure; on Claude Code `Submitted` for the task's whole life, with `Open on claude.ai`. A ready Codex task carries `Show diff`, which opens the attempt's diff (`cloud.taskDiffRead`) in the review pane as a read-only source, `Attempt <n> of <m>` where it ran more than one, and `Bring back` (`cloud.taskApply`), after which the row reads `Cloud task · Applied`, or on a partial apply `Brought back in part · <n> skipped · <m> in conflict` with every path listed; `Undo bring back` puts the files back from the apply's checkpoint through the same dry run and question as `Undo to here`. A Claude Code task carries `Bring back` at every moment, which opens the returned work as a new session beside this one, named after the task, while the row stays `Submitted`. The rows stop and hold nothing, persist with the session across restarts and read the same on every device. **Files:** the cloud task row and the composer's cloud send, and their tests (CREATE) **Tests:** a state the provider did not report never renders; a partial apply lists every skipped and conflicting path; Send while armed calls `cloud.taskStart` and starts no turn. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §The session screen (the cloud task); Spec-004 §Interfaces And Contracts (the cloud operations) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-5-9 — The one fuzzy scorer under test.** The subsequence scorer in `packages/search-ranking`, which the palette, Settings search, the keyboard page, the sessions search and the daemon's `@` file search share, gets its ranking tests there: a query that is not a case-insensitive subsequence matches nothing; an initialism reaches a candidate whose word starts it spells over one that holds the letters mid-word (`cs` ranks `Copy SHA` over `class`); adjacent matches rank over scattered ones; a prefix match ranks first; equal scores keep a stable order between keystrokes; and `matchedIndices` point at the characters the query matched in the original candidate. The scorer's speed is not measured here. **Files:** the scorer's test in `packages/search-ranking` (CREATE), with the package's `vitest.config.ts` and `test` script (CREATE), so the test is discovered **Tests:** the cases above. **Spec coverage:** Desktop App Implementation Notes §Console Libraries (the one fuzzy matcher) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-5-10 — The banners under the session header, live.** One place directly under the session header, above the pane note and the surface, holds the session's banners, each in the shape [Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian)](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#console-design-meridian) (§The session screen) fixes: its own dismissal, blocking nothing and entering no attention count, in the words that section gives. It draws five, each from its own source. A provider that started without part of its settings, from the `session.notice` of kind `settings_ignored`, names the file and the line or key the provider reported and offers `Open the file`, which opens it through `native.openInEditor` in the editor Settings › General names; its `×` puts it away for this start of the provider, the next clean load of the file clears it, and a later start still on defaults draws it again ([Plan-003](./003-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md) T3.29). A provider process that ended on its own, from the `run.failed` carrying `processExit` ([Plan-003](./003-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md) T3.41), reads `Claude Code ended unexpectedly · exit code 137 · Restart ×` (`Codex ended unexpectedly …` on Codex), the exit code the daemon's own and never composed, and `Restart` calls `session.restart` (Plan-003 T3.29); its `×` puts it away for this ending, a later one drawing it again, and an idle session whose provider sleeps draws none. A provider build that changed under a running session, from the `session.notice` of kind `provider_updated` (Plan-003 T3.43), names both builds and offers `Restart the session`, which calls `session.restart` onto the new build; the running turn runs on until then, and no flow row is drawn. A lost folder, from the mount's health on `repo.mountRead` and `repo.mount_health_changed` ([Plan-006](./006-repo-attachment-and-workspace-binding.md) T3.10), names the folder and goes on its own when the folder is back. A pane arrangement the window could not save reads `Pane layout not saved · it will save again on your next change`, its code going to the window's diagnostic capture. `Catching up…` sits in the same place and is T-020p-1C-2's. **Files:** the session view's banner place and its banners in the renderer, and their tests (CREATE) **Tests:** `Restart` and `Restart the session` each call `session.restart` once per press and nothing before it; a `provider_updated` notice under a running turn leaves the turn running and adds no flow row; a `settings_ignored` banner put away stays away when the session is left and opened again, and a later start still on defaults draws it again. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §The session screen (banners under the session header); [Spec-005 §Session Lifecycle](../specs/005-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#session-lifecycle-session_lifecycle) (`settings_ignored`, `provider_updated`) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-5-11 — The drag, the console's own.** One pointer-drag core in the renderer serves these drags: the terminal's tab strip, Preview's tab strip, the waiting messages at the end of the transcript, the pane row (T-020r-5-12) and the session views' titles (T-020r-5-13). Past a small movement the pressed item lifts and takes pointer capture, so a press that never lifts stays an ordinary click, and follows the pointer by `transform`, its neighbors glide apart with FLIP on the item's own document (`element.animate`), and on release the item glides into its slot and its owner commits the new order once — `pty.reorder`, `preview.pageReorder`, `run.queueReorder`, the pane layout or the window's row of views. Escape, or capture lost, puts every item back and commits nothing, and an owner whose commit is refused puts its items back through the core's `settle`. The drag writes the item's `transform` in the `pointermove` handler, and its one frame loop is the edge scroll, which runs on frames from the item's own window only while the pointer holds near an end of a list that scrolls; it binds every listener to the pressed item's own document and runs every animation on the item itself, never touching the hidden console document, so a drag works the same in every window. The keyboard and menu paths beside each drag — a waiting row's up and down arrows, Alt+Shift with an arrow on a pane, the tab menus — make the same change and announce it in the live region. Files dragged in from the system onto the composer keep the platform's native drop. The core is generic non-UI code in the renderer's `lib/`, with its hook in `hooks/`, because the transcript, terminal, preview and sessions features all use it and no feature imports another. **Removed in the same change:** `@atlaskit/pragmatic-drag-and-drop` from the desktop app's `package.json`, with the lockfile refreshed by the install, and the pane layout's drag built on it, the `usePaneDragSource`, `usePaneDropTarget` and `usePaneLayoutDragMonitor` hooks, with `features/sessions/pane-layout/drag.ts` kept to commit and announce a drop the core settles; and Preview's native tab drag in `features/preview/tab-reorder.ts`. The root `knip.json` holds no entry for the library. **Files:** the pointer-drag core, `lib/reorder-drag.ts`, and its hook, `hooks/useReorderDrag.ts` (CREATE), `features/sessions/pane-layout/drag.ts` (EXTEND — a drop's commit through `PaneLayoutStore.reorderPane` and its announcement), the terminal's and Preview's tab strips, the transcript's waiting rows and the pane layout (EXTEND), the desktop app's `package.json` and the lockfile (EXTEND — the library removed), and their tests **Tests:** in the browser tier on real input (§Test, Drags), in the test page's document and in a second document, for the terminal's tabs, Preview's tabs and the waiting rows: the item stays under the pointer while it moves, its neighbors glide apart, a release commits one new order through its call and Escape commits none; a release with the order unchanged calls nothing; a waiting row's arrow key sends one `run.queueReorder` naming the full new order and announces the move. **Budget:** frame time while a tab and a waiting row are dragged with a reply streaming, against the same stream with no drag, measured before and after, with no frame over 16.7 ms at 120 Hz. **Spec coverage:** Desktop App Implementation Notes §Console Libraries (no drag library; [ADR-040](../decisions/040-drag-is-our-own-on-pointer-events.md)); [Spec-003 §Design Decisions](../specs/003-queue-steer-pause-resume.md#design-decisions) (the waiting messages' reorder) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-5-12 — The pane row.** A view's panes form one block beside the conversation: a row of main panes — the inspector, Review, Sidekicks, Preview and the file view, each at most once — as many as are open, with no cap: opening a pane adds it to the row beside the focused pane and replaces none. Each pane keeps its own floor and the conversation keeps its own; when the next pane does not fit, the row scrolls sideways — nothing is refused and nothing folds — and a pane just opened is scrolled into view through the scroll chokepoint, which writes every scroll offset. The terminal sits above or below the main panes, as wide as the row. The block sits to the right of the conversation by default and moves to the left as a whole. A pane's header drags the pane on the console's pointer drag (T-020r-5-11) to a new place in the row, or, dragged past the conversation, moves the whole block to its other side, and the terminal's header drags it above or below the row; with focus in a pane and not in a text field, Alt+Shift with Left or Right moves that pane one place along the row, and with Up or Down moves the terminal above or below it, each a row on the Keyboard page, the move announced in the live region as `Moved the <pane> pane to position N of M.`, or `The <pane> pane was not moved.` where it stays put, a drop back in the same place included. Which panes a session has open, their order and side and where the terminal sits are kept per session in the pane-layout snapshot.
