The row's floors and each pane kind's default width reach main through `window.setMinimumSize` and `window.setDefaultSizes` (T-020r-2-7), sent before the first window opens and again whenever the text size changes, so a window opens at a size the row fits and never maximized. Full width still takes one pane full and leaving it restores the row. A pane moved into a window of its own leaves the row (T-020r-8-5). `⌘I` opens the inspector beside the focused pane, and closing it gives focus back to the pane focused before. A pane's own scroller keeps its vertical scroll and hands a sideways swipe to the row, so a swipe over any pane moves the row. `Move pane left` or `Move pane right` pressed past the row's end that faces the conversation moves the whole block of panes to the other side of it, and pressed past the end at the window's edge moves nothing. A pane whose subject goes closes itself, with one line between the header and the surface naming the pane and why — `Review closed — this session has nothing to review.`, `Terminal closed — this session has no shell.`, `Preview closed — this session has no page or dev server to show.`, `Sidekicks closed — this session dispatched nothing.` — its chip on a project session staying where it stands, grayed with its reason, and opening any pane or leaving the session answering the note; a session whose remembered pane lost its subject opens no pane and draws no note. Preview is an ordinary main pane, kept in the session's layout like the others, so the layout's ephemeral kind and its cascade go: `EPHEMERAL_PANE_KINDS` and `isEphemeralPaneKind` in `routing/panes/kinds.ts`, the `isEphemeral` member in `features/sessions/pane-layout/state.ts`, and the close-with-its-source rule in `features/sessions/pane-layout/store.ts`. **Files:** the sessions feature's pane layout and its snapshot (`features/sessions/pane-layout/`), `routing/panes/kinds.ts`, the session screen, the keyboard rows and the renderer's window-size sender (EXTEND), and their tests **Tests:** `window.setMinimumSize` and `window.setDefaultSizes` are each called before the first window opens and again with new figures after the text size changes; opening Review, Preview and the inspector keeps all three open in that order; a pane opened past the view's width scrolls the row sideways with that pane in view and draws no pane and no conversation under its floor; a header drag and the chord each move a pane one place, and the order holds after a reload; the terminal moved above the row measures the row's width; a pane moved left of the conversation is there after a reload; `Move pane right` on the last pane on the window's edge moves nothing; a Preview pane open at a reload is open after it, and closing the pane it was opened from leaves it open; Review on a session left with nothing to review closes with its note and its chip grays in place; in the Electron tier on real input (§Test, Drags), a pane's header drag lands it where it was released, in the first window and in a second window the console opened. **Budget:** a view with five panes open against one with one pane, for renderer memory at rest and the time to open a pane, measured before and after. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian) (the pane layout) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-5-13 — Dragging a session view.** A view's title — the session's name in its header — drags the view on the console's pointer drag (T-020r-5-11), the title holding pointer capture inside its window and outside it; the header's empty space stays the window's own drag region. Over another view in the same window the two swap places. Past its window's edge the renderer hands main the pointer's point on the screen; main hit-tests the bounds of the windows in its registry and answers with the window under that point, never the hidden console window, and that window draws its drop place in its row of views at its next frame. Released there, the view joins that row at that place and the session leaves the window it came from, which closes when no view is left in it. Past every window, the renderer opens a window of its own with the view in it, and main moves that window with the pointer on every move; released over the desktop it stays where it is, and carried over another window it docks into that window's row. Escape puts the view back where it was and closes a torn-off window. Outside Wayland the drag never uses the platform's native drag, because a native drag released on the desktop reports its end only after the system slides the refused drop back and another app may take the drop. On Linux under Wayland, where Electron neither reads the pointer outside a window nor moves one ([`screen.getCursorScreenPoint`](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/screen) and [`BaseWindow.setPosition`](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/base-window) are not supported there), the title drags with the platform's native drag instead and a torn-off view opens where the compositor places it ([Plan-027](./027-windows-and-linux.md) T27.2.15). The screen point, the window under it and the torn-off window's move cross the `window` bridge namespace as `findWindowAt`, `startTearOff`, `moveTearOff`, `endTearOff` and `dockView`, the members [Preload Bridge Contract](../architecture/contracts/preload-bridge-contract.md) names; main answers with a window id and reads nothing of the renderer's layout, and the renderer places the view. **Files:** main's `windows/` (EXTEND — the hit test and the torn-off window's move), the `window` bridge handler, `PreloadApi` and `PlatformBridge` (EXTEND), the session header and the sessions feature's view registry (EXTEND), and their tests **Tests:** in the Electron tier on real input (§Test, Drags): a title dragged onto the other view in its window swaps the two; dragged over a second window it draws that window's drop place and, released, the session is in that window only and the emptied first window closes; dragged past every window a window follows the pointer and stays where it is released; Escape mid-drag leaves every window as it was; main's hit test answers the topmost window under a point and never the hidden console window or the window being torn off. **Budget:** main's hit test per pointer move and the frame at which the target window draws its drop place, measured, against the frame budget of 16.7 ms. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian) (the row of session views; windows); Spec-021 §Main Process Responsibilities (window management) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-10 (main places windows and answers with a window id; the renderer places the view)
- **T-020r-5-14 — The new-session picker.** The one new-session control, dashed at rest and solid on hover or while open, opens its picker under the sessions track, the whole of the choice a session starts from: `Just chat`, with its sub-line `no repo`, which starts a chat session; a `Recent repos` heading over the recent repositories, each starting a project session there; and an `Elsewhere` heading over `Open folder…` and `Clone repository…`, whose foot and clone card are T-020p-1C-5's. It offers no machine, because a new session runs on the machine in view. On the machine itself `Open folder…` opens the platform's own folder chooser (`native.showOpenDialog` with `pickFolder`) and attaches the folder picked through `repo.attach`, behind the attach controller that already exists (`features/repos/mounts/attach/controller.ts` and its `useAttachController`); on another device it lists the machine's own folders in place over `repo.folderList`, starting at the home folder of the account the service runs as, so the device acts only on what the service listed. A folder that is not a git repository is refused in place in the picker, `Could not attach: not a git repository`, a folder already attached switches the screen to that project, and a pick closes the picker, switches the screen to the new session and puts the caret in the draft. `AttachRepositoryDialog`, a modal the design does not have, goes with its `ignoreFiles` entry. **Files:** the new-session control and its picker in `features/sessions/new-session/` (EXTEND), `features/repos/mounts/attach/` (EXTEND), `features/repos/mounts/attach/AttachRepositoryDialog.tsx` (DELETE), the root `knip.json` (EXTEND — the dialog's `ignoreFiles` entry removed), and their tests **Tests:** `Open folder…` with a folder picked calls `repo.attach` once and opens the new session with the caret in its draft; a canceled chooser calls nothing; a folder that is not a repository draws the refusal in the picker and starts no session; on another device the picker lists what `repo.folderList` returned and opens no chooser. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §The all-sessions list (the new-session control and its picker); [Spec-007 §Required Behavior](../specs/007-repo-attachment-and-workspace-binding.md#required-behavior) (attaching from the picker) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-5-15 — The convert card.** Converting a chat to a project is one flow with three doors — `Attach a repo` on the composer's session strip and in the inspector, `Convert to project…` on the session's one actions list, and a chat row dropped on a project group (T-020r-5-5) — and one card, opening in place above the working line where the worktree setup card runs: one line saying what will happen, a typed path field with no `Browse…`, empty from the strip, the inspector and the menu and holding the project's own path when the row was dropped on its group, and `Cancel` beside `Convert to project`. The typed text travels as data and the daemon checks it: `Convert to project` calls `session.convert {sessionId, path}` ([Plan-001](./001-session-core.md) T6.7). Once it lands the session's shape changes in place with its history kept: its row moves into that project's group, the three pane chips go live, the strip gains its worktree chip and loses `Attach a repo`, and Review opens on the copied files as new changes. **Files:** the composer card's convert card in `features/composer/` (EXTEND), the session strip's and the inspector's `Attach a repo` (EXTEND), and their tests **Tests:** each of the three doors opens the one card, the drop's holding that project's path and the other two's empty; `Cancel` calls nothing; `Convert to project` calls `session.convert` once with the text as typed. **Spec coverage:** [Spec-007 §Required Behavior](../specs/007-repo-attachment-and-workspace-binding.md#required-behavior) (converting a chat); [Spec-001 §Required Behavior](../specs/001-session-core.md#required-behavior) (a chat becomes a project in place); Spec-021 §Session Composer (the session strip); Spec-021 §Pane kinds (the inspector's `Attach a repo`) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-5-16 — Leaving unsaved edits asks first.** One leave guard in `routing/` that a mounted screen registers while it holds unsaved edits — the agent-definition editor, the Skills folder editor and the workflow Builder — and that every navigation away from that screen checks before it leaves: a rail press, a palette act, an address change, a back control and the platform's own Back. While a guard is registered the navigation waits for the screen's own answer, asked once in that screen's own words ([Spec-026 §The editor](../specs/026-agent-definitions-and-peer-invocation.md#the-editor), [Spec-015 §Visual Workflow Builder](../specs/015-workflow-authoring-and-execution.md#visual-workflow-builder)), leaving on a yes and staying with the edits as they were on a no; with nothing unsaved no guard is registered and nothing asks. This task builds the seam and asks nothing of its own. **Files:** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/routing/` (EXTEND — the leave guard), the rail's, the palette's and the router's navigation (EXTEND), and their tests **Tests:** with a guard registered, a rail press to another destination leaves only once the guard answers yes, and a no keeps the screen and its edits; an address change with a guard registered waits the same way; with none registered navigation leaves at once. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §The icon rail; [Spec-026 §The editor](../specs/026-agent-definitions-and-peer-invocation.md#the-editor) (leaving); [Spec-015 §Visual Workflow Builder](../specs/015-workflow-authoring-and-execution.md#visual-workflow-builder) (closing with unsaved changes) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-5-17 — The menu-bar icon's face, live.** On macOS main keeps the menu-bar icon on the face [Spec-021 §Main Process Responsibilities](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#main-process-responsibilities) sets, needs you over working over idle. Needs you: the attention read main already holds for the app-icon count counts anything waiting, read whether or not `Show a count on the app icon` is on. Working: nothing waits and this machine's daemon reports a session `running` on `session.list`, each entry read through `sessionActivityAsOf` (`@ai-sidekicks/contracts/session/directory`) so the face ages to idle exactly when the row does, or a workflow run `running`, from `workflow.subscribe` with no filter, opened first, and `workflow.runList` with `status: ["running"]` read to its last page, so no run that starts between the two is missed. Idle: otherwise, and while the service cannot be reached. Main recomputes the face on each change those reads deliver and at the earliest moment a running session's reading would age out, one timer re-set on each change, never a poll, and calls `tray.setImage` only when the face changes; nothing animates. The working and needs-you faces — `menu-bar-workingTemplate.png` and `menu-bar-needs-youTemplate.png`, each with its `@2x` and the `.svg` it is drawn from — join the idle face in `apps/desktop/resources/`, copied unchanged from the approved icon set, so each name still ends in `Template` and each `@2x` keeps its name, which is how macOS reads a template image and its high-density copy. **Provides:** the menu-bar icon's face, one pure function from the waiting count, the session entries, the running workflow runs and the time to `idle`, `working` or `needs-you`, and the one `Tray` image swap. **Consumes:** Plan-016 T3.1's attention read; Plan-001 T6.2's `session.list`; Plan-014 T1.5's `workflow.runList` and T5.12's `workflow.subscribe`; T-020p-1B-5's `Tray`. **Budget:** main's resident memory and idle processor time with forty sessions, three of them running, and two workflow runs running, against the same machine before the task; the face's reads add no poll and no wake while nothing changes beyond the daemon's own renewals. **Files:** the menu-bar icon's face module in main's `services/` (CREATE), the `Tray` owner in main's `windows/` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/resources/` (EXTEND — the two faces), and their tests **Tests:** `main-unit`: a waiting entry with sessions running reads needs you; no waiting entry and one session running reads working, and the same entry renewed longer ago than the stale bound reads idle at that moment with no new delivery; a workflow run that starts between the subscription and the list read reads working; an unreachable service reads idle; a delivery that leaves the face unchanged calls `tray.setImage` never; `Show a count on the app icon` off leaves needs you in place. **Spec coverage:** [Spec-021 §Main Process Responsibilities](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#main-process-responsibilities) (window size, menus and title — the menu-bar icon) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-5-18 — One section head and one fact style on every Settings page.** Every page's section heads take one class — uppercase, `--meridian-text-faint`, the `tracking-heading` letter-spacing step, a size from the type scale, weight 500 — in place of `.meridian-settings-page__block-title` (`features/settings/settings-page.css`) and the Browser page's `.meridian-browser-settings__section-title` (`features/settings/pages/browser/BrowserPage.css`), which go; a page's facts (`.meridian-settings-page__facts`) read their labels in `--meridian-text-muted` and their values at weight 500 in `--meridian-text` ([Spec-021 §Settings](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#settings)). **Files:** `features/settings/settings-page.css`, `features/settings/pages/browser/BrowserPage.css` and the pages that draw a section head (EXTEND). **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Settings
- **T-020r-5-19 — The restart question card.** A run the daemon halts after a restart because its record and the provider's disagree ([Spec-013 §Fallback Behavior](../specs/013-persistence-and-recovery.md#fallback-behavior)) is drawn as a waiting card where the run stopped, the single card in the composer in the place the approval card takes, and the run takes an entry in the attention list, in amber because a person is needed. The card carries one of Spec-013's two questions in its words, the provider named and the counts read from the halt: the provider-ahead question with `Keep what it did` and `Undo to before it`, or the daemon-ahead question with `Continue from Claude Code's record` and `Continue with a hand-over`, `Codex` read in the provider's place on Codex. It reads the run's `waiting_for_input` state carrying `recovery-needed` over `run.subscribeState`, and the press sends `run.recoveryResolve {runId, choice}`, `keep_provider`, `undo_to_agreed`, `continue_provider` or `hand_over` in that order; the card closes on the state that follows, and it holds no clock. **Consumes:** [Plan-012](./012-persistence-and-recovery.md) T12.5's halt and `run.recoveryResolve`; [Plan-016](./016-notifications-and-attention-model.md) T2.1's `pending_input` entry for the halted run and T3.2's attention reader for the list; T-020p-1C-3 EXTEND's one card at a time. **Files:** the restart question card in `features/composer/` (CREATE), the composer's one-card choice (EXTEND), and their tests **Tests:** a run whose state turns to `waiting_for_input` with `recovery-needed` draws the card with its question and its two choices, and the attention list gains the run's entry; each choice sends `run.recoveryResolve` once with its own choice and nothing else; the card closes when the run's state moves on, and a reload draws it again while the halt stands. **Spec coverage:** [Spec-013 §Fallback Behavior](../specs/013-persistence-and-recovery.md#fallback-behavior); Spec-021 §Transcript View (the restart question card) **Verifies invariant:** none

### Phase 6 — Build Pipeline And Release Manifest

**Goal:** Implementation Steps 11 and 12 — the fuses and the ASAR integrity digest through `electron-builder`, per-platform signing with the notarization submit-and-poll design, the Linux runtime and the Windows half packed into the Windows installers, the release manifest, and the installer-size gate.

**Preconditions.** Built in the Release group ([cross-plan-dependencies.md §Platform order](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#platform-order)). Phase 5 must have merged; the pipeline packages the complete application. No upstream-plan item gates this phase — it consumes only this plan's own output and release-engineering credentials, which §Risks And Blockers tracks separately.

#### Tasks

- **T-020r-6-1 — Fuses and ASAR integrity through `electron-builder`.** `electron-builder.yml`'s `electronFuses` setting carries the fuse posture, and its integrity embed writes the ASAR integrity digest; `electron-builder` applies both after packaging and before it signs, so the signature covers them and no step after signing changes the binary. The packaged-artifact fuse test reads the shipped binary's fuse wire through `@electron/fuses`' `getCurrentFuseWire`, because only the binary proves the setting landed. **Files:** `apps/desktop/electron-builder.yml` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/build/release-artifact-fuses.test.ts` (EXTEND) **Spec coverage:** Desktop App Implementation Notes §Electron Fuses (the fuse posture); Desktop App Implementation Notes §Build And Packaging (the fuses and the digest precede signing) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-3
- **T-020r-6-2 — Per-platform signing and notarization.** the code-signing build step in the desktop app's `build/` folder signing every macOS build with the project's self-signed code-signing identity and one bundle identifier on every build — only Gatekeeper's first-launch acceptance waits on the Apple Developer ID certificate ([BL-108](../backlog.md#bl-108-windows--macos-signing-procurement-evidence)) — and, once that certificate exists, implementing §Notarization Submission And Poll for macOS — detached `notarytool submit --no-wait -f json`, persisted submission id, bounded resumable `wait` / `info` polls under the 168-hour overall abandonment ceiling, `stapler staple` on `Accepted`, `notarytool log` captured on `Invalid` / `Rejected` — plus SignPath Foundation's open-source signing of the NSIS installers and `sidekicks-windows-half.exe` for x64 and arm64 on Windows ([BL-108](../backlog.md#bl-108-windows--macos-signing-procurement-evidence)), which the Windows release waits on, publication of the `.deb` and `.rpm` packages to the project's apt and dnf repositories on Cloudsmith, whose per-repository key signs each RPM on upload and the repository metadata. The packaging targets are `.dmg` and `.zip` on macOS, NSIS for x64 and arm64 on Windows, and AppImage, `.deb` and `.rpm` on Linux; there is no MSI, Snap or Flatpak and no staged rollout. If the project is not eligible for SignPath Foundation, that goes back to the person and the distribution plan does not change silently. **Files:** `apps/desktop/build/code-sign.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/build/code-sign.test.ts` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Desktop App Implementation Notes §Code Signing And Notarization (timeout + retry rather than synchronous blocking; the documented 24–120+ hour queue-delay range this plan's ceiling sits above); Desktop App Implementation Notes §macOS (Developer ID Application cert plus `notarytool` submission and stapling) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-3 (signing runs last, and the notarization poll never re-enters the pipeline ahead of it)
- **T-020r-6-3 — Release manifest and installer-size gate.** the release-manifest script producing the release manifest in the schema Plan-005 T-005r-3-6 defines — a monotonic `version`, `released_at` and each artifact's SHA-256 — published to the GitHub Release beside `electron-builder`'s update files, and a `size-limit` entry, beside the renderer bundle budget in the same config, whose `@size-limit/file` plugin measures each packaged installer with `brotli: false` (no compression, so the number is the file's own size) and fails CI when one exceeds the ADR-015 150 MB ceiling, each installer measured without the Linux runtime archives a Windows installer carries. **Files:** the release-manifest script in `apps/desktop/scripts/` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/tests/budget/` (EXTEND — the installer entry in the `size-limit` config) **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Main Process ↔ Auto-Update Feed (the manifest the release carries); Desktop App Implementation Notes §Build And Packaging (packaged-artifact constraints)
- **T-020r-6-4 — The release workflow, with its one-time vulnerability scan.** `.github/workflows/release.yml` is the repository's one release workflow, started by `workflow_dispatch` alone until the first release; T-020r-7-3 adds the tag-push trigger, after which a tag push releases. Its packaging jobs run `electron-builder` on each platform's own runner for the targets the release ships: `.dmg` and `.zip` on macOS for arm64 and x64, a per-user NSIS installer on Windows for x64 and arm64, and AppImage on Linux for x64 and arm64, with `.deb` and `.rpm`, each taking the app icon from `build/icon.icon`: `mac.icon` points at it, compiled by `actool` on a macOS runner with Xcode 26, with an `.icns` made from it for macOS before 26 and the Windows and Linux icon files made from the same source. Its publish job puts every installer, `electron-builder`'s update files and T-020r-6-3's release manifest and checksum list on the version's GitHub Release, which is the app's update feed. The other release steps are jobs in this workflow and land with their own tasks: signing and the Cloudsmith publication of the `.deb` and `.rpm` (T-020r-6-2), the Linux runtime jobs (T-020r-6-5), the sign-and-notarize job (T-020r-7-3) and the Windows signing step ([Plan-021](./021-rust-pty-sidecar.md) T-021-4-4). Its release-PR job runs `release-please-action` in manifest mode over the sidecar's npm platform packages, as [ADR-022](../decisions/022-v1-ci-cd-and-release-automation.md) Surface 2 sets out, and their publication is [Plan-021](./021-rust-pty-sidecar.md) T-021-5-1's. Every job's install sets `ELECTRON_SKIP_BINARY_DOWNLOAD`, since no release job launches Electron. Before the first release, the workflow scans every dependency once for known vulnerabilities, as a release gate. No license scan runs: a library's license is read when it is picked. There is no provenance attestation, no SBOM and no reproducible-build job. **Files:** `.github/workflows/release.yml` (CREATE — the release workflow), `release-please-config.json` (CREATE — the manifest-mode packages and their overrides), `.release-please-manifest.json` (CREATE — each package's released version) **Spec coverage:** Desktop App Implementation Notes §Build And Packaging (what a release carries)
- **T-020r-6-5 — The Linux runtime, packed into the Windows installers.** The release builds the Linux runtime as the standalone Node.js bundle — the Node runtime, the JavaScript, the SQLite and node-pty native modules, and the CLI — for glibc and for musl on x64 and arm64, the musl archives built in an Alpine container on the Node.js project's `linux-x64-musl` and `linux-arm64-musl` unofficial builds with the native modules compiled there, and publishes them, each with its SHA-256, as the Linux release. Each Windows installer carries, through `electron-builder`'s extra resources, the two archives for its architecture with their SHA-256 and `sidekicks-windows-half.exe` ([Plan-021](./021-rust-pty-sidecar.md) T-021-4-5), so the first start works offline on the same bytes a Linux user downloads. The NSIS installer creates the Start-menu shortcut carrying the app's `ToastActivatorCLSID`, so a click on a notification the banner process posted cold-launches the app ([Spec-017 §Desktop-to-Desktop Delivery](../specs/017-notifications-and-attention-model.md#desktop-to-desktop-delivery)). **Budget:** the Windows installer's size with no Linux archive, with the glibc archive and with both, measured in this task's PR. **Files:** `.github/workflows/release.yml` (EXTEND — the Linux bundle jobs), `apps/desktop/electron-builder.yml` (EXTEND — the archives, the Windows half, the shortcut and its activator) **Tests:** on a packaged Windows build with the network off, the first start brings up the service from the packed archive. **Spec coverage:** Desktop App Implementation Notes §Build And Packaging; Spec-017 §Desktop-to-Desktop Delivery **Verifies invariant:** none

### Phase 7 — E2E Suite, Renderer Test Harness, CI Gate, And Release Runbook

**Goal:** Implementation Steps 14, 15, and 16 — the Playwright `_electron` suite, the renderer test harness, the CI gate, and `RELEASE.md` — and Settings › Runtime drawn live (T-020r-7-5).

**Preconditions.** Phase 5 must have merged. The suite runs on the macOS build; its run on a signed, packaged build, T-020r-7-3's sign-and-notarize release job and T-020r-7-4's release runbook wait for Phase 6, in the Release group ([cross-plan-dependencies.md §Platform order](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#platform-order)). T-020r-7-5 waits on the verbs it calls: [Plan-005](./005-local-ipc-and-daemon-control.md)'s `daemon.*` status, stop, restart, retention, package-cache, backup and configuration methods, `session.terminalProviderSessionList` and the supervisor's update and restore; [Plan-019](./019-data-retention-export-and-deletion.md)'s `daemon.dataExport` and `daemon.dataErase`; [Plan-006](./006-repo-attachment-and-workspace-binding.md)'s `repo.mountList` and `repo.detach`; [Plan-007](./007-worktree-lifecycle-and-execution-modes.md) T3.7 to T3.10's worktree retire, restore and kept-worktree methods (CP-020-7); and [Plan-014](./014-workflow-authoring-and-execution.md)'s `workflow.webhookListenerRead`.

#### Tasks

- **T-020r-7-1 — Playwright `_electron` E2E suite.** The end-to-end tests from §Test — launch, fuses, bridge surface, deep-link — landing in the `e2e` Vitest project (`test:e2e`), whose glob, `tests/e2e/**/*.test.ts`, no other project reaches, and launching the app through `tests/helpers/electron/harness.ts`, the package's single `_electron` launch, with the tier's `dependsOn` on the appropriate build task. The console document's own facts — `window.desktopBridge` defined, `window.require` undefined — are not this suite's: they are the smoke tier's probe, `tests/launch.smoke.test.ts`, directly under `tests/` in the `smoke` project, which spawns the smoke bundle. Playwright is pinned in `package.json` because the `_electron` namespace is still experimental. **Files:** `apps/desktop/tests/e2e/*.test.ts` (CREATE), `apps/desktop/turbo.json` (EXTEND), `apps/desktop/package.json` (EXTEND) **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Acceptance Criteria (the runtime-observable bullets: `window.desktopBridge` defined, `window.require` undefined, fuse wire as declared, token containment); Spec-021 §Process Model (the three-process system the suite exercises end to end) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-1, I-020-2 (the E2E suite is the runtime-side proof of the two invariants whose unit tests are per-module; the deep-link test file drives the `session/<id>` route end-to-end and asserts a malformed URL is refused at the parser boundary)
- **T-020r-7-2 — Renderer test harness.** The renderer's tests drive the fixture bridge, `createFixtureBridge` in `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/services/platform/bridge.fixture.ts`, a real `PlatformBridge` backed by a scripted scenario that runs in `test:renderer` without an Electron process, through the suite helpers in `apps/desktop/tests/helpers/fixture/bridge.ts`; this row adds no second bridge stand-in. What it owes is a remainder IPC dispatcher able to drive **two** Electron clients in one E2E run, which Phase 8's two-client Preview checks drive. **Files:** the two-client dispatcher in `apps/desktop/tests/helpers/` (CREATE), with its test beside it (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Preload Bridge Contract (the fixture bridge must be shape-identical to the real surface or it verifies nothing); Spec-021 §Renderer Responsibilities (renderer tests exercise projections, never authority) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-1 (the fixture bridge implements the `PlatformBridge` type, which takes the preload's `PreloadApi` whole, so a member added to the real surface breaks the fixture at compile time)
- **T-020r-7-3 — CI gate.** The desktop's gate is `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (Implementation Step 15): its `test` job runs lint, the format check and typecheck, and its `desktop` job runs the package's `test` chain one group per matrix leg on every pull request touching `apps/desktop/**` or `packages/**`, the accessibility and endurance tiers among them once T-020p-1C-8 lands them, except the E2E tier, which its `desktop-slow` job runs on the nightly run, on pull requests into `main`, and on a manual run. This task adds a per-platform sign-and-notarize release job in `.github/workflows/release.yml`, the repository's one release workflow, which gains a trigger on tag pushes beside its `workflow_dispatch`. **Files:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (EXTEND), `.github/workflows/release.yml` (EXTEND) **Spec coverage:** Desktop App Implementation Notes §Console Test Tiers (where each tier runs); Desktop App Implementation Notes §Code Signing And Notarization (the release job) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-7-4 — Release runbook.** `apps/desktop/RELEASE.md` — per-platform signing prerequisites, the notarization resume procedure (recovering a submission id and re-polling after a CI job ends), and the rotation procedure for the one signing key the project holds, the macOS code-signing identity kept as a release secret (Windows signing is SignPath Foundation's and the Linux repository key is Cloudsmith's). **Files:** `apps/desktop/RELEASE.md` (CREATE) **Spec coverage:** Desktop App Implementation Notes §Code Signing And Notarization (the operational procedure the mechanism implies) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-7-5 — Settings › Runtime, live.** The Runtime page draws the background service over its own wire, every row reading the service and nothing computed on screen: the service's line, its processor and memory with their read times, and `Check again` from `daemon.status.read`, whose `secretsFile` draws the Linux secrets line where present, and the supervisor's `daemon.status` topic, which on Windows carries `cannotStart {reason}` and `whileSignedOut`; `Restart` and `Stop` through `daemon.restart` and `daemon.stop`, each confirming in place, `Stop`'s confirm counting the terminal Codex sessions `session.terminalProviderSessionList` returns; `Update the background service` over the supervisor's `daemon.requestUpdate()`, `daemon.cancelUpdate()` and `daemon.subscribeUpdate()`, which main relays (T-020r-4-1), its waiting line naming the running work; `Folders this machine can reach` from `repo.mountList`, a project folder's `Remove` through `repo.detach` and a worktree's through `repo.worktreeRetire {worktreeId, discard}`, and each kept worktree from `repo.removedWorktreeList` with `Put back` (`repo.worktreeRestore`) and `Delete now` (`repo.removedWorktreeDelete`); `Keep diagnostic logs for`, `Keep sessions for` and `Delete old data` over `daemon.retentionRead`, `daemon.retentionUpdate` and `daemon.retentionPurge`; the package cache's usage and clears over `daemon.packageCacheRead` and `daemon.packageCacheClear {cache}`; backups over `daemon.backupRead`, `daemon.backupStart` and the supervisor's `daemon.requestRestore(backupId)`, the switch and the folder written through `machineSettings.write`, with `backup.completed`, `backup.failed` and `backup.restored` settling the line; `Export all data` over `daemon.dataExport {destination}` and `daemon.dataExportSubscribe`, the destination from the platform's save dialog; `Erase all data` over `daemon.dataErase {}`; and the port, `Ask me after one start leads to`, `Stop a run after`, `Max steps per turn`, `Spend limit`, `Tokens per run`, the tool-process memory cap, the package cache limit, `Record traces` and `Record raw provider messages` over `daemon.configRead` and `daemon.configUpdate`, the port's state from `workflow.webhookListenerRead`. Each act that stops work or deletes data fires once, on its confirm's press. **Files:** the Runtime settings page and its tests (EXTEND) **Tests:** `Stop`, `Restart`, `Delete now` and `Erase everything` call nothing until their confirm is pressed and then call once; a field refusing a value (`That is not a size.`, `1 or more, or empty for unlimited`) writes nothing and keeps what it had. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Settings (Settings › Runtime) **Verifies invariant:** none

### Phase 8 — Preview And Detached Panes

**Goal:** Implementation Step 17 — Preview, the session's own browser pane on the desktop's Chromium with a headless browser in the daemon where no desktop runs, and a side pane in a window of its own. [ADR-034](../decisions/034-embedded-browser-for-preview.md) records the choice of a native page view driven by Playwright's tool server in the daemon.

**Preconditions.** Phase 7 must have merged: the E2E suite and the two-client harness are this phase's test bed. The page host and a pane's own window both build on main's registry of windows and its window-open handler (T-020p-1B-5) and on the renderer's view registry (T-020r-5-3). [Plan-005](./005-local-ipc-and-daemon-control.md) CP-005-10 registers the daemon's `preview` and `browser` roots and nothing more; the daemon's Preview side is one build unit, and this plan holds its tasks: T-020r-8-2 builds everything that answers on those roots. T-020r-8-4's `Self-hosted git hosts` section also waits on [Plan-008](./008-gitflow-pr-and-diff-attribution.md) T8.7, which serves the `gitflow.host*` verbs.

#### Tasks

- **T-020r-8-1 — The Preview page host in the main process.** A `WebContentsView` per open page in `session.fromPartition('persist:preview')`, added with `contentView.addChildView`, positioned from the rectangle the renderer publishes (`browser.publishPaneRect(pane, rect)`) and clipped to the part of it the renderer reports still visible; when a console window reloads or its renderer crashes, main hides every page view in that window until a fresh rectangle arrives, so a dead window never leaves a page painted; with `paintWhenInitiallyHidden: true` and `backgroundThrottling` left at its default. Every window is a `BaseWindow` (Phase 1B and T-020p-1B-5), because a `BrowserWindow`'s own never-navigated page target hangs any Playwright attach that reaches it. **The debugger link, with no debug port:** `webContents.debugger` is attached to each Preview view and to no other `webContents`; main reports each view as `preview.pageTargetReport {pageId, targetId}`, takes the service's commands as `preview.pageDebuggerSend {pageId, message}` and returns replies and events as `preview.pageDebuggerReport {pageId, message}`, all on the connection main dials to the service, and bridges page open, close and lifecycle to the service's relay. There is no `--remote-debugging-port` and no `--remote-debugging-pipe`. **The agent's size, fitted:** main answers `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` for a page itself — it keeps the asked width and height with the session that asked, sends the override back on that session with `dontSetVisibleSize: true`, the asked `deviceScaleFactor` and a `scale` that fits the pane's rectangle, sends it again on every rectangle change, and forgets it when that session detaches or when the person returns the page to filling the pane; while it holds a size it multiplies by the scale the points of every `Input.dispatchMouseEvent`, the `x`, `y`, `radiusX` and `radiusY` of every touch point of `Input.dispatchTouchEvent` (an absent radius counting as 1), and `Input.synthesizeTapGesture` and `Input.emulateTouchFromMouseEvent` (the last rounded to whole numbers), and leaves wheel deltas and `Input.dispatchDragEvent` as sent. It never calls `webContents.enableDeviceEmulation`. **The rest of the host:** `webContents.setZoomFactor` per page from `preview.zoom`, reset to 1 when a page opens; freeze for marking with `capturePage()` first and `view.setVisible(false)` second, the scale read from the returned image and never from `getScaleFactors()`; a popup the page opens becomes a page in the list through the preview session's own `setWindowOpenHandler` and `preview.pageOpen`, never a window; a download a page starts opens the system save dialog while the page is on this machine's own desktop window — the Preview feature asks for it through the `browser` namespace, and main owns the dialog and the file write, so the window gains no file access — and, where no person at this machine can choose, as when the page is reached from another device, `will-download` is answered with `event.preventDefault()` and the refusal reported to the service, which sends the line back to the pane; `preview.pageCookiesRead`, `preview.pageCookiesWrite`, `preview.pageCookiesClear` (`ses.clearData({dataTypes: ['cookies'], …})` for one registrable domain) and `preview.pageSiteDataClear {origins?}` (`ses.clearData({origins, dataTypes})` for one site, `ses.clearData()` for all); the page's right-click menu, a native menu main builds from the view's `context-menu` event with the console's rows (Back, Forward, Reload through `preview.navigate`; Cut, Copy, Paste, Select all; Copy link; Open in the default browser) and never the page engine's; and the process inventory from `app.getAppMetrics()`, reading only `workingSetSize` and `peakWorkingSetSize` on macOS. **The page budget:** when `process.availableMemory()` falls below the memory gate's headroom ([Spec-015 §Execution semantics](../specs/015-workflow-authoring-and-execution.md#execution-semantics)), main releases the oldest page nobody is looking at, one every 1.5 s until the reading is back above it and never the page on screen, and releases a page unseen for ten minutes; a released page's view is destroyed, because `webContents` has no suspend, and `preview.pageList` marks it `released` with its address kept, so it reloads when shown. No page count and no per-page memory cap stands beside this rule. **A page moves with its pane:** a page whose pane moves into a window of its own moves whole — `removeChildView` from one window and `addChildView` into the other, with no reload and the same page, socket and debugger link — and main refits the held size to the new pane; on the return of that window main puts the page back in its session's view, and on a quit it closes it. **The `browser` bridge namespace:** `browser.publishPaneRect(pane, rect)`, `browser.act(pane, act)`, `browser.capturePage(pane)`, `browser.subscribe(pane, handler)` (the page host's events, `downloadOffered` and `downloadRefused` among them), `browser.saveDownload(pane)` (the system save dialog for the download the pane's page offered, on this machine's own desktop window), `browser.publishPageChords(chords)` and `browser.subscribePageChords(handler)`, so the chords the console keeps still work while focus is in the page. **Budget:** four open pages, one of them streaming a screencast, against no page open, for resident memory, processor time while idle and while streaming, and the process count — measured before the task and after. **Files:** the Preview page host in the main process and its tests (CREATE), the bridge's `browser` handler and its test (CREATE), the desktop's preload API type, `PreloadApi`, in `shared/preload-api.ts` and the `PlatformBridge` interface in the renderer's `services/platform/` (EXTEND — the `browser` namespace) **Tests:** the debugger is attached to the Preview views and to no console window; a popup becomes a page and opens no window; a download on this machine's own window writes only where the save dialog chose, and a refused one writes nothing to disk and leaves the page where it was; per-site `Clear cookies` removes a cookie set on the parent domain and leaves every other site's data; with a fitted size held, the agent's click lands on the point it aimed at; below the memory threshold the oldest unseen page is released first and the page on screen never is; a page moved into a pane's own window keeps its document without a reload; a console window's reload hides its page views until the next rectangle arrives. **Spec coverage:** [Spec-021 §Detached panes](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#detached-panes); Preload Bridge Contract (the `browser` namespace) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-1 (the `browser` namespace carries a rectangle, acts, chords and a captured picture, never a cookie or a credential)
- **T-020r-8-2 — The daemon's Preview.** Built in `packages/runtime-daemon`. **One path, no debug port:** one function returns a session's Playwright browser context — on the desktop the context Playwright reaches with `connectOverCDP(transport)` over the relay on main's debugger link (T-020r-8-1), otherwise the context of the headless browser — so nothing downstream, a workflow's browser steps included, branches on which host is live. **The relay** joins main's page targets into one browser whose target list is exactly the Preview pages. Its page-session filter is a hard requirement: on a page's session only `Target.setAutoAttach`, detaching that session's own children and `Target.getTargetInfo` for its own target pass, and every other `Target.*` and `Browser.*` command is refused, so a page cannot list, attach to or read the console's own windows. It answers browser-level commands itself, turns a tab the agent opens into a Preview page and a closed one into a strip change, and sends `targetCreated`, `targetInfoChanged` and `targetDestroyed` to every session that asked for discovery; it carries separate browser contexts (create, dispose, list, and `createTarget` with a `browserContextId` carried on every target's info), frames and workers as targets of their own, `Browser.getWindowForTarget`, `Browser.setWindowBounds` and `Browser.resetPermissions` on page sessions, an attach to the browser target only from the root session, announced with `attachedToTarget`, a detach of what auto-attach attached when it is turned off and of every page session when a browser session ends, a failed close answered `{success: false}`, and `Target.*` commands on a page session answered from its own target list; it answers `Runtime.runIfWaitingForDebugger`, hands the transport one message per task, hands `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` and — while main holds a size — the input commands main rescales to main, and refuses `Emulation.setVisibleSize` on every page session and `Input.synthesizeScrollGesture` and `Input.synthesizePinchGesture` while a size is held. **The headless host:** `chromium.launchPersistentContext` over Playwright's own launch pipe with no port — an installed Chrome or Edge through `channel` when one is present and not blocked by policy, otherwise Playwright's Chromium, fetched once on first need as one flow row (`Fetching Chromium 152…`) — one browser with a context per session, each with `acceptDownloads: false` and the refusal line raised from Playwright's `download` event, `userDataDir` always a profile of ours, closed after ten idle minutes; the headless host releases the oldest page nobody is looking at once memory falls below the gate's headroom, one every 1.5 s, never the page on screen, as main does; where the Linux system libraries it needs are missing, `browser.chromiumRead` answers `cannotStart {reason: missingSystemLibraries, installStep}` with Playwright's own install-deps command for the version the service carries. A page's title, address, history and load state are read from the debug protocol's `Target.*` and `Page.*` events on whichever host is live, and change only when they really changed. **Focus emulation:** right after it attaches, the service sends `Emulation.setFocusEmulationEnabled {enabled: true}` on every page it serves, on both hosts, while an agent's session is attached, so a page out of sight — never shown, minimized or covered by another window — still gives frames and takes the agent's clicks. **The live picture for another device:** the service opens its own debugger session on the page (`newCDPSession(page)` on a `noDefaults: true` connection), streams `Page.startScreencast` JPEG frames at quality 60–80 sized to the pane and acknowledges each on `Page.screencastFrameAck`, forwards wheel, touch and keys as `Input.dispatchMouseEvent`, `Input.dispatchTouchEvent` and `Input.dispatchKeyEvent`, with touch emulation off and the page's visual zoom kept at (1, 1), and maps frame pixels to page points from each frame's metadata and the size main holds; it runs only while a remote pane is open. **Who sets what on a page:** every service connection passes `noDefaults: true`, and after any Playwright client detaches the service re-asserts focus emulation and the screencast's state; interception and network conditions are the agent's alone, through its session's tool connection, and the service's sessions, the daemon's reads and a workflow's browser steps never send `Fetch.enable` or a network condition. **The agent's tools:** `playwright-core`'s `tools.createConnection`, one connection per session, handed that session's browser context, behind the shared `sidekicks` server ([Plan-003](./003-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md) T3.15 leg 3), which lists the browser tools in the session's entry for it and hands each call to that session's connection; the connection has no route of its own, and is never a stdio child and never its own browser. The entry's admission and its registration with Claude Code and Codex at session start are the shared server's. `browser_run_code_unsafe` is never in the entry's tool list, and `Browser tools for sidekicks` off leaves the browser tools out while the entry stays for the daemon's other tools. Every browser call takes the same approval path as any other tool under the session's permission level, and the transcript gets one browser-call row from Claude Code's `mcp__<server>__<tool>` shape and Codex's `mcpToolCall` shape alike. **Marks:** at mark-commit time the service takes `ariaSnapshot({ mode: 'ai', boxes: true })`, hit-tests each mark against the boxes, carries per mark its number, its note, `{ref, box}` (a stroke: its points and color), the page's address and its width, and treats a ref as valid only for the snapshot that minted it; `preview.marksSend` hands the picture to the driver's staged-file conversion ([Plan-003](./003-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md) T3.42), which sends it as an image block to Claude Code and as a `localImage` item to Codex. **Site data:** one site-data set per machine; `browser.siteDataList` rows carry `hasCookies`; `browser.siteDataForget`, `browser.siteDataClear` and `browser.siteCookiesClear {origin}` clear per registrable domain on both hosts (through main's page-host methods on the desktop, `context.clearCookies({domain})` and the site's storage on the headless host); `browser.siteSignIn {origin}` opens that site in a page of its own that belongs to no session; `browser.chromiumRead` and `browser.chromiumFetch` name the Chromium in use and fetch it again. When one host takes over from the other, never continuously, cookies carry across (Playwright's `expires` mapped to Electron's `expirationDate`), local storage and IndexedDB are copied by script, passkeys do not cross, and the pane says so. **Pages and servers:** the per-session page store (address, title, icon, order, which is active, zoom) behind `preview.pageList` — a subscription whose record carries `released`, the favicon, load state and zoom — `preview.pageOpen`, `preview.pageClose`, `preview.navigate`, `preview.pageActivate`, `preview.pageReorder` and `preview.zoom`, so after a service restart main's views reconnect without reloading and after an app restart every page comes back `released`; `preview.pageOpen` and `preview.navigate` answer the address actually loaded, with `movedFrom: port` when a port carried from WSL took the next free one; and `preview.devServerList`, a subscription listing the dev servers in the session's project, those this session did not start included, over one probe loop per daemon across the machine's listening sockets (a branch per platform, with a short-timeout HTTP probe that checks only that a server answers): it names the framework from the command line of the process that owns the port, matched against its short list, and the port alone when nothing matches, probing nothing in the page for a name; it marks `startedHere` on a server this session started, by its agent or in its own shell, read from the process tree the probe already walks, and clears it when that process ends; and it ticks only while a project session is live. **Dependencies:** `playwright-core` 1.63.0, whose `lib/coreBundle` export carries the tool server, with the shared server's transport on `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.32.0, already a direct daemon dependency; `@playwright/mcp` is not added, because its tool server is the one `playwright-core` already ships and its releases pull in a pre-release Playwright ahead of the pin. **Files:** the daemon's Preview module in `packages/runtime-daemon` and its tests (CREATE), the `preview` and `browser` contracts in `packages/contracts` (EXTEND — `packages/contracts/src/preview/` and `packages/contracts/src/browser.ts`), `packages/runtime-daemon/package.json` (EXTEND — the dependencies) **Tests:** a page session's `Target.getTargets` lists only Preview pages, and its attach to any other target is refused; a target waiting for the debugger resumes once `Runtime.runIfWaitingForDebugger` is answered; the agent's click lands on a page covered by another window; after a Playwright client detaches, frames keep flowing and focus emulation stays on; a dev server another session started is listed with no `startedHere`, and this session's mark clears when its server process ends; with `Browser tools for sidekicks` off the session's entry carries no browser tool and still carries the daemon's other tools; the screencast keeps flowing past three frames; a mark resolves to the ref and box of the snapshot taken at commit; a browser call raises the same approval a shell call does. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Detached panes; Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian) (the Preview pane) **Verifies invariant:** none — the page-session filter is proved by this task's tests
- **T-020r-8-3 — The Preview pane.** The `browser` pane kind keeps its registration and kind string, and its body becomes Preview: registered through the pane registry, with no pane-chrome tab strip and no devtools act. The header's `Preview` chip between Review and Terminal, read by assistive technology as the control for the pane, always drawn and grayed with its reason in its own hover title when the session has neither a page nor a server, and never opening the pane while grayed; `⌘⇧B`, inert while the chip is grayed. The pane frame: a pane in the view's pane row (T-020r-5-12), opening at the row's end and able to take the full width, with its own drag edge that reads out its width, its floor and its ceiling, a double-click and `Home` reset and the width kept on pointer-up; the sessions flyout collapses on open, and nothing restores it. The address line in the design's order, the address field as the control, spoken as `Address` and with spell-checking off — Enter navigates, typed-but-unsent text survives a page self-update, its own Escape discards; back and forward always drawn and grayed until the history has somewhere to go; the width readout only while the edge is under the pointer; the size the agent holds as a chip (`390 × 844`) with `Fill pane` beside it, whose press returns the page to the pane's size and drops the chip, `Offline`, or both; where the port moved, the address with its faint note (`127.0.0.1:5174 (5173 was in use)`); and, while a page is open, the hand-off arrow at the line's end, which opens the same address in the system browser through `native.openExternal`. The address parser accepts an http or https address, a bare host, `localhost:<port>`, a bare port (`3000` opens `localhost:3000`), an IP and a path on the current page, and refuses everything else onto the pane's own refusal line with the exact cause in words — a scheme the pane cannot open, or text that is no address — leaving the page where it was, never a search. The page tab strip at two or more pages, one tab per page with the favicon, replaced by a turning mark while the page loads, the title falling back to the host, a loopback page's port after its name (`:<port>`), and a per-tab `×`, a middle-click on a tab closing it exactly as its `×` does with nothing drawn for the gesture, drawn and activated like the terminal's tab strip, a typed address navigating in place; with one page the reload control turns while it loads. Tabs drag to reorder within the strip (`preview.pageReorder`) through the console's own pointer drag (T-020r-5-11), the tab following the pointer and its neighbors gliding apart to open the landing slot, the end slot included, and a canceled drag keeping the order; the tab's own menu, opened by a right-click, the menu key or Shift+F10, moves it the same way with `Move left`, `Move right`, `Move to start` and `Move to end`, a row that cannot move disabled, and the strip takes no chord of its own; after a move, from the menu or a drag, the live region says `Moved <tab label> to position N of M.`; a canceled drag says nothing, and a refused move says the refusal's own sentence. The body's five states — the host rectangle, the live picture, the server rows — a single server opening straight onto its page rather than listed, and a page whose dev server is gone showing the rows rather than a dead page — or the one `No dev server is running in <project>` line with the address still live, the load error with `Try again`, and, where the headless browser cannot start for missing Linux system libraries, the line `Preview's browser cannot start on <machine>: it needs system libraries that are not installed. Install them with: sudo npx playwright@<version> install-deps chromium`, as text — plus the frozen picture under a pressed tool. The geometry publisher, publishing the pane's rectangle to the page host and the part of it still visible against everything that clips it on every frame the pane moves or changes size — a pane-edge drag, the stack divider, the sessions list opening, a scroll that shifts it without resizing it — derived from the pane's own size at every width; an overlay covering the visible part hides the page, the page lets pointer events through while a pane edge is under the pointer, and a pressed tool reports the page not visible for the whole frozen state. The three tools — comment, dashed box and pen — as icon-only toggles, one pressed at a time, the pressed one in the accent on a 12 % wash, each with a tooltip and an accessible name; the hue slider only under Pen, resting at the accent with its thumb wearing the next stroke's color, a stroke keeping the color it was drawn in for life; the marks layer with comment pins, boxes and `perfect-freehand` 1.2.3 strokes, notes under comments and boxes, a stroke's own `×`, renumbering that skips strokes, and `Clear marks` / `Send marks` present only while a mark is; Escape or a second press returning the live page with the marks kept; the marks chip in the composer's one attachment strip, reading `<N> marks on <path>` (`1 mark on <path>`) with its count following the marks that exist now, carried by the ordinary Send, with `Send marks` focusing the draft. Escape priority: a pressed tool ahead of full width. Browser-act rows in the transcript in words, with the screenshot inline in the row's result slot, no cost, and the machine named when the call came from another device. A loopback address in a tool row's output or a shell opens Preview at that address on a plain press; in a reply a plain press opens it in the machine's own browser, and a modifier press opens it in Preview while the session runs on this machine. Nothing but a person's act opens Preview: a page or a server appearing, or an agent's browser call, never opens it. Zoom by `⌘=` and `⌘-` through 50 · 67 · 75 · 80 · 90 · 100 · 110 · 125 · 150 · 175 · 200 %, and `⌘0` back to 100 %, while focus is inside Preview, per page and 100 % for a new page, with no button, figure or menu. From another device the pane shows the live picture with `Runs on <machine>` on its address line. **Files:** the Preview pane body and its parts in the preview feature (`features/preview/`), registered under the `browser` pane kind (CREATE), the desktop app's `package.json` (EXTEND — `perfect-freehand` 1.2.3), and their tests **Tests:** the chord does nothing where the chip is grayed; a search phrase lands on the refusal line and navigates nowhere, and `3000` navigates to `localhost:3000`; the tab strip appears only at two pages; pressing a second tool releases the first; Escape with a tool pressed releases the tool and leaves full width alone; a plain press on a loopback address in a tool row opens Preview, in a reply opens the system browser, and a modifier press in a reply opens Preview, none of them navigating the window; an agent's browser call with Preview closed leaves it closed; the hand-off arrow is absent while no page is open and opens the page's address through `native.openExternal`; the marks chip's count drops when a mark is cleared after `Send marks`; the pane and every part of it has no horizontal overflow at 1440 × 900 and no dialog, overlay or scrim; a slider moved between two strokes leaves the first stroke its color; one discovered server opens its page with no list; an overlay opened over the pane hides the page and closing it shows the page again. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian) (the Preview pane) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-8-4 — Settings › Browser.** One site-data set per machine, with no per-session partition and no node-wide switch: `Remember site data`, on by default, and `Browser tools for sidekicks`, both written through the machine settings members (T-020r-2-8); the sites with saved data from `browser.siteDataList`, each with `Forget`; `Clear all site data`; beside a site's `Forget`, `Clear cookies` on a site that reads `Saved cookies` (it holds an unexpired cookie) and `Sign in` on one that reads `Saved site data` (other saved data and no cookie), with no row saying the person is signed in or out, because a saved cookie proves neither; and the Chromium in use — the desktop's own, an installed Chrome or Edge, or Playwright's — with its version, when it was fetched and `Fetch it again`, or, where Linux system libraries are missing, the line with the command that installs them. `Self-hosted git hosts`, under `A self-hosted forge registered here lights the pull-request half of Review.`, lists one validated host per row with `Remove` (`gitflow.hostList`, `gitflow.hostRemove`) and a field, `Add a host, such as gitlab.internal.lan`, with `Add host` (`gitflow.hostAdd {host}`), refusing in place with `That is not a host name.` or `<host> didn't answer as GitHub or GitLab. Sign in to it with gh or glab, then add it again.` and saving nothing. **Files:** the Browser settings page (EXTEND — the machine-level model replaces any per-session one) and its tests **Tests:** no control on the page is scoped to a session; `Forget` on one site leaves every other site's data; a refused host adds no row. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian) (Settings › Browser) **Verifies invariant:** none
- **T-020r-8-5 — A side pane in a window of its own.** `⌘⇧A` on the focused side pane, or its header's `Open in new window`, opens the pane in its own window through the renderer's `window.open`; main's window-open handler (T-020p-1B-5) builds the window, with no kept place at the width the renderer last handed for that pane kind through `window.setDefaultSizes` and the display's full work-area height, and adopts the child document, the renderer copies its stylesheets into the child and renders the pane there sharing its stores and daemon subscriptions, and nothing crosses the bridge for it. The window's `Return to session` (busy `Returning to session…`), `⌘⇧A` pressed in it, and its close each return the pane to its session's view, or to the window used last when that view is gone. A pane's own window belongs to its session and outlives the window it came from; its place and size are kept per pane kind, and it comes back across quits from the kept layout. The chords scoped to a pane follow it into its window. With no side pane open, the palette's `Open in new window` stays listed and refuses in place with `Open a side pane first.` A crash of the renderer takes every window with it; main closes them, and the reloaded renderer reopens every window, its views and every pane's own window, unless the crashes repeat ([Desktop App Implementation Notes §Repeated renderer crashes](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#repeated-renderer-crashes)). Preview's page moves with its pane (T-020r-8-1). **Files:** the pane-layout snapshot (EXTEND — a pane's own window), the renderer's window client (EXTEND), the pane header (EXTEND — `Open in new window`), the keyboard rows (EXTEND), and their tests **Tests:** closing a pane's own window returns the pane to its session's view; closing the window a pane came from leaves the pane's window open; after a restart every pane's own window is back at its place. **Spec coverage:** [Spec-021 §Detached panes](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#detached-panes) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-7 (the pane's window is built by the locked constructor around the renderer's own child, with no preload of its own), I-020-10 (the renderer places the pane; main builds the window and reads nothing of the layout)
- **T-020r-8-6 — Preview's still picture while its pane moves.** Preview's page is a native view main places from the rectangles the pane publishes, so it trails a pane that moves: about 10 to 15 points behind a row scrolling sideways at 600 px/s ([electron#32751](https://github.com/electron/electron/issues/32751), [electron#39993](https://github.com/electron/electron/issues/39993)). When Preview's pane starts to move — the pane row scrolling sideways, a pane or a divider dragged, the window resized — the pane asks for its still picture: main captures the page (`capturePage()`) and hides the live view (`setVisible(false)`), as the freeze for marking does (T-020r-8-1), and the pane lays the picture in its slot, where it moves with the row. When the motion stops — `scrollend`, the pointer's release, the resize's end — the pane publishes its rectangle and main shows the live view there. A video playing in Preview pauses while the row moves. **Files:** the Preview pane and its geometry publisher (EXTEND), the Preview page host in the main process (EXTEND), and their tests **Tests:** in the Electron tier, during a sideways scroll of the row the picture stays in the pane's slot with no gap between it and the pane's edge in any frame; after `scrollend` the live view is shown at the pane's final rectangle; a drag of the pane's header and a window resize each swap the picture in and the live page back. **Budget:** the time from the motion's start to the picture in the slot, measured. **Spec coverage:** Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian) (the Preview pane) **Verifies invariant:** I-020-1 (the `browser` namespace carries a rectangle and a captured picture, never a cookie or a credential)

## Test And Verification Plan

- **Unit tests** (Vitest `main-unit` project — `apps/desktop/src/main/**/*.test.ts` and `apps/desktop/build/**/*.test.ts`, co-located beside the module under test; the project itself is registered by T-020p-1B-3, without which none of these files is matched by any glob — see §Testing posture):
  - `updater.test.ts` — mock `electron-updater`; assert a release whose `version` is not above the last one seen is refused and nothing from it is downloaded; assert a download that fails `electron-updater`'s SHA-512 check is discarded before staging.
  - `bridge/*.test.ts` — per-namespace unit tests; assert auth material is stripped from responses.
  - `build/*.test.ts` — the packaging-side units in the same project: `release-artifact-fuses.test.ts`, which reads the packaged binary's fuse wire, and `code-sign.test.ts`, which asserts the notarization poll-and-resume without packaging a real release; the installer's size ceiling is T-020r-6-3's `size-limit` check.
- **E2E tests** (Playwright `_electron`, `apps/desktop/tests/e2e/*.test.ts`):
  - Per §Target Areas Test section. End-to-end tests covering launch, fuses, bridge surface, deep-link.
  - The `sidekicks://` link end-to-end test carries both address forms: a valid `sidekicks://session/<id>` reaches the rendered session and a valid `sidekicks://workflow-run/<runId>` the run's page, and a malformed URL is refused at the parser boundary with nothing routed. It is one of the end-to-end tests §Target Areas lists.
  - Bridge-surface negative test is the primary enforcement of [Spec-021 §Acceptance Criteria](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#acceptance-criteria) "No auth material on `window.desktopBridge`": asserts no property name matches `/token|dpop|secret/i` and no property type contains those keywords.
  - **Renderer test harness** — the fixture bridge (`services/platform/bridge.fixture.ts`, with the suite helpers in `tests/helpers/fixture/bridge.ts`), exercisable from `test:renderer` without spawning Electron, plus a remainder IPC dispatcher capable of driving two Electron clients in one E2E run. The dispatcher is owned by T-020r-7-2 in the remainder's E2E phase, and the two-client checks Phase 8's Preview runs wait on it. The fixture bridge implements the `PlatformBridge` type rather than a hand-written copy of it, so a member added to the real surface breaks the fixture at compile time.
- **Contract tests** (TypeScript conditional-type tests in `apps/desktop/src/shared/preload-api.test-d.ts`, beside the desktop's preload API type, `PreloadApi`):
  - The preload API type does not accept a shape with a `token: string` property; the type-test fails to compile if the interface is edited to leak auth material.
- **Lint**:
  - The two ESLint selectors in `apps/desktop/eslint.config.mjs` — a window or web view built outside the window factory, and a hardened `webPreferences` key written as anything but its hardened literal.
- **Release-gate tests**:
  - Signed artifacts pass platform-native signature checks: the macOS code signature (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`) on every build, and Gatekeeper (`spctl --assess --type execute`) once the Developer ID certificate exists ([BL-108](../backlog.md#bl-108-windows--macos-signing-procurement-evidence)), the Authenticode signature (Windows, `Get-AuthenticodeSignature`), `dpkg-sig --verify` (Linux `.deb`), `rpm --checksig` (Linux `.rpm`).
  - Auto-update version check → press-to-download → SHA-512 and code-signature check → stage → apply → daemon-handshake exercises end-to-end in a CI matrix job per platform.
- **Bundle-size verification**:
  - The `size-limit` check of T-020r-6-3 — its `@size-limit/file` plugin measures each packaged installer with `brotli: false`, so the number is the file's own size, without the Linux runtime archives a Windows installer carries, against the ADR-015 150 MB ceiling, beside the renderer bundle budget in the same config. Fails the CI build on regression.

### Adversarial-Tampering Boundary

The remainder is almost entirely a tampering surface — an untrusted renderer, an untrusted inbound URL, an untrusted update feed, a credential store, a crash-report channel, and a page the agent drives — so the `000-plan-template.md` threat classes are enumerated here with the task that owns each arm. Every arm lands inside a phase; none is a floating aspiration.

| Threat class | Arm | Owning task |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Canonicalization round-trip | A `sidekicks://` URL survives parse → normalize → re-serialize with no action or argument reinterpretation. Deep-link parsing is the plan's only untrusted-input parser. | T-020r-4-4 |
| Intake validation parity | Each IPC handler's own validation is at least as strict as the contract schema — a payload the schema rejects must not pass the handler. [Spec-021 §Trust Stance](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#trust-stance) makes every renderer-originated payload untrusted, so handler-level leniency re-admits what the contract forbids. | T-020r-2-6 |
| Mutation-isolation symmetry | Structured-clone discipline in both directions: a renderer-held reference cannot mutate main-process state, and a renderer-supplied object is cloned on the way in. | T-020r-2-6 |
| Crash-report exfiltration | A crash dump planted with a PASETO token, a DPoP proof, a session id, an absolute file path, and a content payload yields a kept report with every one absent, and nothing leaves the machine: no crash is uploaded, and the raw dump never leaves it. | T-020r-3-2 |
| Update-feed tamper | A release whose `version` is not above the last one seen is refused with nothing from it downloaded; a downloaded artifact whose SHA-512 does not match the release's update file, or on Windows whose publisher does not match, is discarded before staging. Both refusals are fail-closed with no state mutation. | T-020r-4-2 |
| Preview page escape | A Preview page's debugger session reaches only its own target: every other `Target.*` and `Browser.*` command is refused, so a page cannot list, attach to or read the console's own windows, and no debug port is opened on either host. The agent's tool route behind the shared `sidekicks` server refuses a request without the session's secret in its path, from a peer that is not loopback, carrying an `Origin` header or with a body not declared JSON, before any method runs. | T-020r-8-1, T-020r-8-2, [Plan-003](./003-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md) T3.15 leg 3 |

### Testing posture

The `@ai-sidekicks/desktop` package's tests are Vitest projects declared in `apps/desktop/vitest.config.ts`, with the renderer's tiers declared in `apps/desktop/vitest/tier-projects.ts` and spread into it. Each project has its own `test:<project>` script and its own task in `apps/desktop/turbo.json`, and every project's `include` globs are disjoint from every other's, so nothing is discovered twice:

- **`smoke`** (`test:smoke`) — node environment, includes `tests/*.test.ts`: exactly the files directly under `tests/`, because `tests/**/*.test.ts` would also take every tier under `tests/<tier>/**`. Spawns the BUILT `electron-vite --mode=smoke` bundle and asserts the [Spec-021 §Security Hardening Baseline](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#security-hardening-baseline) runtime invariants (`window.desktopBridge` defined; `window.require`, `process`, `global` all `undefined`). `dependsOn: ["build:smoke"]` same-package. Its files run one at a time, because each spawns a full Electron process tree.
- **`main-unit`** (`test:main-unit`) — node environment, the in-process units under `src/main/**`, `src/preload/**`, `src/shared/**`, `build/**`, `scripts/**` and `tests/helpers/**`, each beside the module it tests. `dependsOn: ["^build"]` and not `build:smoke`: plain TypeScript units need no `electron-vite` bundle.
- **`renderer`** (`test:renderer`) — happy-dom environment, the renderer's unit tests beside their source, `src/renderer/src/**/*.test.{ts,tsx}`, plus the scenario contract check under `tests/helpers/scenario/contract-check/`, which the node projects exclude. `dependsOn: ["^build"]`.
- **The console's tiers** of [Desktop App Implementation Notes §Console Test Tiers](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#console-test-tiers) — `browser` and `accessibility` in browser mode, and `bundle`, `e2e` and `endurance` in a node environment, each over its own folder under `tests/`. `bundle` depends on `build`, and `e2e` and `endurance` on `build:fixtures`.

The three build flavors, `build`, `build:smoke` and `build:fixtures`, are `cache: false`, and so is every test task. The flavors all write `out/**` and differ in what their `define` compiles in, and a Turborepo cache hit restores the files it saved without emptying the directory first, so a cached restore over a sibling flavor's output would leave that flavor's chunks beside the restored ones; running the build makes the switch between flavors total. A test task asserts against a tree, and a cached pass would report on a tree that is no longer there.

The package-level `test` script (the entry point `pnpm --filter @ai-sidekicks/desktop test` resolves to) is a chain of `turbo run` invocations — `test:main-unit` with `test:renderer`, then `test:browser`, then `test:bundle`, then `test:smoke`, then `test:e2e` — because one invocation would schedule the three build flavors at once and hand a tier the wrong bundle; `test:accessibility` and `test:endurance` run only by name until T-020p-1C-8 makes them gates. The outer `test` task in `apps/desktop/turbo.json` carries `cache: false` and no `dependsOn` of its own. It inherits the root `test` task's `inputs`, which declare `src/**`, `test/**`, `vitest.config.*`, `tsconfig*.json` and `package.json` and so exclude both `tests/**` and `build/**`; its `cache: false` is what keeps an edit under `apps/desktop/tests/**` from cache-hitting the orchestrator and skipping the suites inside it.

The two ESLint selectors and `factory.test.ts` hold the locked `webPreferences` block ([Spec-021 §Security Hardening Baseline](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#security-hardening-baseline)). The runtime smoke-test assertions in `launch.smoke.test.ts` run against the BUILT bundle; running smoke against unbuilt sources is not a supported configuration.

When the Playwright `_electron` E2E suite ships per Implementation Step 14 (T-020r-7-1), its tests land in the `e2e` project, `tests/e2e/**/*.test.ts`. A project added to this package takes a glob no other project reaches, and lands with the five registrations `apps/desktop/AGENTS.md` lists for a new Vitest project.

## Rollout Order

1. Scaffold `apps/desktop/` + bridge type + vite config + eslint config (steps 1, 2, 3, 13).
2. Land the window factory + preload + IPC handler skeletons (steps 4, 5, 6).
3. Land crash reporter (step 8) — must be wired early so it catches startup crashes during subsequent step landings.
4. Land auto-updater + deep-link (steps 7, 9).
5. Land the renderer's layout — router, layout chrome, composer (step 10). Gate: the bridge types from step 2 must be stable. Feature-view bodies are **not** landed here; each owning plan lands its own view into its own subtree and this step mounts it.
6. Land build pipeline (step 11) + the release manifest (step 12). First packaged release candidate emits from this step.
7. Land E2E suite (step 14) + CI gate (step 15) + release runbook (step 16).
8. Land Preview and a side pane's own window (step 17, §Phase 8).
9. Manual per-platform smoke on macOS arm64 + x64, Windows 11, Ubuntu 24.04 LTS, Fedora 41. A first-run pass on each: the app opens on the sessions list with nothing to answer, and Settings › Providers installs and signs in a provider.
10. Release V1 on the one release channel once its checks pass.

## Rollback Or Fallback

- **Renderer-level bug post-release**: auto-update ships a patched binary within 24 hours of detection; users receive it on next update-check cycle (max 4 hours) or explicit "check for updates" action.
- **A bad release**: the app does not roll itself back; the next release fixes it.
- **Daemon fails to start after 5 backoff attempts**: the app enters offline read-only mode; user can view the last-synced session state but cannot issue new runs or approvals. Manual retry button surfaced.
- **Auto-update check failure — an older release or a mismatched download**: a release whose version is not above the last one seen is refused with nothing downloaded; a downloaded artifact whose SHA-512 or Windows publisher does not match is discarded before staging. Either failure is logged; next update-check cycle re-attempts.
- **Notarization queue delay (macOS, once the Developer ID certificate exists — [BL-108](../backlog.md#bl-108-windows--macos-signing-procurement-evidence))**: the release pipeline's detached-submit + resumable-poll shape (§Notarization Submission And Poll) lets a delayed release candidate complete asynchronously — the submission id is persisted, Apple's Notary service keeps processing across CI-job boundaries, and no single job is held open. Only the 168-hour overall ceiling abandons a submission, and abandonment is a manual release-engineering decision, not an automatic resubmit. If notarization fails outright (rare), `notarytool log` is captured and the artifact is not distributed.

## Risks And Blockers
