- **`electron-vite` v6 stability.** The build runs on Vite 8, which only the v6 line declares (`vite: ^6.0.0 || ^7.0.0 || ^8.0.0`), and v6 is still a beta. **Decision: pin `6.0.0-beta.5` exactly**, the newest release, so a lockfile refresh cannot move the build tool outside a reviewed bump; the three builds and the smoke launch pass on it. Alternative considered: direct Vite + `electron-builder` wiring without a wrapper (gives maximum control; loses HMR convenience and source-code-protection baked-in). Alternative rejected because V1 desktop is a greenfield Electron app and `electron-vite` is the common production pick for one. Alternative also considered: `@electron-forge/plugin-vite` — still marked experimental in v7.11.1 (2026-01-12) per [Electron Forge Vite plugin docs](https://www.electronforge.io/config/plugins/vite). Rejected: an experimental plugin is not a V1-appropriate foundation.
- **Playwright `_electron` experimental API.** Marked experimental in the [Playwright class-electron docs](https://playwright.dev/docs/api/class-electron). Accepted: this is the only viable option (Spectron is archived). Mitigation: design test suites to tolerate minor API churn between Playwright releases; pin Playwright in `package.json` and review changelogs on every bump.
- **Crash dumps hold heap memory.** A Crashpad dump contains heap memory, which in main includes the daemon session token. **Mitigation:** nothing is uploaded — `crashReporter.start({ uploadToServer: false })`, with no crash SDK in any process — and dumps are kept on the machine only while `Keep crash reports` is on; the service builds each kept report stripped of tokens, session ids, paths and content, symbolicates it on the machine (`minidump-stackwalk` 0.27.0, measured before it is adopted), and deletes the dumps with the diagnostic logs.
- **SignPath Foundation eligibility.** Windows signing goes through SignPath Foundation's free program for open-source projects, which this plan applies for; its terms ask for an OSI license with no commercial dual-licensing, no proprietary components, an active and already-released project and a published code-signing policy, and the certificate is issued to SignPath Foundation, which is then the publisher Windows and the updater's publisher check see. The Windows release waits on that signing ([BL-108](../backlog.md#bl-108-windows--macos-signing-procurement-evidence)). If the project is not eligible, that goes back to the person, and the distribution plan does not change silently.
- **Smart App Control reputation cold-start on Windows.** A freshly-signed binary has low reputation in Windows Defender's Intelligent Security Graph. First-launch UX on Windows 11 will be rough until reputation accumulates. Accepted for V1. Mitigation: every release is signed by the same SignPath Foundation certificate, so reputation accrues to one publisher. The release notes and the README's install section carry the `Run anyway` guidance, because the prompt shows before the app can run.
- **Notarization queue delays on macOS.** Per [Desktop App Implementation Notes §Code Signing And Notarization](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#code-signing-and-notarization) — Apple's notarization queue can take 24–120+ hours, per [Apple Developer forum thread 813441](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/813441). The release pipeline's per-platform signing step therefore does **not** hold a job open on a synchronous wait: it submits detached, persists the submission id, and polls in bounded increments under a 168-hour overall abandonment ceiling set above the documented 120-hour upper bound (§Notarization Submission And Poll). Accepted as a release-latency risk; does not block V1 if release engineering is staffed for async release cutting. A timeout shorter than the documented delay range would not be a timeout at all — it would be a guaranteed resubmission cycle, and Apple's queue treats each resubmission as new work.
- **Electron 44 pin and the branch cadence.** Electron 41 is end-of-life (2026-08-25), so the 44.x target is not optional; the branch itself is supported until 2027-03-02 and V1 must expect at least one further major bump before then. The console's `browser` pane kind depends on the detached-`WebContentsView` bounds fix, which landed on 44 and reached 42 / 43 only as late backports, so V1 admits 44.x alone and carries no per-branch fix floor. **Mitigation:** the pin is exact in `apps/desktop/package.json`, a bump is its own change that re-reads [Desktop App Implementation Notes §Electron Version And Support Window](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#electron-version-and-support-window), and [ADR-015 §Decision](../decisions/015-electron-desktop-app.md#decision)'s supported-branch subset moves with the same PR that moves the pin. **Two legs ride the pin move.** (i) Electron 44 is Node ABI 149 and no `better-sqlite3` 12.x release ships a prebuild for it, so the daemon's binding would compile from source on every machine; [ADR-021 §Decision](../decisions/021-v1-toolchain-selection.md#decision) therefore moves `packages/runtime-daemon` to `better-sqlite3` 13.0.3 (Node-API, platform prebuilds bundled) **in or before** the PR that moves the Electron pin — the pin may not move first. (ii) [ADR-023](../decisions/023-electron-main-process-window-retention.md)'s window-retention mechanism holds on Electron 44.5.1, where the shipped lifecycle guard passes on macOS; each later pin move re-runs it, and a divergence is a failure of ADR-023's mechanism, never a test to loosen.
- **Console built ahead of Phase 5 (Phase 1C).** The console lands against the fixture bridge before the remainder phases that give several of its surfaces live wires, so the risk is a surface whose fixture shape drifts from the wire that eventually lands. **Mitigation:** each fixture is typed from the same interface as the real boundary it substitutes, the `PlatformBridge` among them, so a wire change breaks the fixture at compile time; a surface is wired live only against a wire its owning plan has registered; and a console PR that wires a surface live against an unregistered wire is a review rejection. Adoption at Phase 5 is a mount-and-move, never a rewrite (T-020r-5-1 EXTEND). The console's main-process floor is Phase 1B, not Phase 3: until T-020p-1B-1 / T-020p-1B-2 merge the renderer has no served origin, so the first console PR follows the Phase 1B PR rather than racing it.
- **Renderer clipboard deprecation pitfall.** Electron 40+ deprecated direct `navigator.clipboard.*` from the renderer per [Desktop App Implementation Notes](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md) §Renderer Bundle. The ESLint `no-restricted-imports` rule is the CI gate; a renderer file that imports `@capacitor/clipboard` or similar cross-platform clipboard library bypasses the rule. **Mitigation**: document in `apps/desktop/README.md` that clipboard access must route through the `PlatformBridge`'s `native.copyToClipboard`; add a runtime assertion (`expect(navigator.clipboard).toBeUndefined()` in renderer boot) that fails fast if a transitive dep re-introduces renderer-clipboard access.
- **Single-instance-lock race with deep-link.** [Spec-021 §Main Process Responsibilities](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#main-process-responsibilities) requires `app.requestSingleInstanceLock()`. Without the lock, a `sidekicks://session/<id>` URL arriving at a second instance would race with the first instance's daemon state. The lock + `second-instance` event handler is the correct pattern, but it introduces a subtle bug: if the first instance is mid-crash during URL arrival, the second instance gets the lock and may re-route the URL. Accepted: routing to a session is idempotent, so the race is a UX oddity not a security issue. Documented in `apps/desktop/RELEASE.md`, which T-020r-7-4 writes.

Each item names the part of the plan that delivers it: **(Partial)**, a partial supplement, or **(Remainder)**.

- **(Partial)** `apps/desktop/` scaffolded as a workspace package with the Electron runtime (pinned to 44.x by T-020p-1B-4), `electron-vite` `6.0.0-beta.5`, `react` v19 / `react-dom` v19, and the `@ai-sidekicks/contracts` + `@ai-sidekicks/client-sdk` workspace deps.
- **(Remainder)** The heavy dependency set deferred from the partial is added: `electron-builder` 26.17.x, `electron-updater` 6.8.10, `@electron/fuses` (for the packaged-artifact fuse test), `@playwright/test`.
- **(Partial)** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/services/platform/` exports the `PlatformBridge` interface from [Preload Bridge Contract](../architecture/contracts/preload-bridge-contract.md); conditional-type negative test passes.
- **(Partial)** Every window is a `BaseWindow` and every document it shows a `WebContentsView` created with `contextIsolation: true`, `sandbox: true`, `nodeIntegration: false`, `webSecurity: true`, a preload script, and the custom protocol load (not `file://`). The two ESLint selectors refuse a window built outside the window factory and a hardened key written as anything but its hardened literal, and `factory.test.ts` checks the factory builds through the locked block.
- **(Partial)** The `window.desktopBridge` surface has no `any`-typed escape hatch; the bridge type negative test catches leaks of `token` / `dpop` / `secret` names.
- **(Partial)** Renderer ESLint config rejects `electron` / `node:*` / `fs` / `child_process` / `net` / `os` / `path` / `process` / `./src/main/**` / `./src/preload/**` imports from `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/**`; CI gate fails on violation.
- **(Partial)** `app.requestSingleInstanceLock()` is held at startup.
- **(Remainder)** `second-instance` handler receives deep-link URLs on Windows / Linux; `open-url` handler receives them on macOS.
- **(Remainder)** Release builds ship with the Electron Fuses from [Spec-021 §Security Hardening Baseline](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#security-hardening-baseline) set as declared through `electron-builder`'s `electronFuses`; the packaged-artifact fuse test reads the packaged binary's fuse wire and asserts it matches.
- **(Remainder)** Release builds embed the ASAR Integrity Digest through `electron-builder`'s integrity embed, which `electron-builder` applies with the fuses **before** it signs.
- **(Remainder)** Auto-updater runs `electron-updater` with its stock GitHub provider, refuses a release whose `version` is not above the last one seen, and stages a download only after its SHA-512 check and, on macOS and Windows, the operating system's code signature pass; downloads start on the person's press and a `.deb` or `.rpm` install names its package manager's command instead of updating itself; the app does not roll itself back.
- **(Remainder)** The crash reporter is the first thing called in main's entry point and starts Crashpad with `uploadToServer: false`; no crash SDK is initialized in any process; dumps stay on the machine while `Keep crash reports` is on, and the kept report the service builds carries no tokens, session ids, file paths or content payloads, proven by a negative test against a planted dump.
- **(Remainder)** Playwright `_electron` E2E suite covers launch, fuses, bridge surface, deep-link. Tests tolerate Playwright API churn by pinning a specific Playwright version in `package.json`.
- **(Remainder)** The renderer test harness ships: the fixture bridge, typed as `PlatformBridge` and exercisable from `test:renderer`, plus a two-client IPC dispatcher for E2E.
- **(Remainder)** Code-signed release artifacts pass the macOS code-signature check on every build and Gatekeeper once the Developer ID certificate exists ([BL-108](../backlog.md#bl-108-windows--macos-signing-procurement-evidence)), the SignPath Foundation signature on Windows 10 and 11 ([BL-108](../backlog.md#bl-108-windows--macos-signing-procurement-evidence)), `rpm --checksig` (Linux `.rpm`) and apt's signed Release file (Linux `.deb`), with each RPM and the apt and dnf repository metadata signed by Cloudsmith's per-repository key, and every release publishes its files and a checksum list. Once that certificate exists, macOS signing uses the detached `xcrun notarytool submit` + resumable-poll + `stapler staple` design in §Notarization Submission And Poll, under a 168-hour overall abandonment ceiling — above the 24–120+ hour queue-delay range [Desktop App Implementation Notes §Code Signing And Notarization](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#code-signing-and-notarization) documents, so a normal queue never trips it.

No mounted view duplicates daemon-side logic — a property this plan **verifies** at the composition boundary rather than one it implements.

- **(Partial supplement — Phase 1C)** The console is in-tree in the renderer's folders with every pane kind [Spec-021 §Pane kinds](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#pane-kinds) names, the composer at its pinned home, the settings pages, and the fixture scenarios the fixture launch loads, with fixture code outside the product (I-020-8); every console PR passed the bundle-size budget, the structure gates and the tiers of [Desktop App Implementation Notes §Console Test Tiers](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#console-test-tiers) in place when it landed, and the accessibility and endurance tiers, landed after the macOS build is complete, gate every PR from then on (I-020-9).
- **(Remainder)** Phase 3's startup composition inherits the Phase 1B legs unchanged and its test proves the order survives; Phase 5 adopts the Phase 1C console by mount-and-move with no re-authored surface, and every surface whose wire has landed reads it live.
- **(Remainder)** Main's registry of windows builds every window through its window-open handler and brings back the window used last at start; several sessions show at once across windows, a window refusing only a second view of a session or a destination already on screen; closing every window stops no session (T-020p-1B-5, T-020r-5-3).
- **(Remainder)** The composer's draft, voice, running-commands list and cloud task row, the sessions list's groups with the inspector's `Related` and `Tags`, the keyboard and find keys, and Settings › General, Appearance and Runtime read their wires live (T-020r-5-2, T-020r-5-5 to T-020r-5-8, T-020r-7-5); the Windows installers carry the Linux runtime and the Windows half (T-020r-6-5).
- **(Remainder)** Every drag is the console's own pointer drag and works in every window — the terminal's and Preview's tabs, the waiting messages, the pane row and the session views — with `@atlaskit/pragmatic-drag-and-drop` and its code gone; a view holds as many main panes as are open in a row that scrolls sideways, the terminal above or below it; a view's title swaps it with another view, docks it into another window or tears it off live (T-020r-5-11, T-020r-5-12, T-020r-5-13); and Preview shows its still picture while its pane moves (T-020r-8-6).
- **(Remainder)** Preview runs on the desktop's page host and the daemon's headless browser through one relay with no debug port, its page-session filter refusing every command beyond a page's own target, and a side pane opens in a window of its own and returns to its session (§Phase 8 — Preview And Detached Panes).

## Delivery Split

Plan-020 splits into a partial delivery and a remainder under the substrate-vs-namespace carve-out; the units in [cross-plan-dependencies §Units](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#units) say where each of its unbuilt tasks lands:

- **Partial deliverable (Phase 1)** — workspace package substrate (`apps/desktop/src/{main,preload,renderer}/` directory tree + electron-vite toolchain + minimal entrypoints + the preload API type and the `PlatformBridge` interface). Standalone (no upstream plan dependencies); unblocks [Plan-001 Phase 5](./001-session-core.md#phase-5--client-sdk-and-daemon-wiring) and [Plan-021 Phase 3](./021-rust-pty-sidecar.md#phase-3--rustsidecarptyhost). See §Partial PR Sequence above.
- **Partial supplement — Phase 1B** — the renderer-load substrate: privileged scheme + bundle handler, the window's load, the locked window constructor, main's registry of windows with its window-open handler and window-place file, the application menu, and the `main-unit` Vitest project. Standalone (gated only on Phase 1 merged; no upstream plan dependency), so the Phase 1C console runs in the app without waiting on Plan-005 R3; T-020p-1B-4 moves the Electron and `better-sqlite3` pins. See §Partial PR Sequence.
- **Remainder** — Implementation Steps 6–12 (step 10 reduced to the router, `layout/` and `features/composer/` per §Renderer, since each Signature Feature view is created by its owning plan in that plan's own subtree) + 14–17: IPC handlers, crash reporter, auto-updater, deep-link, renderer layout + composer, build pipeline, release manifest, Playwright E2E, renderer test harness, CI gate, runbook, Preview and a side pane's own window. Decomposed into Phases 2–8 by [§Remainder PR Sequence](#remainder-pr-sequence). Strictly **downstream of Plan-005** (consumes the daemon IPC contract), **downstream of Plan-025** (consumes the control-plane tRPC + relay-WSS client), and **parallel to Plan-021** (both are desktop-surface plans but Plan-021 is owned by the daemon, not the main process).
- **Partial supplement — Phase 1C** — the console's renderer folders at §Target Areas Renderer, built by T-020p-1C-1..8 over the Phase-1B origin, inside the [Spec-021 §Console Design (Meridian)](../specs/021-desktop-app-and-renderer.md#console-design-meridian) envelope, against the fixture scenarios. Standalone (gated only on Phase 1B merged; no upstream plan dependency); **adopted** by the remainder's Phase 5 (T-020r-5-1 EXTEND). Its live wiring for any surface waits on the plan that registers that surface's wire, or on the remainder phase that registers it.
